# DIVERGENCE.md — zed-kask fork divergence from upstream Zed

> **Purpose:** the headline doc for upstream-sync conflict resolution. Every
> `git fetch upstream && git merge upstream/main` consults this file to know
> which crates/files are divergent and why. Referenced from
> `kask/docs/architecture/zed-host-architecture-plan.md` (§13.4).
>
> **Rule:** everything under `kask/` is ours (additive; upstream never touches
> here → near-zero merge conflict). Everything else tracks upstream; the only
> divergences are the D-seams listed below + the `[workspace.members]` /
> `[workspace.dependencies]` arrays in the root `Cargo.toml`.

## Status at a glance

- **Release 0.40.0** — the workspace version, inherited by the `zed` package (D7; pinned by `kask/scripts/check-version-sync.sh`). Current upstream base: upstream main `37faef81f7` (the 2026-10-27 re-link + merge; see Upstream-sync history).
- **Divergence surface: D1–D96** — 71 active seams in the table below (plus the three removal records D90–D92); 22 retired (see Retired seams — numbers are never reused; D66 retired onto upstream at the 2026-10-27 sync). These seams, the supporting files, and the root `Cargo.toml` workspace arrays are the only edits outside `kask/`.
- **`kask/` is additive** — `git merge upstream/main` never conflicts with it, and hKask crates never depend on zed-kask crates (see Governing invariant).
- **Reading order:** the seam table is the operative register; per-seam follow-up rulings sit directly under it; the Upstream-sync runbook is the merge procedure; dated sync/merge records are archived at the end under Upstream-sync history.

## The divergence surface (D1–D96)

Every hKask integration maps to a named, isolated change in zed-kask. These
are the _only_ edits to zed-kask's tree outside `kask/`. Any hKask behavior
that would require touching other Zed crates is a smell — push the logic into
an hKask crate behind one of these seams instead.

| D1  | Skill execution (`lisp_eval` + `render_template` tools)                             | `crates/agent/src/tools/skill_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/lisp_eval_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/render_template_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/record_skill_feedback_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` + `crates/agent_skills/agent_skills.rs` + `crates/zed/src/main.rs`                                                                                                                           | `SkillTool` follows **upstream Zed**: reads the `SKILL.md` body from disk via `agent_skills::read_skill_body` and injects it via `render_skill_envelope`. The model reads the body and follows the instructions. **2026-09-10 merge (`9b452f1961`):** `agent.rs` adopts upstream's `SkillTool::with_body_resolver` registration and promotes `skill_body_resolver_for_project` to production; slash activation uses the same resolver. Project-local bodies come through project buffers (remote workspaces and unsaved edits), global bodies through the filesystem. The Kask outcome/feedback hooks, dependency checks, core-skill overrides and authorization policy remain requirements. `skill_tool.rs` now dispatches through `with_body_resolver`, retaining all three `record_skill_outcome` sites and the dependency/authorization flow. The 81-test agent skill slice passed, including project-buffer resolution, authorization, dependency failure and outcome recording. Kask's disk-seeded core skills remain distinct from upstream's `BuiltIn` skill catalogue. Resolver pins: `test_project_skill_body_resolves_through_buffer` (agent) and `test_adding_remote_skill` (remote_server; wired to the same `with_body_resolver`/project-buffer path rather than the removed `SkillTool::new` constructor); hook pins below remain. **`lisp_eval` tool:** built-in (`crates/agent/src/tools/lisp_eval_tool.rs:374@LispEvalTool::run`), uses controlled `hkask_lisp::eval_sandboxed_with_cancellation`; delegated host decode/eval now uses public `evaluate_lisp_controlled` (`lisp_eval_tool.rs:487@evaluate_lisp_controlled`), while pure `evaluate_lisp` retains the uncontrolled budget API. Registered in `add_default_tools`. `hkask-lisp` workspace dependency in `crates/agent/Cargo.toml`. **`render_template` tool:** built-in (`crates/agent/src/tools/render_template_tool.rs`), renders prompt templates from `kask/registry/templates/` with `minijinja`. Before rendering, it parses the existing contract header and rejects missing top-level inputs unless they are explicitly nullable, defaulted, or `required: false`; malformed context now fails visibly instead of rendering plausible output from undefined variables. `all_registry_templates_conform` walks the shipped registry from `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, rejects malformed or scalar metadata headers, and enforces the no-Python-template policy; it cannot silently skip on the test working directory. **Stanza-2 multi-line comment skip (2026-09-27):** the body-param-stanza scanner only skipped single-line Jinja comments, so a param stanza following a multi-line design comment leaked verbatim into rendered prompts — 27 shipped templates (operator-ordered registry sweep, Algedonic card `1baa0cb7`), plus `listening/apply-template-rag.j2`'s orphaned `verbosity`/`thinking_budget` pair (a mangled edit had removed its `[inference]` stanza header) and `media/logo-discovery-map.j2`'s legacy pre-convention metadata block (never parsed by the header detector; comment-wrapped). The skipper now applies the header loop's multi-line `find("#}")` skip. Pinned by `test_strip_body_inference_stanza_after_multiline_comment` and a new `all_registry_templates_conform` assertion that no stripped template body contains an inference-param line (`temperature`/`work_effort`/`verbosity`/`thinking_budget`); observed RED on the pre-fix tree (28 leaking templates listed) before the fix, GREEN after, and the post-fix census passes over all 275 shipped templates. Path traversal protection via `canonicalize` + `starts_with`, applied to both the top-level ref and `{% include %}` resolution (`bind_registry_loader`: includes resolve within the registry root and receive the same frontmatter stripping as the top-level render; pinned generically — corpus-derived, no hardcoded file names — by `corpus_includes_resolve_and_strip` and `test_include_traversal_is_blocked`). Template base path wired via `agent::set_template_base_path()` (OnceLock) in `main.rs` at startup. `minijinja` workspace dependency in `crates/agent/Cargo.toml`. Registered in `add_default_tools` (alongside `lisp_eval`). **Production template seeding:** `agent_skills::seed_templates` (build.rs embeds `kask/registry/templates/*.j2` via `include_str!`) writes the compiled-in templates to `{kask_data_dir}/skills/registry/templates/` at startup, always (dev and production), so MCP servers (corpus) that read `HKASK_TEMPLATE_ROOT` find them regardless of CWD. `kask_bridge::mcp_env()` always emits `HKASK_TEMPLATE_ROOT` — when the operator hasn't overridden `corpus.template_root`, it resolves to `{data_dir}/skills/registry/`; when overridden, it uses the operator's value. **In-process delivery (2026-09-18):** the editor process never has `HKASK_TEMPLATE_ROOT` set (env injection reaches MCP server children only), so in-process curator-memory chunk tagging threads the root from settings — `KaskSettings::resolved_template_root()` (shared `resolve_template_root` helper, same rule) → `RealMemoryPort` → `WriteContext` → `render_deployed_tagging_prompt_at`; and the swarm server's `config_env` allowlist now carries `HKASK_TEMPLATE_ROOT` + `HKASK_CLASSIFIER_MODEL` for `local_knowledge.rs` agent-response tagging — transitive reads through `hkask-inference` that a crate-local env-var grep cannot see. Seeding also removes `.j2`/`.jinja`/`.yaml` files no longer shipped (and directories left empty), so a retired skill's templates stop resolving from the seeded copy (2026-09-25: 13 retired namespaces, 78 files, were still renderable). **Retired-skill directory sweep (2026-09-28):** the skill-side analog — `seed_shipped_skills_from_source` visits only shipped ∪ dev-source names, so a retired skill's copied directory lingered loadable in installed binaries' global skills dir (stale methodology whose templates may no longer resolve; `kata-coaching` after the ebcd901c80 fold was the live instance). `remove_retired_skill_copies` runs at the top of both seeding paths, removing `RETIRED_SKILL_NAMES` directories — the curated record of every skill that shipped and was later removed (derived 2026-09-28 from git history of `.agents/skills/*/SKILL.md` deletions; the retirement change appends its name there in the same commit). The record is load-bearing in CI (2026-09-30): `kask/scripts/check-retired-record.sh` fails a change range that retires a skill without the same-commit append, and `kask/scripts/check-skill-crossrefs.sh` derives its deleted-skill name list from the record. User safety: only listed names, and only directories holding nothing but the copied `SKILL.md` — symlinks, extra-file directories, and non-directories are refused with a warning, so user-adopted directories and user skills are never swept. Pinned by `test_seed_templates_writes_all_shipped_templates_to_disk`, `test_seed_templates_overwrites_existing_files`, `test_seed_templates_removes_templates_no_longer_shipped`, `test_seed_shipped_skills_removes_retired_skill_copies`, `retired_skill_names_are_absent_from_the_authored_tree` (agent_skills), `mcp_env_always_emits_template_root`, `mcp_env_template_root_follows_data_dir_override`, `mcp_env_template_root_override_emits_user_value`, `resolved_template_root_method_matches_env_emission_rule` (kask_bridge), `explicit_root_variant_renders_the_deployed_template` (hkask-inference), and the updated `swarm_config_env_includes_all_read_vars`. **PDCA:** model-coordinated — the SKILL.md body describes convergence criteria; the model uses `lisp_eval` for deterministic checks and `render_template` for structured prompt scaffolding. **Core-skill frontmatter enforcement** (retained): `parse_skill_file_content_for_loading` refuses `core: true` on non-`CORE_SKILL_NAMES` skills. Marketplace-installed skills (`SkillSource::Public`) and `load_marketplace_skills` removed (skill marketplace retired). D28 storage remains. **Skill outcome recording (operator-ratified 2026-09-08):** `SkillTool::run` fires `agent::record_skill_outcome(skill_id, success, error)` at its outcome points — success on a rendered envelope, failure on missing dependencies or an unreadable body; not-found and authorization-denial are request errors, not skill outcomes, and are deliberately not recorded. The hook is a re-settable `ProcessGlobal` in `agent.rs` (`set_skill_outcome_recorder` / `record_skill_outcome` — the same pattern as `set_mcp_tool_outcome_recorder`); `main.rs` wires it to `RegulationLedger::record_skill_span(skill_id, "outcome", payload)` on the GPUI-global tokio runtime, feeding the metacognition loop's `sense_feedback_drift` (per-skill success-rate decline). This is the write side the read side shipped without — until this wiring the `SkillSpanStore` was permanently empty and drift sensing could never fire. Scope: measures skill *activation* reliability, not work quality (which happens across subsequent turns). The vacuous manifest gate `kask/scripts/check-skill-span-namespace.sh` + its selftest are deleted with this wiring (they validated a `ledger.span_namespace` field no manifest ever declared, in a manifests directory that does not exist — a flagged-but-unfixed "0 = 0" pass); the enforcement is now the runtime pins below. The `refuse_skill_catalog_read` no-op stub and its pin tests are deleted (D1-revert residue: the function returned `Ok(())` unconditionally — a gate that gated nothing, with two tests pinning that it does nothing). **Skill operator-feedback recording (2026-09-09; channel (a) retired 2026-09-24):** the `operator_feedback` ledger reader (the metacognition loop's "declining operator acceptance" drift trend) shipped with no writer — that half of drift sensing read a permanently-empty phase. One producer fires the process-global hook, mirroring the outcome recorder above: `OperatorFeedbackRecorder` + `set_operator_feedback_recorder` / `record_operator_feedback` in `agent.rs` (re-settable `ProcessGlobal`; an unavailable durable recorder returns a typed failure rather than claiming the disposition was stored). The operator's direct rating: the `record_skill_feedback` built-in tool (`tools/record_skill_feedback_tool.rs`; registered only in Curator sessions by `NativeAgent::new_session`, per the D59 evaluation-separation ruling; input: `skill_id`, `accepted`, optional `note`) is the algedonic review's recording surface for the operator's skill evaluation. The former advice-apply bridge and its old test were removed with the queue in Phase D; only the Curator-only `record_skill_feedback` can record a skill verdict. `main.rs` builds the shared `OperatorFeedbackObservation { accepted, note }` payload, persists it synchronously to the curator's `RegulationArchive`, then feeds `RegulationLedger::record_skill_span(skill_id, "operator_feedback", payload)` on the GPUI-global tokio runtime; startup reloads chronological archive records into that bounded working view. Metacognition parses the same type and excludes malformed observations before minimum-sample gating and prior/current window splitting rather than counting them as rejection — the write side the acceptance-drift reader shipped without. Pinned by `operator_acceptance_reads_the_writer_contract`, `malformed_feedback_does_not_create_a_false_drift_window`, `operator_feedback_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable` (agent.rs — records + re-settable), `records_the_operators_disposition` (record_skill_feedback_tool.rs — the direct rating reaches the hook), and `test_native_session_reads_curator_status_without_curator_mutations` (native sessions cannot record skill verdicts). **Three-gate visibility completion (2026-09-09, post-rebuild review):** the tool initially shipped registered but invisible — `Thread::enabled_tools` drops any built-in absent from the shipped `write`/`ask` profile allowlists (`assets/settings/default.json`; `AgentProfileSettings::is_tool_enabled` treats unlisted as disabled), and the tool was missing from all three documented silent-drop gates (the `tools!` macro registry, the profile lists, the permission-UI `EXCLUDED_TOOLS`). Fixed in one pass: `RecordSkillFeedbackTool` added to the `tools!` macro (the SkillTool precedent — macro-listed, per-session-constructed), `"record_skill_feedback": true` added to the write + ask profiles, and an `EXCLUDED_TOOLS` entry (telemetry hook, no permission-relevant side effects). Gate 1 was testless — the silent-drop class had no pin — so `every_built_in_tool_is_listed_in_the_default_write_profile` (tools.rs; `serde_json_lenient` dev-dep parsing the shipped JSONC) now requires every `ALL_TOOL_NAMES` entry to be present in the write profile (true, or false for a deliberate disable — the `ask_user` pattern); observed RED naming the missing tool, GREEN after the profile fix. **Curator tool visibility (2026-09-25):** `curator_directive` and `curator_clear_algedonic_log` were registered in Curator sessions but dropped by gate 2 (absent from the write/ask profiles) — the same registered-but-invisible class. Added to the `tools!` macro, both shipped profiles, and `EXCLUDED_TOOLS`; native sessions still never register them. Pinned by `test_curator_session_registers_directive_tool` (now asserts both reach the model request, observed RED first). **Delegated skill access (F3, 2026-09-24; `host/onto_anchor` added 2026-10-14):** local swarm agents get the Curator's skill tools with one implementation and two callers. `skill_tool.rs` exposes `resolve_invocable_skill` + `activate_skill` (lookup, dependency check, envelope, outcome recording — `SkillTool::run` keeps its authorization step between them); `render_template_tool.rs` exposes `render_registry_template`; `lisp_eval_tool.rs` exposes `evaluate_lisp` and `evaluate_lisp_controlled`; `onto_anchor_tool.rs` exposes `resolve_onto_anchor`; `agent.rs` adds `activate_delegated_skill` over the app-published `SkillIndex`. `kask_bridge/src/host_skill_tools.rs` wraps the governed `ToolPort` so `host/skill`, `host/render_template`, `host/lisp_eval` and `host/onto_anchor` flow through the existing IPC allowlist + parent-grant checks; the swarm grant always carries them. Delegated activation is not operator-prompted (the card declaration is the authorization, as for `mcp_tools`). Pinned by `host_skill_tools` tests (incl. `onto_anchor_runs_the_editor_resolver`), `delegated_grants_follow_settings_and_launch_lifecycle` (kask_bridge) and `declared_skill_loads_through_the_host_skill_tool` / `undeclared_skill_is_refused_before_dispatch` (swarm). Pinned by `test_skill_tool_returns_content`, `test_system_prompt_skills_section_describes_body_retrieval`, `test_skill_tool_missing_dependency_fails_fast` (dependency pre-check), `skill_outcome_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable` (agent.rs — hook records + is re-settable), and `test_skill_tool_records_outcome` (skill_tool.rs — success/missing-deps recorded, not-found not recorded). **Teaching input deserialization (lisp-repair L1, 2026-09-28):** the shared `deserialize_maybe_stringified` helper (a previously unrecorded fork addition in `crates/agent/src/tools.rs` that tolerated stringified nested arguments) is replaced by `deserialize_teaching_field`: every rejection names the field, the received shape, and the accepted shape, surfaces inner missing/unknown-field detail, and strips position noise — an error that does not teach produces identical retries (the 2026-09-28 audit lockout anatomy: 5 identical malformed-env emissions hard-refused by the per-input tracker). Per-field wrappers: `lisp_eval` (form/env/max_steps/max_depth; `maxSteps`/`maxDepth` serde aliases apply emitted budgets instead of silently dropping them), `edit_file` (edits + the partial-input edits), `render_template` (context). `evaluate_lisp`'s unbound-symbol error names the sorted env bindings and, when an `env`-named binding is present, the flatten fix — shared with `host/lisp_eval` dispatch. **TypeError teaching (lisp-repair L3, 2026-10-02):** a `TypeError` whose actual is `list` fires only when env bindings carry JSON objects — the wrapped-scalar signature (`{"a": {"n": 1}}` instead of `{"a": 1}`), observed live as three identical retries in one session including one under explicit deliberation (`skill_use_issue:hypothesis-framer` / `skill_use_issue:region-routing`) — and names the object-carrying bindings plus the flatten fix; a type error without object bindings, or with a non-list actual (the objects are not the culprit), keeps the plain message. The env field doc carries the flat-scalar example. Pinned by `env_wrapped_scalar_type_error_teaches_the_flatten_fix` (observed RED on the pre-fix tree, GREEN after) plus the two plain-message controls (`type_error_from_an_array_binding_keeps_the_plain_message`, `type_error_with_object_bindings_but_non_list_actual_keeps_the_plain_message`) in the emission-variant matrix (`crates/agent/src/tools/lisp_eval_emission_matrix.rs`, 31 tests) and the updated `test_wrong_edit_field_names_produce_actionable_error`. **Cognitive-scaffolding hardening (2026-10-14; map-schema workaround replaced 2026-10-20):** (1) the original shared permissive-map schema override for `lisp_eval.env` and `RenderTemplateToolInput::context` has been removed in favor of the provider-neutral JSON-text contract recorded below; the initial field-value schema had pressured providers to wrap scalars as objects; (2) `render_template_tool.rs`'s duplicate comment-skipping and `[inference]` header-splitting loops (`template_metadata_header` vs `strip_frontmatter`) are unified into `split_template_frontmatter`, closing the whitespace/CRLF divergence where `strip_frontmatter` stripped a header that `template_metadata_header` missed (silently bypassing `validate_contract_inputs`); (3) `RenderTemplateToolInput::template_ref` now uses `deserialize_teaching_field`. Current pins: `context_schema_advertises_json_text`, `context_and_env_advertise_the_same_json_text_wire_schema`, `header_with_trailing_whitespace_or_crlf_does_not_bypass_contract_validation`, and `non_string_template_ref_teaches_field_and_shape`; the former permissive-map shape assertions were replaced, not retained. **Defensive-degradation & absent-key rulings (2026-10-14, R-1..R-5):** (R-1) `nth` rejects negative indices with a teaching `Runtime` error (`nth: negative index -1 — indices are 0-based…`) instead of wrapping to `usize::MAX` and returning `null`; (R-2) `string=` errors with `TypeError` on non-string scalars/lists (`Int`/`Float`/`Bool`/`List`) while preserving `Nil -> false` for optional/absent JSON keys; (R-3) `assoc` rejects non-string primitive scalars (`Int`/`Float`/`Bool`) as the alist argument, a new strict `(assoc! key alist)` builtin errors naming the missing key and the sorted available keys when absent, and `evaluate_lisp` teaches `assoc!` / `is_null` when a numeric comparator receives `nil`; (R-4) `evaluate_lisp` teaches that `assoc` returns the value directly (not a `(key . value)` cons pair) when `car`/`cdr` fails on a scalar in an `assoc`-containing form, and the two `(cdr (assoc ...))` skill forms (`scenario-planning/SKILL.md`, `upstream-rebase/SKILL.md`) are fixed; (R-5) `maxSteps`/`maxDepth` camelCase budget aliases are ratified. Pinned by `lie_fix_nth_negative_index_errors_typed`, `lie_fix_string_equals_errors_on_non_string_and_allows_nil`, and `assoc_strict_and_absent_key_teaching_close_vacuity_and_cdr_traps` (observed RED before, GREEN after). |
| D2  | Curator agent                                                                           | `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/agent_ui.rs` + `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` + `crates/hkask-steer/`                                                                                                                                                      | `Agent::Curator` variant; `CURATOR_AGENT_ID`; selectable in Agent Panel. The Curator overlay's `CURATOR_STATIC_CONTEXT` (`crates/agent/src/curator_agent_server.rs`) is appended via `Thread::set_static_context` (delegates to `KaskThreadState::set_static_context`) and rendered by the `## Session Context` block in `system_prompt.hbs`; that block is a **sibling** of the `{{#if (or user_agents_md has_rules)}}` custom-instructions guard (not nested inside it), so it renders even for projects with no `.rules` and no personal `AGENTS.md` (regression: previously nested → silently dropped). Pinned by `test_system_prompt_renders_session_context_without_rules_or_agents_md` in `templates.rs` and `test_curator_sessions_carry_panel_prompt_with_full_tool_surface` in `agent.rs`. The separate, test-only full-template override (originally `43e6e38426`) was removed: no production caller selected it, while the Curator and Steer overlays still compose with the base template. **`with_extra_static_context` is zed-kask's mode mechanism.** Upstream Zed has no modes; Cline/Roo deliver phase constraint via a runtime mode token. zed-kask instead composes a per-context overlay onto the base prompt through this one channel: `CURATOR_STATIC_CONTEXT` (~1.1 KB) plus, in Steer, `swarm_panel::steer_system_prompt` (~9.7 KB) or `kanban_panel::steer_system_prompt` (~2.2 KB). Steer conversations retain the full MCP surface: operator ruling 2026-09-18 deprecated per-server scoping because panel work composes company, scenario, research, media, kanban, swarm, and other domains. The overlay frames the workflow and highlights relevant tools; it is not an authorization boundary. Two consequences follow, and both have bitten: (1) an overlay is captured **once** at conversation construction, so any panel state it bakes in must invalidate the conversation on change — the shared `hkask_steer::SteerSurface` ( `crates/hkask-steer/` ) owns this lifecycle (`ensure` / `invalidate`); the compose flow calls `steer.invalidate()` for this reason; (2) an overlay that advertises tool names must derive them from the server's actual tool list, or a rename degrades to "tool not found" at dispatch — since 2026-09-04 the Steer overlays (swarm, kanban, media) RENDER their tool lists from the server's build.rs-generated `TOOL_NAMES` (`hkask_steer::render_grouped_tool_advertisement` / `render_tool_names`; since the 2026-10-22 ABW removal the swarm panel renders `parse::SWARM_TOOLS` — the server's full 37-tool `TOOL_NAMES` const — directly, with no cloud/local split), so the lists cannot drift (the 2026-09-03 media consolidation shipped stale names because the lists were hand-written); `hkask_steer::verify_tool_advertisement` remains the check on prose tool mentions, and the panels' prompt-token tests (`steer_prompt_advertises_only_known_tools` + `server_tools_are_all_advertised`, in every panel) are the CI enforcement. **Steer split (2026-09-07, operator ruling that only `kask_bridge` may depend on zed crates):** the portable half of `hkask-steer` — `advertised_tool_names`, `render_tool_names`, `render_grouped_tool_advertisement`, `verify_tool_advertisement` + their 8 unit tests — moved to `kask/crates/hkask-steer-core` (std + `log` only, no editor closure); `crates/hkask-steer` keeps the `ConversationView` lifecycle (`SteerSurface`/`SteerContext`/`ensure_steer`/`ThreadPicker`) and re-exports the core fns, so all panels' `hkask_steer::` call sites and the prompt-token tests are unchanged. Treat these as the two standing obligations for any new overlay. **Interaction-mode block (2026-09-27):** `NativeAgent::new_session` reads the user's `kask.curator.interaction_mode` (D9) for each new Curator thread and appends `curator_agent_server::interaction_mode_context(mode)` after the captured Curator context, so a changed level applies to the next thread without reconnecting. Pinned by `test_curator_session_carries_the_users_interaction_mode` and `each_level_gives_the_curator_distinct_guidance`; its tool mentions are covered by `test_curator_overlay_advertises_only_registered_tool_names`. **Values-grounding block (2026-10-08):** `CURATOR_STATIC_CONTEXT` carries the Magnifica Humanitas values charter's eight values (V1–V8) and its discernment test ("does this make human life more human?"), plus a standing pull instruction (`curator_federated_search`) before value-laden decisions — the charter's runtime reach into the Curator context (investigation report 2026-10-08, recommendation R1; the charter's Wiring list records the wiring in the same pass). Pinned by `curator_static_context_carries_values_grounding`. **Resume-path re-application (2026-10-08):** the per-session surface — the shared `CuratorStatusTool` plus, for Curator agents, the overlay static context with the dyad level, `CURATOR_AGENT_ID` (D6), and the curator action tools — is extracted into `NativeAgent::apply_session_surface`, called from BOTH `new_session` and the resume path (`open_thread`, before `register_session`). `Thread::from_db` restores neither session tools nor kask state (`KaskThreadState::new()` defaults), so a Curator thread resumed across a restart rendered without `CURATOR_STATIC_CONTEXT` and lost its curator tools while `register_session` still tagged it `CURATOR_AGENT_ID` — the UI kept saying Curator (observed live 2026-10-08: a resumed Curator investigation thread carried the label with no overlay). Subagent threads register directly (`create_subagent_thread`) and never receive the curator action tools (D59). Pinned by `test_resumed_curator_thread_reapplies_the_overlay` and `test_resumed_native_thread_keeps_curator_tools_out` (both observed RED on the pre-fix tree). **State-block per-session fetch (2026-10-08):** the `## Current System State` health snapshot is appended per Curator session in `NativeAgent::apply_session_surface` — with a bounded wait (12 × 5s) for the loop's first snapshot, since `MetacognitionLoop::run` skips the first immediate tick and the first snapshot lands ~30s after launch — NOT baked once at `CuratorAgentServer::connect`. The connect-time fetch captured the app-start state before the loop's first tick, and the cached connection (`AgentConnectionStore`) served every Curator thread that empty block for the whole session (observed live 2026-10-08: a fresh Curator thread hours after restart rendered the full overlay with no state block, while `curator_status` — which reads the provider live — returned a real snapshot). A snapshot that never arrives after the bounded wait logs a warning naming the cause, never a silent empty. Steer conversations (the same session path) gain the block too. Pinned by `test_curator_session_fetches_the_state_block_per_session` (observed RED on the pre-fix tree; the fake provider models the loop ticking between connect and session creation). **Refactored 2026-08-27:** the Steer conversation lifecycle (previously hand-rolled per panel as `ensure_steer_conversation` + `steer_conversation`/`steer_connection_store` fields) is shared in `crates/hkask-steer/`; the hand-mirrored kanban tool lists (`ADVERTISED_KANBAN_TOOLS`, `parse::KANBAN_TOOLS`) are deleted in favor of the kanban server's generated `TOOL_NAMES`. **One-call helper added 2026-08-28:** `hkask_steer::ensure_steer` combines `verify_tool_advertisement` + `SteerSurface::ensure`; panels pass a `SteerContext` + prefixes and the helper bakes the prompt, lands the view, and caches it. It is the panel-facing API; the raw `ensure` remains exposed for tests with a stub factory. **Portfolio analysis panel (added 2026-08-28, redesigned 2026-09-17) removed 2026-10-15** with the portfolio MCP server (operator ruling: no position tracking): `crates/portfolio_panel`, its status-bar button and `init` registration, and the `hkask_mcp_portfolio::TOOL_NAMES`-derived prompt advertisement are deleted; the Steer overlay set is three (swarm, kanban, media). `crates/hkask-steer/src/vertical_split.rs` remains, now media-panel-only; its clamp/zero-height tests still pin the divider behavior. **Refactored 2026-08-26:** `agent_static_context` moved from `Thread` to `KaskThreadState` (`crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs`); the later-removed full-template override was moved there in the same refactor. The former per-server scope field, setters, builder, `enabled_tools` filter, and exclusion tests were deleted by the 2026-09-18 ruling. `test_context_server_tools_remain_cross_domain` now pins that independently registered servers remain visible together, and `test_curator_sessions_carry_panel_prompt_with_full_tool_surface` pins that workflow overlays still compose with Curator context. (The refactor plan doc `kask/docs/plans/thread-hooks-refactor-2026-08-26.md` has since been removed; this entry is the record.)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| D3  | Governed MCP child-process tool runtime                                                                  | `crates/zed/src/main.rs`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `McpRuntime` implements `ToolPort` directly (call metering + `reg.mcp.cap` spans and a `SpanKind::ToolCompleted` RegulationRecord at target `reg.mcp`; the `reg.tool` ontology tag was removed in e0ca5d5f7f) and is passed wherever a `ToolPort` is needed — no bridge adapter. MCP servers run as child processes (stdio). Daemon transport deleted; identity from `ServerContext.webid` (`HKASK_WEBID`). **`McpRuntime::invoke` performs NO per-call authorization.** The former capability-match gate was deleted 2026-08-12: all three production mint sites derived the `DelegationToken`'s `resource_id` from the same tool name they passed to `invoke`, so `is_valid_for` compared a caller-supplied value against itself and could not deny — it ran on every tool call and denied nothing while being advertised here as "the enforced gate." `DelegationToken`, `panel_default_token`, `capabilities_match`, `capability_from_server_id`, and `ToolPortError::CapabilityDenied` are gone; `invoke` now takes `agent: WebID` (accounting identity, not a credential). Authority lives at the boundaries whose list the caller does not choose: the inference IPC parent-held grant intersected with `tool_allowlist` (fail-closed, D8), the swarm card `mcp_tools` allowlist, and the per-server MCP env/credential allowlists. Previously pinned by `tests/invoke_gate.rs::{unregistered_agent_is_auto_registered_not_denied, exhausted_ceiling_trips_the_runaway_breaker, no_governance_dispatches_unmetered, unreachable_server_reports_unavailable_not_failed, only_unavailable_is_retryable, interrupted_is_never_auto_retried, interrupted_and_unavailable_are_distinguishable, unknown_tool_is_not_found_not_unavailable}` (public-API metering + retry-classification tests); that file and the inline `reconnect_path_tests` module were both deleted with the condenser teardown and **not restored** — `runtime.rs` has no `#[cfg(test)]` section and the `kask/crates/hkask-mcp/tests/` directory no longer exists. **Restored 2026-08-24:** the metering + retry-classification behavior is now re-pinned by `runtime.rs::metering_tests::{unregistered_agent_is_auto_registered_not_denied, exhausted_ceiling_trips_the_runaway_breaker, no_governance_dispatches_unmetered, only_unavailable_is_retryable, interrupted_is_never_auto_retried, interrupted_and_unavailable_are_distinguishable, unknown_tool_is_not_found_not_unavailable}` (7 inline tests). Settings-change restart: governed McpRuntime instances are started once at login; `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` (a `SettingsStore` observer) diffs each server's computed env (`kask_server_env`) against the launch baseline and restarts changed servers via `McpRuntime::stop_server` + `start_server_with_env`, so e.g. a `kask.swarm.mode` toggle re-routes the governed path; the restart logs the *changed env keys* (never values — several are credentials) because a restart tears down live connections and fails every in-flight panel call, so "env changed" alone could not distinguish a deliberate toggle from a startup-ordering artifact. **Connections self-heal.** A dead connection used to be sticky and unrecoverable: the keeper task's `running.waiting()` arm exited without removing the entry, `start_server_with_env` early-returned on a presence-based `connections.contains_key` check, and `get_peer` handed out the corpse forever — so every call returned `Transport closed` until an operator changed a setting. Four mechanisms now close that loop (`kask/crates/hkask-mcp/src/runtime.rs`): (1) the keeper task reaps its own connection when the service loop exits on its own, guarded by a generation stamp so a late-exiting keeper cannot remove a healthy replacement installed by a reconnect; (2) `get_peer` filters peers whose `is_transport_closed()` is already true, covering the window before the keeper is scheduled; (3) `start_server_with_env` records a `LaunchSpec` so `call_tool_inner` reconnects on demand and retries **once**, bounded by `RECONNECT_COOLDOWN` (5s) so a crash-looping binary cannot become a process-spawn storm; **(4) a per-server health supervisor** checks transport liveness every 60s (`HEALTH_CHECK_INTERVAL`) and proactively removes dead connections before the next tool call discovers them, with a circuit breaker that stops auto-healing after `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_HEALTH_FAILURES` (3) consecutive failures and logs an operator-actionable error. **The breaker is real as of 2026-08-29** — before that, the supervisor only degraded its check interval and restarted forever (the live crash-loop defect: a keyless instance respawned a new pid every interval with no stop condition, contradicting this advertised invariant). The stop is not a permanent outage: any explicit start (settings-change restart, operator action, `call_tool_inner`'s on-demand reconnect) spawns a fresh supervisor whose first healthy check resets the counter. The degraded-interval mechanism (`HKASK_MCP_DEGRADED_HEALTH_CHECK_INTERVAL_SECS`) was removed with it. Breaker + idempotence pinned by `reconnect_integration.rs::{health_breaker_trips_after_consecutive_restart_failures, second_start_with_same_env_is_a_noop}` (invariants I4/I5 of the MCP lifecycle review). The supervisor does not call `try_reconnect` directly (the future is not `Send`); it removes the dead connection and relies on mechanism (3) for healing. `start_server_with_env` also retries spawn+handshake up to `STARTUP_MAX_RETRIES` (3) with exponential backoff (500ms→10s) before reporting failure, and pipes child stderr to `tracing` with `target: "hkask.mcp.child"` and the `server_id` field for attributed diagnostics. A TOCTOU race between concurrent `start_server_with_env` calls (e.g. startup + on-demand reconnect) is handled by checking at insert time: the loser's keeper is cancelled and its child process killed, the winner's connection is kept. `stop_server`/`shutdown_all` drop the launch spec and clear `health_failures` so a deliberate stop is not resurrected. The idempotency check is now liveness-based, not presence-based. **Retry safety is classified by provable delivery, not by "was it a transport error".** `ToolPortError` gained two variants: `Unavailable` (no live peer accepted the request — the tool provably did not run, so a retry cannot double a side effect) and `Interrupted` (a live peer accepted it and the connection then failed — **the outcome is unknown**). The distinction is forced by rmcp, which reports both a failed send (`service.rs:921`) and a dropped response channel (`service.rs:555,566`) as the same `ServiceError::TransportClosed`: once a request reaches a live peer, a transport loss is not proof of non-delivery. `is_retryable()` is true for `Unavailable` only; `Interrupted` is never auto-retried, at any layer, because doing so would duplicate state-changing effects (two `kanban_task_create`s, a `swarm_hire` charging credits twice). Internally `DispatchError::{NotDelivered, Interrupted, Failed}` carries this, and only `NotDelivered` drives the one-shot reconnect-and-retry. Reconnect-path recording and cooldown are pinned by `runtime.rs::reconnect_path_tests::{unknown_server_cannot_be_reconnected, stop_server_clears_the_reconnect_path, shutdown_all_clears_every_reconnect_path, reconnect_is_rate_limited_by_the_cooldown, failed_start_still_records_a_launch_spec_for_later_reconnect}`. Unknown servers have no launch specification; failed starts retain one for later recovery, and deliberate stop/shutdown clear it. Healing was previously verified against real child processes by `tests/reconnect_integration.rs` (5 tests, `--features test-fixture`), which SIGKILLs a controllable fixture MCP server (`src/bin/mcp_test_fixture.rs`) and asserts the next call reconnects and is served by a *new* pid; that file was deleted with the condenser teardown and **not restored** — the `kask/crates/hkask-mcp/tests/` directory no longer exists. The mutation tests (`killed_server_is_reconnected_on_the_next_call`, `dead_connection_is_reaped_from_the_runtime`) are gone with it, so the four self-heal mechanisms are currently unpinned. **Restored 2026-08-24:** `tests/reconnect_integration.rs` is restored with 7 integration tests (`--features test-fixture`, `--test-threads=1`): `killed_server_is_reconnected_on_the_next_call` (mechanism 3 — on-demand reconnect), `dead_connection_is_reaped_from_the_runtime` (mechanism 1 — keeper reaps), `stop_server_then_call_returns_unavailable` (deliberate-stop clearing), `startup_retry_with_backoff` (startup retry), `health_supervisor_removes_dead_connection_without_tool_call` (mechanism 4 — health supervisor, using `HKASK_MCP_HEALTH_CHECK_INTERVAL_SECS=1` to reduce the 60s wait), plus `second_start_with_same_env_is_a_noop` and `health_breaker_trips_after_consecutive_restart_failures` (the I4/I5 invariants cited above). The four self-heal mechanisms are now pinned. The fixture's kill path uses `libc::kill` and **asserts** success, because an earlier version that swallowed the result with `let _ =` made the whole suite pass vacuously with all healing disabled. **Server-side replay protection resolves the `Interrupted` ambiguity for the three tools that cannot absorb a duplicate.** `Interrupted` is unavoidable at the client, so it can only be *resolved* at the server: `hkask-mcp-kata-kanban/src/idempotency.rs` stores `(tool, key) → first response` in the kanban database, and `kanban_task_create` / `kanban_board_create` / `kanban_task_spawn` accept an optional `idempotency_key` and return the original response (plus `replayed: true`) instead of re-running. The claim is a single `INSERT OR IGNORE` whose zero-rowcount *is* the already-seen signal, so two concurrent replays cannot both win — no read-then-write race, and no transaction (`dyn DatabaseDriver` exposes none). Reservation is two-phase (`reserve` → work → `record`) because the work happens between: a key claimed but never recorded is `Pending`, reported as unknown rather than silently re-run, since whether it landed is precisely what is unknown; a cleanly-failed call calls `release` so a retry starts fresh instead of inheriting that verdict. Only these three needed it — `task_update`/`task_assign`/`task_delete`/`board_delete` already converge, and `swarm_clone_to_local`/`swarm_push_to_cloud`/`swarm_create_local_agent` are keyed by `agent_name` (the two cloud bridges were removed with the ABW backend, 2026-10-22). `kanban_goal_create` also accepts an `idempotency_key`. Goals now persist in the encrypted kanban DB through resolution until curator-memory acknowledgment (operator ruling 2026-09-16); their replay protection shares the durable kanban idempotency driver. A same-key create after restart returns the original live goal instead of minting a duplicate or a ghost pointer. Pinned by `goal_replay_protection_survives_a_restart_and_replays_the_live_goal` and `goal_replay_is_absorbed_within_the_process` (the latter's in-memory driver correctly reports `idempotency_durable: false`). **`swarm_hire` was already protected** by `spend_gate`'s consume→spend→refund over a single-use `ConsentStore` token (`spend_gate.rs` — `authorize_hire`/`complete_hire`; atomic across processes via `DELETE`-rowcount; historical record — `swarm_hire`, the spend gate, and the consent store were removed with the ABW cloud backend, 2026-10-22), so a replayed hire fails closed rather than double-charging; the client-side warning remains because the *outcome* is still unknown, which is a query problem, not a safety one. `kanban_panel` generates one key per operator **gesture** (before the retry loop, not per attempt — per-attempt keys would defeat the mechanism entirely) and widens its retry to cover `Interrupted` for exactly `IDEMPOTENT_TOOLS`. Because the panel injects the key into a raw JSON object, nothing ties the two sides together at compile time and a rename would silently disable protection via `#[serde(default)]` — pinned by `wire_field_name_matches_what_clients_send`. Non-durable degradation is labelled (`idempotency_durable: false`) when the kanban DB is in-memory, per the advertised-invariant rule. Previously pinned by `tests/idempotent_creates.rs` (15, incl. cross-process replay, row-count oracles, and `wire_field_name_matches_what_clients_send`) + `kanban_panel::tests` key-policy tests; the file was deleted with the condenser teardown and **restored** — `tests/idempotent_creates.rs` is live (17 tests as of 2026-08-29, including the two goal-replay pins above).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| D5  | Keychain access                                                                         | `kask/crates/hkask-keystore`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | **Single keychain namespace architecture.** Active data-service API keys (Exa, Tavily, Brave, SerpAPI, Firecrawl, FMP, EODHD, FRED, etc.) are stored in zed's `CredentialsProvider` keychain namespace under `kask://credentials/<key>` (label `zed-github-account`, attribute `url=kask://credentials/<key>`). **Inference-provider keys (OpenRouter, DeepInfra, RunPod, KiloCode) are the one exception (2026-08-31 consolidation; KiloCode added 2026-09-19): each lives at its provider's `api_url` keychain slot (`https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`, `https://api.deepinfra.com/v1/openai`, `https://api.runpod.io`, `https://api.kilo.ai/api/gateway`) — the same slot zed's `ApiKeyState` reads — resolved by `kask_bridge::credential_url_for_key`; nothing reads or writes `kask://credentials/{openrouter,deepinfra,runpod,kilocode}` anymore (the former two-slot state was the split-brain behind the 2026-08-31 DeepInfra 401: a stale kask-slot copy fed MCP servers a dead key while the user's fresh key sat unread at `api_url`). `build_mcp_server_env` injects only credentials named by a remaining child's explicit allowlist. Native-only provider keys — RunPod OCR and KiloCode chat — stay at their provider slots and never enter child env or child-restart decisions. The server's `resolve_credential` reads API keys from env vars only — there is NO `service=hkask` keychain fallback for API keys. The `service=hkask` namespace no longer exists (its startup purge was deleted 2026-09-25 once the keychain held no entries); do not write to it. The single internal key is `hkask_db_passphrase` (underscore), living in the same `kask://credentials/` namespace; there is ONE passphrase (`HKASK_DB_PASSPHRASE`) for every SQLCipher DB — no separate swarm-memory passphrase exists. There are NO `*_enabled` settings toggles — the key's presence in the keychain IS the toggle. The `.env` file loading has been fully removed — the keychain is the single source of truth for API keys. The a2a secret threading was removed (self-referential token verification — security theater); the in-process `DelegationToken` that replaced it was itself removed 2026-08-12 (see D3, per-call capability gate removed: it compared a value with itself). The unused RunPod S3 credential registry entries are removed; their keychain slots were purged and the one-time purge deleted 2026-09-25. Training-only HuggingFace/Nebius settings, recovery, read and injection paths were removed 2026-10-17; any pre-existing dormant kask keychain entries remain operator-owned and unread. **ABW removal (2026-10-22):** the ABW API key credential was removed with the cloud swarm backend (the `HKASK_ABW_API_KEY` data-service descriptor and the swarm server's credential allowlist entry are gone). `kask_bridge::purge_removed_abw_api_key` — called once at zed startup, next to this seam's keychain wiring in `crates/zed/src/main.rs` — deletes any pre-existing `kask://credentials/hkask_abw_api_key` entry and logs the outcome loudly (Decision A of the ABW-removal plan (plan artifact deleted on execution 2026-10-25 per the house docs lifecycle; git history holds it): a dormant third-party secret with no remaining read path or management surface is worse than a logged one-time delete; delete is a no-op when the entry is already absent). The live RunPod provider `api_url` key is untouched. **Reduct.cloud removal (2026-10-22):** the Reduct.video cloud connection was removed from the media MCP server end-to-end (20 `reduct_*` tools, the `tools/reduct.rs` client, the `REDUCT_API_KEY` data-service descriptor and the media server's credential-allowlist entry, the Data Services settings row, the provider profile `reduct.yaml`, and the media panel's Reduct group — plan artifact deleted on execution 2026-10-25 per the house docs lifecycle; git history holds it). `kask_bridge::purge_removed_reduct_api_key` — called once at zed startup, next to the ABW purge in `crates/zed/src/main.rs` — deletes any pre-existing `kask://credentials/reduct_api_key` entry and logs the outcome loudly (the same Decision A posture). Local educt keeps the Reduct transcript-linked-media reference model (D35); the local media surface is unchanged at 71 tools. **Startup credential-recovery sweep (2026-10-26, cards 810bf6af/fec4e7c7):** a transient keychain failure (`KeychainError::Timeout`/`Platform` — the wedged-portal class, live 2026-10-25/26 when the gnome-keyring daemon died mid-startup) no longer degrades a session until restart: `kask_bridge::credential_recovery` (fork-owned module — `CredentialRecoverySweep`, the canary probe, 7 tests) classifies a probe of `kask://credentials/hkask_db_passphrase`; `NotFound` stays terminal (genuinely absent is configuration, not a fault) while Timeout/Platform arm a bounded retry sweep that re-fires `SettingsStore::notify_observers` once on portal recovery, re-triggering the provider credential loads and the MCP env rebuild (the 2026-10-26 live recovery through incidental observer fires is the reference shape). Armed from zed's main.rs deferred task (`arm_startup_credential_recovery`); happy path silent — a healthy startup probes once at T+20s and disarms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| D6  | Thread → memory                                                                         | `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` + `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` + `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` + `kask/crates/hkask-types` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/memory.rs`                                                                                                                                                     | `MemoryPort` trait in `hkask-types` (carries `agent_id` so the port routes by owning agent). `BridgeMemoryPort` + `RealMemoryPort` in `kask_bridge`. Global hook `agent::set_memory_port()` (Mutex — re-settable), wired in the deferred task at startup (no longer gated on Zed user — uses fallback identity "kask" if user hasn't resolved). Thread turn completion ingests via `cx.background_spawn()`. `Thread` carries `agent_id: AgentId` (now on `KaskThreadState`, accessed via `thread.kask.agent_id()`) — identity by construction (2026-09-28): `ZED_AGENT_ID` at `KaskThreadState::new()`, overridden to `CURATOR_AGENT_ID` by `NativeAgent::apply_session_surface` — called from `new_session` AND the resume path (`open_thread`), since `Thread::from_db` resets to the default — when the agent is the Curator, inherited from the parent by subagents (`inherit_from`). No read-site default remains (the former `unwrap_or_else(ZED_AGENT_ID)` at the `run_tool` failure stamp is deleted), so a thread can never be silently classified — the misread class the 2026-09-27 write-tool reachability probe reported (h_mem `cd1ef984`). **All turns are ingested** — both curator and zed agent turns are written to the curator's DB. **In-process tagging template root (2026-09-18):** `RealMemoryPort` threads the host-deployed template registry root into `WriteContext` so write-time chunk tagging renders `tag-passages-batch` in-process via `render_deployed_tagging_prompt_at` — the `HKASK_TEMPLATE_ROOT` env seam only reaches MCP server children (the shared resolution rule is recorded under D1). Pinned by `chunk_tagging_renders_from_the_threaded_template_root` (kask_bridge ingest tests). **In-process classifier-setting precedence (2026-10-18):** `main.rs` now resolves write-time chunk tagging through `KaskModelsSettings::classifier_model_for_host()` only. The saved Settings → Kask → Models classifier is authoritative for host-process inference; an inherited `HKASK_CLASSIFIER_MODEL` is child/CLI configuration and cannot override a later operator choice. MCP children continue to receive the same trimmed saved value through `emit_models_env`, while direct CLI processes may intentionally use the env resolver. Blank/whitespace settings visibly degrade to structural-only tagging. Pinned by `host_classifier_uses_saved_setting_and_normalizes_whitespace`, `host_classifier_treats_blank_setting_as_unconfigured`, `saved_classifier_reaches_host_and_mcp_children_without_fallback` (kask_bridge), and `kask_host_classifier_uses_the_saved_setting_not_process_env` (zed main source pin). `RealMemoryPort::ingest_turn` branches on `agent_id`: Curator turns go to the curator's sovereign `agents/curator/curator.db` as a curator-perspective h_mem (Private, `curator_webid`) + a shared copy (Shared) for curator recall. Non-curator turns get a shared copy only (no curator-perspective h_mem). **Recall is curator-scoped (operator ruling 2026-09-28):** the former non-curator recall path — `BridgeContextInjector::inject_context` calling the `MemoryPort` trait's `recall_context`/`recall_thread`, whose only production impl returned a hardcoded empty, then filtering, heading, and absence-signaling that guaranteed-empty result — is removed. `inject_context` returns empty for non-curator threads (no headers, no filters, no false "No relevant memory found" hypocognition message); `RealMemoryPort`'s redundant no-op trait impls are deleted (the trait's default no-op recall methods remain as the `hkask-types` port contract); the curator path is unchanged. The agent-side injector stays wired for interface stability — wiring zed-agent recall (thread-scoped or shared-store) is a separate product decision. Pinned by `non_curator_injector_returns_empty_without_absence_message` (context_injector.rs). The curator recalls its own memory via `recall_context_curator` / `recall_thread_curator` (inherent methods on `RealMemoryPort`, not on the `MemoryPort` trait). **The episodic/semantic distinction has been completely removed** — all h_mems are unified; the ontology blob carries dual-axis anchoring (PKO process + DC state) but there is no type distinction. Consolidation is confidence-based cleanup only (delete low-confidence, prune to budget) — no promotion, no re-tagging. **Swarm memory lives in the swarm MCP server, not the bridge.** Local swarm agents execute via `LocalSwarmRuntime::delegate` inside the `hkask-mcp-swarm` process, so their turns never reach the bridge's `Thread`/`ingest_turn` — the bridge cannot observe them. The shared `swarm_memory.db` (one DB for all swarms and all agents — a single knowledgebase swarms share) is written and read entirely in the swarm server: `local_knowledge::ingest_turn` stores each delegation's full turn (task + response + model) as an h_mem under a per-turn entity (`agent:<agent_id>:turn:<uuid>`) plus an embedding of the task, wired into `swarm_delegate_local` + `swarm_execute_plan_local`. `local_knowledge::recall_turns` + the `swarm_recall_local` MCP tool recall prior turns by semantic similarity via the real `InferencePort::embed` (IPC bridge → `LanguageModelEmbeddingPort`); `search_similar` has no agent filter, so a turn any agent produced is retrievable by any other (the shared-knowledgebase property). The bridge-side `SwarmStore`, `recall_context_swarm`, the `swarm_store` field, and `memory_health_json`'s `swarm_store` entry were **deleted** as dead surface. Pinned by `local_knowledge::tests::{ingest_turn_stores_full_turn_retrievable_by_recall, recall_spans_all_agents_shared_knowledgebase, ingest_turn_skips_and_recall_errors_when_store_unavailable, multiple_turns_accumulate_in_shared_knowledgebase}` + the updated `memory_health_json_reports_degraded_without_healing` (curator-only `degraded` now). **Refactored 2026-08-26:** the `agent_id` field and all kask-specific per-thread state moved from `Thread` to `KaskThreadState` (`crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs`). The turn-loop call sites use `self.kask.agent_id()` / `self.kask.check_tool_retry()` etc. (The refactor plan doc `kask/docs/plans/thread-hooks-refactor-2026-08-26.md` has since been removed; this entry is the record.) **Curator memory-edit tool gating removed 2026-09-01:** the `is_curator_memory_edit_tool`/`is_curator_thread` gate that restricted `memory_insert`/`memory_update`/`memory_resolve_contradiction` to curator threads was removed by operator decision — live evidence showed models cannot reliably emit calls to functions absent from their visible list (4/4 announce-then-stop on GLM 5.3), so the gate produced silent no-effect stops and blocked operator-approved memory writes. `is_curator_memory_edit_tool`, `is_curator_thread`, and the Pin B6 classifier test were deleted; the D44 marker text, the then-present failure map's #4/#7 (that unwired map has since been removed), the curator server tool descriptions, and `memory-system-specification.md` §9/§10 were updated in the same pass. Pinned by `test_curator_memory_edit_tools_available_to_non_curator_threads` (`crates/agent/src/tests/mod.rs`). **Goal custody cutover (2026-10-20, D58):** response-only goal capture, the turn-scoped goal buffer, score-result ingestion reports and turn-end pruning are removed. Service-owned assessment/indexing drain through `kask_bridge::GoalHost`, independent of turn completion; one current recall row preserves source/criteria and separate histories without score-to-confidence mutation (canonical contract in D58). The obsolete `GoalEvent` type and `ports.rs` re-export are removed; current goal custody does not pass through `MemoryPort` turn ingestion. Turn-chunk ingestion and recall described above are unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D7  | App-identity                                                                            | `crates/paths/src/paths.rs`, `crates/cli/src/main.rs`, `crates/release_channel/src/lib.rs`, `crates/zed/src/zed/mac_only_instance.rs`, `crates/title_bar/src/title_bar.rs` + `crates/title_bar/Cargo.toml` (in-app updater and simulation removed; updates via the terminal script), `crates/zed/Cargo.toml`, `kask/scripts/build/install.sh`, `script/bundle-linux`, `script/bundle-mac`, `script/bundle-windows.ps1`, `script/snap-build`, `nix/build.nix`, `nix/modules/packages.nix`, `flake.nix` | `APP_NAME`→`Zed-Kask`, `app_identifier`→`Zed-Kask-Editor`, `app_id`→`dev.zed-kask.Zed-Kask`, `display_name`→`Zed-Kask`, port offset +500, binary `zed-kask`, remote dirs `.zed-kask_server` / `.zed-kask_wsl_server`, bundle IDs `dev.zed-kask.*`, URL scheme `zed-kask://`. **Flatpak runtime code removed (2026-08-27):** upstream's CLI `mod flatpak` (sandbox escape via `flatpak-spawn --host`, hard-coding upstream Zed's `FLATPAK_ID=dev.zed.Zed` and binary layout) and the `FLATPAK_XDG_{CONFIG,DATA,STATE,CACHE}_HOME` overrides in `paths.rs` are deleted — zed-kask is not distributed as a flatpak, and honoring upstream Zed's sandbox env vars would launch upstream Zed's editor / point zed-kask at upstream Zed's data dirs when the CLI runs inside upstream Zed's sandbox. Pinned by `check-zed-isolation.sh` (`mod flatpak|flatpak-spawn|FLATPAK_ID` in `crates/cli/src/main.rs`, `FLATPAK_XDG_` in `crates/paths/src/paths.rs`). **Hard Zed-isolation invariant:** zed-kask is installed and updated only through `kask/scripts/build/`; it may write only `zed-kask`, `hkask-mcp-*`, zed-kask config/data, and zed-kask icons. Upstream Zed's updater is not initialized or imported by the zed binary. **Download-only updater removed (2026-10-02, operator austere-code ruling):** the `install-binary.sh`/`update-zed-kask.sh` pair, the updater bundle `install_updater_bundle` installed into `share/zed-kask/install`, and the `Update Zed-Kask` menu action (D16) are deleted — no releases are published, so every download path 404'd (`releases/latest` verified twice); the menu item launched a script that could never succeed. Updates are a source rebuild (`script/install.sh`; `--fast` for quick rebuilds). The isolation gate's archive-install suite died with the path; the shared `install-common.sh` safety functions (`publish_binaries` — staged, per-file-atomic publication, which replaced the former `prepare_install_dir`+`install_binary` delete-first pair so a failed install can no longer remove a working installation — plus `assert_kask_binary_destination`, `assert_not_zed_owned_path`) remain pinned. The `task` crate dependency left orphaned in `crates/zed/Cargo.toml` is removed in the same change. The old `UpdateVersion` entity and production-reachable `SimulateUpdateAvailable` action were removed: they could display fake download/install progress and a `Restart to update Zed` menu item despite there being no in-app updater. `check-zed-isolation.sh` pins their absence. Legacy Zed bundlers for Linux, macOS, Windows, and Snap are fail-closed; upstream release workflows, the Zed desktop template, and Flatpak/Snap resources are deleted; macOS `[package.metadata.bundle-*]` sections (including `osx_url_schemes = ["zed"]`) are removed — zed-kask is Linux-only and does not bundle for macOS. The nix derivation (`nix/build.nix`) is build-only (consumed by the devshell for dev tooling); the installPhase produces `zed-kask` (not `zed`), installs no icons or `.desktop` files, and `nix/modules/packages.nix` exports no installable packages. `flake.nix` carries zed-kask identity (description, no `zed.cachix.org`). Installer destinations are canonicalized and rejected if they resolve inside Zed's app, data, config, command, or launcher paths. Enforced dynamically at the installer mutation boundary and by `kask/scripts/build/check-zed-isolation.sh` (CI: the `Kask invariants` workflow, `.github/workflows/kask-invariants.yml`). Residual: the script's pattern checks are grep-based — they pin the exact upstream identifiers (`FLATPAK_XDG_`, `mod flatpak`, workflow and crate names), so a reintroduction under a renamed identifier would slip past them; the crate-absence and fail-closed-bundler checks are structural and rename-proof. **Workstation isolation (added 2026-09-01, from the live incident):** an upstream Flatpak Zed window opened on this workspace ran its rust-analyzer at 5.7 GB RSS / 263% CPU for 5 hours (`cargo check --workspace` over ~450 crates) — the project-level `lsp` disable alone did not stop it. The repo's `.zed/settings.json` now disables rust-analyzer in BOTH forms (the `lsp` section AND the Rust `language_servers: ["!rust-analyzer"]` exclusion — older Zed builds honor only one), pinned by `check-zed-isolation.sh`. **Operator rules:** develop zed-kask in zed-kask (RA is intentionally disabled in-project — the workspace is too large for live indexing); do not open this repo in upstream Zed; never grant upstream Zed's agent write access to zed-kask's data/config dirs — a folder is a folder, and no install-side isolation can stop an editor you point at the repo from indexing it. |
| D8  | Bridge + adapters                                                                       | `kask/crates/kask_bridge/` (incl. `src/inference_socket.rs`, `src/condenser_bridge.rs`) + `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` (`ThreadCondenser`) + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` (deterministic pre-shrink execution, segmented dispatch, PromptTooLarge rescue) + `crates/agent/src/kask_compaction.rs` (deterministic pre-shrink + budget-aware segment planning) + `crates/language_model/src/registry.rs` (`try_read_global` — non-panicking registry read for the rescue's compaction-model resolution) + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (condenser wiring) + `crates/agent_settings/src/prompts/compaction_prompt.txt` (structured brevity and merge rules)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | **Budget-aware segmented summarization (2026-09-28; supersedes the 2026-09-11 two-half design `494e61550e`/`b667569505` after its documented no-token-fit limitation failed in production):** the live incident — a thread at ~3.2M tokens of history vs OpenRouter's 1,310,720-token endpoint limit — had both the turn and the manual-compaction rescue rejected with the same 400: `plan_halves` byte-balanced a fixed two-way split with no budget notion, so a history dominated by one exchange larger than the whole input budget produced a ~3.19M-token summarization request. `plan_compaction` (`crates/agent/src/kask_compaction.rs` — pure request algebra, no `Thread`/`App`) now plans against the compaction model's input capacity (`compaction_input_capacity` over `max_input_tokens`/`max_total_tokens`/`max_output_tokens`), translated to a per-thread **calibrated** byte budget — the current history's model-visible bytes over the last completed request's reported input tokens (cache reads included), trusted only within [1.0, 4.0] bytes/token (outside that window the denominator is stale — e.g. a thread rescued after growing past its window — and the measured ~2.0 bytes/token fallback applies; live zed-kask tool output measured 2.0, D8 precompression validation) × 0.85 safety factor, minus the measured per-request prefix/instruction overhead. Calibration makes the ratio cancel out of the fit decision: a history segments exactly when its token count exceeds ~85% of capacity, so byte-heavy prose threads are not over-segmented and token-dense threads are not planned over the window — the two failure modes of the fixed 3-bytes/token constant in the first cut (2026-09-29 operator report: common threads left the two-half path and over-segmented; for content denser than 2.55 bytes/token the constant also planned requests that would still be rejected). Fitting splittable histories keep the ratified two-half split (existing pins unchanged); over-budget histories pack into **balanced** N chronological segments at safe boundaries (a tool call is never separated from its result) — with `n = ceil(remaining/budget)` segments left, a segment closes at the first boundary past `remaining/n` bytes, never past budget, so no single request is near-window when boundaries allow a finer split — summarized concurrently in batches of ≤ 8 (`MAX_CONCURRENT_SEGMENT_SUMMARIES`) and merged chronologically through one merge request (`merge_request` generalized to N summaries); an indivisible history that exceeds the budget is head+tail elided on the summarizer's request copy only — an honest in-band marker names the elided byte count, and the stored thread history is never modified. A capacity below `MIN_COMPACTION_CONTEXT_WINDOW` is not a usable budget: the legacy two-half split applies unchanged there (bytes balance, no fit guarantee). `stream_compaction` in `thread.rs` keeps the streaming, per-call usage accounting, cancellation, and insertion. No recursion, retry queue, new settings or persistence format. Per-call high-water usage accounting prevents concurrent streams from being conflated. Truncation drains final usage but rejects the summary; empty/failed/cancelled phases never commit a marker. The native `crates/agent_settings/src/prompts/compaction_prompt.txt` retains its structure and adds restrained brevity, uncertainty/negation preservation, and later-correction rules. Pins: `test_two_half_compaction_runs_concurrently_then_merges_in_order`, `test_two_half_compaction_failure_or_cancellation_saves_nothing` (both in `thread.rs` — they exercise `Thread::compact`, the real call path, and pin the preserved fitting-history behavior), `test_compaction_pre_shrinks_oversized_tool_results` + `test_segmented_compaction_over_budget_history` + `test_oversized_indivisible_history_compacts_via_elision` (thread.rs — the pre-shrink, segmentation, and last-resort rescue paths), the `kask_compaction.rs` planner suite (`pre_shrink_windows_oversized_results_and_keeps_small_ones`, `pre_shrink_collapses_adjacent_repeats_with_counts`, `pre_shrink_has_no_exemptions_on_the_summarizer_copy`, `calibration_decides_segmentation_in_tokens_not_bytes`, `below_floor_capacity_keeps_legacy_two_half_plan`, `replay_only_raw_output_does_not_distort_planning`, `two_half_plan_balances_bytes_and_keeps_indivisible_history_single_pass`, `sub_cap_over_budget_history_plans_budget_fitting_segments`, `oversized_tool_result_is_windowed_not_split`, `oversized_indivisible_prose_elides_for_summarizer_only`, `balanced_packing_keeps_the_largest_segment_small`, `merge_request_lists_summaries_in_chronological_order`), and `compaction_template_preserves_meaning_while_requesting_brevity`. **Previous-summary refinement deleted (2026-09-11, `b667569505`; operator ruling — no backward-compatibility requirement, and the block introduced in `d54fcf4f8f` was never in the functional contract nor seam-recorded):** `build_compaction_request` no longer feeds a prior summary back; repeated compactions regenerate from the full history, and `test_compaction_refines_prior_summary` was deleted with the block. **Manual compaction precompression (`46f9c476a2`) — superseded 2026-09-29 by the in-planner deterministic pre-shrink (operator direction: the deterministic pre-screening "isn't really doing anything"; confirmed by the incident numbers — the manual compaction request carried 3,187,749 of the turn's 3,247,298 text-input tokens, a 1.8% reduction, because the hook exempted protected tools, JSON, failed results, and the latest exchange — exactly the content a coding thread is made of — and automatic compaction skipped it entirely; its 9x synthetic shrink was measured on repeated output real threads don't have):** `ThreadCondenser::precompress_history` is deleted (trait method, `BridgeThreadCondenser` impl, `condenser_bridge.rs`'s `collapse_repeated_lines`, and the pins `history_precompression_preserves_protected_content_and_routes_algorithms` + `test_manual_compact_precompression_failure_leaves_history_unchanged`); the trait keeps `compress_tool_result` for ingestion only (`run_tool` + `NO_COMPRESS_TOOLS` unchanged — ingestion policy, no longer compaction policy). `kask_compaction::pre_shrink` now owns the deterministic stage for BOTH manual and automatic compaction, on the summarizer's request copy only: pass 1 run-length collapse (3+ identical adjacent non-blank lines → one + exact count, marker `[preceding line repeated N more times]`, moved from the bridge with identical semantics), pass 2 head+tail windowing of any tool-result text over `clamp(budget/32, 8 KiB, 64 KiB)` (marker `[… compaction elided N bytes to fit the context budget; the full text remains in the thread history …]` — the same elision primitive as the last-resort path) — NO exemptions (tool name, JSON, error state, position): windowing cannot corrupt structure the way the condenser's mid-content `...` elision can, and the stored history keeps the full text. `stream_compaction` logs the shrink stats (`Compaction deterministic pre-shrink: A -> B bytes (P% reduction; N results windowed, M repeat runs collapsed)`) so a no-op stage is operator-visible — the observability the previous design lacked. `test_manual_compact_forces_summary` now proves compaction no longer consults the condenser (the request carries the original text with a marker condenser wired). Full specification with diagrams and reference models (RLE, head/tail windowing, extractive-before-abstractive staging, map-reduce hierarchical summarization, LPT-balanced partitioning, empirical ratio calibration): `kask/docs/architecture/compaction-pipeline-spec.md`. **PromptTooLarge rescue (2026-09-29):** a turn request rejected because input + output reserve exceeds the model's context is permanent for that request but recoverable for the thread — one forced compaction changes the request. The threshold check (`compaction_message_target_ix`) keys off the last completed request's usage, so a single round whose growth exceeds the remaining headroom sails past it and dies as a permanent rejection (live incident: a request at 917,786 input tokens against a 917,504-token effective ceiling — the 1,048,576-token window minus the 131,072-token output reserve — over by 282 tokens; the provider counts input + max_tokens against the window). `run_turn_internal`'s error branch now routes `ProviderRejection { category: PromptTooLarge }` through `perform_prompt_too_large_rescue` — one forced compaction per turn (bounded by a per-turn flag), using the manual flow's unconditional target with the threshold path's trailing-user handling (the turn's not-yet-answered prompt stays verbatim after the summary), then `continue` so the loop rebuilds the request from the compacted history. Gated on auto-compaction being enabled (operator-disabled auto-compact keeps manual Compact as the recovery); the fall-through paths (disabled, nothing to summarize, rescue compaction failed) preserve the old fatal behavior including `mark_token_limit_exceeded` via `handle_completion_error` — the rescue itself deliberately does NOT mark, because the synthesized usage would re-fire the threshold check at the top of the next loop iteration (a second compaction over the just-compacted window, observed in test development) and a successful rescue refreshes the indicator with the retry's real usage.
