The rescue resolves the compaction model through `Thread::compaction_model`, which now reads the registry via `LanguageModelRegistry::try_read_global` (added for this seam; `read_global` panics in harnesses that never wire the registry global) and falls back to the thread's own model — the documented fallback, now non-panicking for every compaction path; the insertion target is the shared `trailing_user_aware_insertion_ix` (one rule, one home — the threshold path and the rescue cannot drift apart). The rejection's reported input-token count is the rescue compaction's calibration denominator (`stream_compaction`'s `reported_input_tokens` parameter): at rescue time the last completed request's usage predates the growth that caused the rejection, and the 2.0 bytes/token fallback can over-budget token-dense content — planning a summarization request that is itself rejected, killing the turn at the moment recovery matters most. Transient failures of the rescue compaction retry INSIDE the rescue, bounded by the shared `RetryStrategy` machinery (the threshold path retries its compaction through the turn loop; the rescue cannot — the loop-top check will not re-fire, and routing through the loop would re-send the known-oversized request on every cycle); permanent errors fail the rescue immediately, and the whole episode — initial attempt plus transient retries — is the turn's one rescue. Pins: `test_prompt_too_large_rescue_compacts_and_retries`, `test_prompt_too_large_rescue_is_bound_to_once_per_turn`, `test_prompt_too_large_rescue_respects_disabled_auto_compact`, `test_prompt_too_large_rescue_retries_transient_compaction_failure`, `test_prompt_too_large_marking_self_heals_next_turn` (the dead-turn marking makes the next turn's threshold check compact first — emergent, correct, now pinned) (thread.rs); `reported_token_count_keeps_dense_history_segments_within_capacity` (kask_compaction.rs — the calibration datum is load-bearing on dense content); `test_prompt_too_large_marks_token_usage_exceeded` + `test_prompt_too_large_uses_reported_token_count` (tests/mod.rs, updated to pin the marking contract on the fall-through path with auto-compact disabled; the auto-compact settings helper is shared with thread.rs's test module — one helper, one home). **Upstream filing not pursued (operator policy 2026-09-30: no suggestions or pushes to upstream zed)** — the discrete-threshold overshoot the rescue fixes exists in upstream Zed's compaction code too, but the fix stays fork-only by deliberate policy; the upstream-rebase per-seam verdict for this behavior is RETAIN, and this record is its permanent home. **THE bidirectional seam** — implements all ports over zed-kask facilities. `InferencePort` over `LanguageModel`, `LanguageModelEmbeddingPort` (resolves credentials from `INFERENCE_PROVIDERS` + env var, no `LanguageModelRegistry` lookup), `resolve_model_names` (generic provider-prefixed model-name resolution), `BridgeMemoryPort`, `BridgeContextInjector`, `BridgeCuratorContextInjector` (curator-scoped recall via `RealMemoryPort::recall_context_curator`), `BridgeThreadCondenser`, `BridgeMetacognitionProvider`, `InferenceIpcServer`, `KaskSettings`. Channel pattern solves GPUI/tokio `Send`+`Sync` boundary. `LanguageModelInferencePort` holds **two** mpsc channels: `tx` (non-streaming `InferenceRequest` → `InferenceResult`) and `stream_tx` (streaming `StreamInferenceRequest` → `InferenceStreamChunk` stream). The GPUI-side receiver task polls both via `tokio::select!`. The shared `collect_completion` collects non-streaming events into a single `InferenceResult` via `StreamAccumulator`; its streaming mode forwards `Text`/`Thinking` events immediately as `InferenceStreamChunk`s (live thinking-trace for skill execution) and accumulates only metadata (`ToolUse`, `Stop`, `UsageUpdate`) into a final chunk. `StreamAccumulator` is the shared event-processing struct used by both paths. The `generate_stream` trait override uses `futures_util::stream::unfold` to convert the tokio mpsc receiver into a futures stream (no `tokio-stream` dependency). `generate_stream` does **not** support `model_override` (hardcoded to `None`) — the since-removed skill-cascade executor's `call_inference_stream` called `generate_stream` without an override; `generate_stream_with_model` (added 2026-08) threads `model_override` through `StreamInferenceRequest.model_override` to `collect_completion`, which calls `resolve_model` with the override — so streaming + override preserves the live trace (the prior claim that it fell back to non-streaming `generate_with_model` was stale). The inference IPC protocol carries one MCP-server-to-zed dispatch method: `tool_invoke` (governed `McpRuntime` via the injected `ToolPort`). The parent-held grant intersected with per-request `tool_allowlist` is the authority boundary for delegated dispatch (fail-closed before dispatch). A child cannot authorize itself by supplying its own list — pinned by `ipc_child_cannot_expand_parent_grant` and the D8/D9 launch tests above. The call meter is **fail-open on an unregistered agent**: it auto-registers at `DEFAULT_RUNAWAY_CALL_CEILING` and logs the wiring gap rather than refusing, because a missing seed is a wiring omission, not an authorization decision. The prior fail-closed cap silently broke delegated paths — `main.rs` seeded only the `swarm-panel` persona while this dispatch mints `kask-panel`, which derive different WebIDs, so every IPC tool call was refused with `EnergyBudgetExceeded` while this row claimed the seed made them "succeed while remaining bounded." The one pre-dispatch refusal is an exhausted per-tick ceiling (runaway-loop breaker; resets each regulation tick). **Inference IPC socket path management:** `kask_bridge::inference_socket` (a re-settable `Mutex<String>`) owns the socket path state, replacing a local `OnceLock<String>` in `main.rs` that could not be updated when the IPC server restarted (e.g. when the real model resolved after the no-op fallback). `main.rs` calls `kask_bridge::set_inference_socket_path` after `InferenceIpcServer::start` succeeds, and `kask_bridge::get_inference_socket_path` when building MCP server env maps via `build_mcp_server_env`. The `OnceLock`'s `set()` was write-once — a second IPC server start (model resolved after no-op fallback) silently dropped the new socket path, leaving MCP servers with a stale or empty `HKASK_INFERENCE_SOCKET` env var and all inference calls failing with "IPC bridge not configured." **Both MCP server paths re-synced on socket set:** after `set_inference_socket_path`, `main.rs` calls both `sync_kask_mcp_servers` (ContextServerStore path) AND `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` (governed McpRuntime path). Previously only the ContextServerStore path was re-synced, so governed McpRuntime servers kept their stale env (no socket) and all skill execution / panel inference calls failed even though the socket was live. **Model-resolved re-wire (2026-08-30):** the deferred task's inference-stack wiring is extracted into `wire_kask_inference_stack` (`main.rs`) and, when the registry has no default model at deferred-task time, retried by a `LanguageModelRegistry` event subscription that latches on success — previously the wiring was one-shot, so a model resolving after startup left the no-op IPC server in place for the whole session (sensed as `InferenceModelAvailable=0.0`, escalated as `model_unavailable`). `try_wire_edit_prediction_port`'s AtomicBool latch was fixed the same way (latch on success, not on attempt — a failed attempt previously left edit predictions unwired for the session). **Stable per-process socket + swappable backend (2026-10-02, P7h — the startup restart storm):** the socket path is now pid-keyed and stable for the process lifetime (`generate_socket_path` drops the per-instance nanosecond nonce), and the serving backend (inference port, embedding port, tool port) lives behind `IpcBackendSlot` — a `RwLock` slot created once at `InferenceIpcServer::start`, read per request by `handle_connection`, and swapped in place by `kask_bridge::swap_inference_ipc_ports`. The model-resolved re-wire (`wire_kask_inference_stack`) is swap-first: when the no-op server from the deferred task holds the socket, the re-wire replaces its ports and the socket path — and therefore every MCP child's `HKASK_INFERENCE_SOCKET` — never changes; fresh-start remains the fallback for the no-server case (the no-op start failed, or the model was already configured at deferred-task time). The no-op start also publishes `HKASK_INFERENCE_TIMEOUT_SECS` from the same setting the real wiring uses, so that env key is stable across the swap too. Root cause (measured 2026-10-02, P7h in the repair plan): the per-instance nonce made the no-op→real transition an env CHANGE — restart-by-design — so the first launch wave (launched against the no-op socket ~3 s after process start) was killed and respawned when the real model resolved ~25 s later: 30 restart events (portfolio 4× in 13 s, companies/corpus/curator 2× each), ~2.5 min to fleet stability. Pins: `generate_socket_path_is_stable_within_a_process`, `backend_slot_swap_replaces_serving_ports_in_place`, `swap_inference_ipc_ports_without_a_server_returns_false_and_keeps_the_socket_path` (kask_bridge), `kask_restart_baseline_is_recorded_at_initiation` (zed main.rs source pin). **Intended-model latch (2026-10-26, card fec4e7c7):** `wire_kask_inference_stack` returns a three-state `InferenceStackWiring` (`NotWired` / `WiredToConfigured` / `WiredToZedDefault`) instead of `bool`; the model-resolved re-wire subscription is armed for both `NotWired` and `WiredToZedDefault`, latches only on `WiredToConfigured`, re-reads the live kask settings per event (no deferred-task snapshot — mid-session retargets or clears of `kask.models.default_model` are honored while the stack is unwired or on the fallback), resolve-checks the configured model before any fallback re-wire (one registry lookup per event, no port churn), and swaps the serving ports in place when it resolves — the zed-default fallback no longer outlives a late-resolving provider credential (live-observed twice 2026-10-27: the fallback WARN followed by the re-wire within one second). |
| D9  | Settings + credentials                                                                  | `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/settings.rs` + `crates/settings_content/src/settings_content.rs` + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page.rs` + `crates/settings_ui/src/page_data.rs`                                                                                                                                                                  | `KaskSettings` struct + `"kask"` section in settings.json. **Curator interaction mode (2026-09-27, operator ruling):** `kask.curator.interaction_mode` (`CuratorInteractionMode` in `settings_content`, re-exported by `kask_bridge`; default `collaboration`) is the user-set level of the user–Curator dyad — Level 1 control, Level 2 collaboration, Level 3 learning collaboration; Level 4 is a future release with no variant. Edited by the Interaction Mode dropdown on the Settings > Kask > Curator page (`crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page/curator.rs`); consumed by D2. Pinned by `curator_interaction_mode_is_read_from_the_user_setting`. Semantics: `kask/docs/architecture/functional-interaction-spec.md` §9. **Companies investor required return setting (2026-09-14, operator-approved):** `kask.companies.investor_required_return` is the single production default for the investor equity hurdle. The settings content and Companies UI persist it; `mcp_env` always emits `HKASK_INVESTOR_REQUIRED_RETURN`; the companies-only config allowlist admits it; the server rejects missing, malformed, or out-of-range values instead of carrying a model fallback. Expectations uses it as the equity component of modified WACC (`E/(D+E) × target return + D/(D+E) × debt cost × (1−tax)`), and financial issuers use it directly. Reports expose the target, weights, and modified WACC. **Dedicated QA generator (2026-09-11, operator-approved):** `kask.models.qa_generation_model` is an empty-by-default provider-prefixed string, exposed under Settings → Kask → Models (`pages/kask_page/models.rs`) and saved by the generic input dispatcher (`kask_page.rs`). The host `Default`/`From<Content>` → `mcp_env` → corpus-only allowlist chain emits `HKASK_QA_GENERATION_MODEL`; env changes use the existing D3 restart observer. Corpus single/prepared-batch QA resolves explicit tool model > this setting, never the chat model. Unknown models fail through the registry, malformed/unconfigured choices fail before dispatch; no generator ID is defaulted. Pins: `qa_generation_control_has_a_save_dispatch_arm`, `qa_generation_model_persists_and_reloads` (settings_ui, real writer/reload on FakeFs), `qa_generation_setting_round_trips_and_reaches_only_its_consumer` (kask_bridge). The bridge-override and corpus-routing pins (`qa_generator_override_bypasses_incompatible_chat`, `qa_generator_routing_is_independent_of_chat`) were removed with the tuple-response and batch-API rework (`8abc324d70`, `3443af0ba4`); that routing is **not currently pinned**. These are offline checks, not catalog/live-provider verification. **QA verifier setting deleted; grounding gate replaces model-verifies-model (2026-09-18, operator-approved):** `kask.models.qa_verification_model`, `HKASK_QA_VERIFICATION_MODEL`, `resolve_qa_verification_model`, the Settings → Kask → Models control, the corpus-only allowlist entry, and their pins (`qa_verification_control_has_a_save_dispatch_arm`, `qa_verification_model_is_independent_and_provider_qualified`, `qa_verification_setting_round_trips_and_reaches_only_corpus`) are deleted. The 2026-09-17 verifier wiring enabled a second model to review the generator's output — circular self-authorization with no external evidence (the same flaw FEVER's claim-verification design and PROV-O's provenance model reject: verification must cite sources the verifier does not author). Its replacement is the re-executed `corpus-qa-grounding-v1` gate (2026-09-18, operator-approved; the earlier same-day fact-score report design never shipped and its staged hardening was discarded): `corpus_generate_qa_batch` produces unverified candidates only (protocol `prepared-qa-grounding-candidate-v1`, `grounding_status: pending_external_verification` on generated rows / `not_applicable_skip` on skips; no model reviews, corrects, or accepts during generation). The new server-owned tool `corpus_ground_generated_qa` writes a hash-bound bundle (`manifest.json` + `grounding-rows.jsonl`) deterministically with zero inference: each row carries `row_key` (prompt_id+qa_type+physical line), the candidate raw-line SHA-256, source identity, preserved candidate terms, server-recomputed `TermResolution`s through the published ladder, and per-claim records — evidence quotes byte-checked against uniquely identified canonical chunks, the answer byte-checked inside the row's own source-grounded evidence or recorded honestly as `model_inference`, the instruction premise recorded `not_applicable` with its unperformed check. No verified/authorized/confidence fields exist in the bundle. `corpus_ingest_qa` then re-executes everything before dedup/output/DB: re-hashes the bundle, checks row bijection, requires `has_current_canonical_terms()` sources, recomputes ontology resolutions, requires each artifact row to equal its re-execution, and admits a row when every `cited_substring` claim is strength 2 (`tool_verified`/`platform_derived`) and at least one such citation exists; a synthesized answer that is not byte-exact inside the row's own source-grounded evidence is admitted as a `model_inference` observation and persisted with `answer_provenance: model_inference` (model-mediated, never relabelled verified). **Policy reversal reconciled 2026-09-19 at operator direction:** the original V1 clause — "`model_inference` answers fail closed ... until an independent semantic oracle is specified" — is **superseded** by `ed9be534cc` ("Harden QA grounding gate to admit cited-substring claims") and `50628f8a01` ("Document corpus QA gate's model_inference admission policy"). Residual risk, recorded honestly: no semantic oracle adjudicates answer-vs-citation entailment, so an admitted `model_inference` answer is an observation, not authority; admission rests entirely on the byte-verified citation claims. Reverting to fail-closed remains the option if this is not the intended disposition. Manifest identity and canonical ontology persist into the QA JSONL and h_mems via `HMemOntology::with_candidate_term`. Pins: `ingest_requires_grounding_manifest_before_any_write`, `self_reported_verification_cannot_open_ingestion_gate`, `grounding_manifest_hash_mismatch_fails_closed`, `grounding_manifest_requires_bijective_row_coverage`, `grounding_gate_recomputes_ontology_resolutions`, `model_inference_answer_is_admitted_and_persisted_as_model_mediated`, `citation_not_byte_exact_fails_closed_despite_paraphrase_answer`, `exact_source_grounded_qa_round_trips_with_manifest`, `grounding_gate_runs_before_output_and_db_open` (ingest_tests), `tool_surface_is_exactly_26_registered_tools`. No model can promote its own output to verified; Stage 8 semantic audit and operator acceptance stay outside generation and ingestion. Historical pilot artifacts (v2–v8) stay immutable evidence and remain auditable by the mechanical jq's historical protocol branches. **Keychain locations (one key, one location; see D5):** active data-service keys are stored in zed's `CredentialsProvider` keychain under `kask://credentials/<key>`; inference-provider keys (OpenRouter, DeepInfra, RunPod, KiloCode) live at their provider `api_url` slots. `credential_urls_for_mcp()` now derives only the keys named by remaining child credential allowlists; native-only RunPod OCR and KiloCode stay out of child env/restarts. Retired HuggingFace/Nebius entries have no UI, recovery, read or injection path; any old keychain values remain dormant operator-owned data. There are NO `*_enabled` settings toggles — the key's presence in the keychain IS the toggle. The `KaskDataServiceSettings` struct and its `*_enabled` bool fields have been deleted (2026-08-24); `credential_urls_for_mcp()` emits the remaining registered data-service keys and eligible inference-provider keys; the retired RunPod S3 entries are gone, and KiloCode chat has no MCP-child key. The single internal key `hkask_db_passphrase` lives in the same `kask://credentials/` namespace. `resolve_credential` reads API keys from env vars only (no keychain fallback); `HKASK_DB_PASSPHRASE` resolves via the canonical 2-tier helper (ctx.credentials → env → `kask://credentials/hkask_db_passphrase`). There is no `HKASK_SWARM_MEMORY_PASSPHRASE` — one passphrase covers every SQLCipher DB. **`nudge_mcp_servers` restart trigger:** after `write_credential` / `delete_credential` writes a keychain URL to the keychain, it calls `nudge_mcp_servers(cx)` which calls `SettingsStore::notify_observers(cx)` — a direct `Effect::NotifyGlobalObservers` push via `global_mut` that bypasses the file-write path entirely. This fires the `SettingsStore` observer → `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` → `build_mcp_server_env` (re-reads keychain) → restart. The trigger predicate is `credential_url_feeds_mcp_servers`: it fires only for URLs in `credential_urls_for_mcp()` — credentials actually granted to remaining children. Native-only RunPod OCR refreshes its provider directly and does not restart MCP children. The startup mirror (`mirror_kask_credentials_to_providers` / `mirror_credential_to_provider`) is deleted (2026-08-31) — see D5 for the one-key-one-location consolidation. **`permission_denied` classification standard:** missing credentials must surface as `McpToolError::permission_denied` with the env var named. **Canonical `HKASK_DB_PASSPHRASE` resolution helper:** `hkask_mcp_server::server::resolve_db_passphrase(&ctx.credentials)` — 2-tier chain (ctx.credentials → `resolve_credential` which does env → `hkask-keystore` keychain `hkask-db-passphrase`). **First-run provisioning (one chain, 2026-08-30):** `hkask_keystore::keychain::provision_db_passphrase_string` owns the whole chain (env → keychain → first-run default `"allostery"`, with an empty/whitespace-only env value treated as unset — the guard the bridge's old inline chain carried, moved into the chain and pinned by `empty_env_passphrase_falls_through_to_keychain`); `kask_bridge::identity::{provision_agent, provision_db_passphrase}` are thin adapters over it (no inline passphrase logic remains in the bridge); called at governed MCP server launch (`kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/mcp_servers.rs`, inside `provision_default_passphrase`); `build_mcp_server_env` then mirrors the provisioned passphrase into zed's keychain via `write_credentials` so the `ctx.credentials` tier of the 2-tier chain resolves it. Pinned by `provision_stores_default_when_absent_and_is_stable_after` (live-keychain, snapshot/restore). **Startup-coordinated passphrase rotation (2026-09-18, operator-approved Option A):** the Security page schedules a change via `kask_bridge::schedule_db_passphrase_rotation` into the keychain pending slot (`hkask_db_passphrase_pending`, defined in `hkask-keystore/src/keychain_keys.rs`) plus a pending record (`maintenance/pending-db-rotation.json` under the data dir — confirmed rotate paths and the last application error only; the passphrase never touches disk). At editor startup, `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (immediately after the D8 inventory init) calls `kask_bridge::run_pending_db_passphrase_rotation()` before any database opens: every confirmed database is classified with `hkask_storage::verify_database_key` (old key → rotate; new key → already done, e.g. a prior run crashed between rotation and the keychain write; neither → surfaced manual-recovery error), the old-key set is rotated with rollback, and only after all databases are consistent is the new passphrase written to the main `hkask_db_passphrase` slot and the pending intent deleted — the keychain write is always last. Failures record their error in the pending record, keep the old passphrase everywhere, and surface on the Security page with a cancel action. The page's inventory JSON inputs live under an advanced sub-section with plain-language captions. Pins: `verify_database_key_accepts_the_matching_passphrase_and_rejects_a_wrong_one`, `verify_database_key_never_manufactures_a_missing_database` (hkask-storage); `apply_rotates_every_confirmed_database_and_preserves_rows`, `apply_completes_a_crashed_rotation_and_skips_already_rotated_databases`, `apply_rolls_back_to_the_old_passphrase_when_one_rotation_fails`, `apply_rejects_a_database_that_neither_key_opens`, `apply_reports_nothing_to_do_for_the_same_passphrase`, `pending_record_round_trips_through_disk` (kask_bridge `passphrase_rotation.rs`); `change_passphrase_control_schedules_through_the_bridge_and_is_gated` (settings_ui). | D14 | Streaming text reveal timer interval | `crates/acp_thread/src/acp_thread.rs` (`StreamingTextBuffer::TASK_UPDATE_MS`) | Upstream hardcodes `StreamingTextBuffer::TASK_UPDATE_MS = 16` (60fps). During active streaming, this fires a `Markdown::append` + `cx.notify()` on the foreground thread 60 times per second, saturating the render loop and starving the `BlinkManager`'s 500ms timer — the cursor flashes irregularly/rapidly. Zed-kask increases it to 50ms (20fps): still smooth for text reveal (the human eye can't perceive text appearing at >20fps) but 3x less render pressure. The former once-per-reveal `EntryUpdated` change was dropped in the 2026-09-24 sync: upstream #64667 replaced the pending-string buffer with a source cursor and emits per chunk. **Grid extension RETIRED (2026-10-02, `3f6f844703`):** the 2026-09-29/09-30 shared-grid alignment (REVEAL_GRID_EPOCH/reveal_grid_delay, then `gpui::frame_grid`) is deleted with the D84 redraw caps — the A/B measured the clustered build at 22-35 ms per draw (early-session; 54-115 ms by ~1 h) vs 5.9-8.3 ms reverted, so the coalescing layer was net-harmful; the reveal timer keeps the plain fixed 50ms interval. Pinned by `test_streaming_reveal_timer_interval_kask_contract` (the 50ms interval). **2026-10-27 minimization audit: KEEP (operator ruling)** — pure render preference at one-line cost, and the benefit was experienced live; retained deliberately. |

On macOS the platform overrides this title with the bundle name ("Zed-Kask" via D7); the rename only takes effect on the cross-platform title-bar `ApplicationMenu` (Linux/Windows, `crates/title_bar/src/application_menu.rs`). | D18 | Media + graph + kanban + scenarios block renderer (viz widgets in markdown) | `crates/markdown/src/markdown.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs` + `crates/hkask-viz-core/` + `crates/hkask-media-widget/` + `crates/hkask-media-benchmarks/` + `crates/hkask-graph-widget/` + `crates/hkask-kanban-widget/` + `crates/hkask-scenarios-widget/`                                                                        | New `media_block_renderer: Option<MediaBlockRendererFn>` field on `MarkdownElement` (an optional callback that intercepts fenced code blocks before the default code block renderer). Called only for the D18-admitted fenced languages (`media`, `graph`, `kanban`, `scenarios`, `swarm_delegate_results`) — returns `Some(div)` to render the matching media or viz widget, or `None` to fall through to default code block rendering. The callback type `MediaBlockRendererFn` and the `.media_block_renderer()` builder method are the only upstream additions. The widget implementations live in `crates/hkask-media-widget/`, `crates/hkask-graph-widget/`, `crates/hkask-kanban-widget/`, `crates/hkask-scenarios-widget/`, and `crates/hkask-swarm-widget/` (zed-kask-side — they render GPUI elements, so they depend on `gpui`/`theme` and live outside `kask/` to preserve the §13.1 invariant that hKask crates never depend on zed-kask crates). Wired in `render_agent_markdown` (`conversation_view.rs`) via `.media_block_renderer(hkask_viz_core::block_renderer())` — the `hkask-viz-core` registry composes all widget renderers into one callback so the upstream D18 field/builder/dispatch stay unchanged. The media widget's timestamp pacing, capacity-one latest-frame mailbox, lossless lifecycle channel, stable-Asset-keyed bounded runtime registry, render-heartbeat suspension of offscreen loading/playback, visible-surface reactivation without autoplay, and 1/8/32 production-shaped GPUI benchmark live entirely behind the unchanged D18 callback. **Transcript-linked audio recovery (2026-10-28):** workspace rodio enables MP3, FLAC, Vorbis, and MP4/AAC in addition to WAV; the shared byte decoder supplies its known length and seekability so MP4 metadata located after the media data remains reachable, and a contained Symphonia shim derives mono/stereo channels from OpusHead before delegating to libopus. Load/decode failures render as a compact dismissible status rather than a full-height transcript dead end, and the transcript workspace keeps Back above a bounded 140px player. The workspace presents human recording names, transcript text before annotation inventories, excerpt-based highlight/cut rows, and plain-language reel actions rather than transcript/annotation UUIDs, full paths, raw layer lists, or source-word indices; technical lineage stays in Detail. **Follow-along viewport tracking (2026-10-28, media-refactor plan R1):** the workspace renders as ONE virtualized `gpui::list` (header rows, paragraph blocks, and the annotation sections as tail items; the pending label editor stays pinned above), so long recordings render only the visible blocks; the 4 Hz follow task keeps the marked paragraph block in view through an item-anchored `ListState::scroll_to` (a pixel-based reveal computes against unmeasured virtualized heights and scrolls nowhere), a manual scroll suspends the tracking (any tick-visible offset change not from the follow's own reveal), and a Follow chip in the workspace header re-enables it and reveals the current mark — the reference model's capability (1), "the transcript is the index", now holds in experience rather than only in data (the mark-only follow of the S3c era is gone). Pinned by `follow_along_scrolls_the_marked_block_into_view` (RED-verified against a reveal-disabled build), `manual_scroll_suspends_follow_and_the_toggle_re_enables`, and `block_index_for_word_matches_block_boundaries`; `transcript_audio_regression_formats_decode` (each fixture produces decoded samples), `human_recording_labels_avoid_internal_identifiers`, and `transcript_media_failure_keeps_navigation_and_curation_reachable` — re-pinned to the virtualized reachability contract: Back renders at the list top without scrolling even on the tightest pane, and the mode chips plus transcript text render when the list is scrolled to them (the pre-virtualization "painted anywhere in the window" assertions described a world that painted everything, visible or not). Media entities use a dedicated bounded 32-entry strong registry because D18 elements are ephemeral across parent renders; render heartbeats suspend active work offscreen. Non-media viz entities keep the separate bounded 32-entry LRU. `crates/hkask-media-benchmarks/` is an isolated workspace package whose feature graph excludes `test-support`. The kanban/scenarios widgets are inline chat-stream renderers for their respective fenced blocks. **Portfolio widget removed 2026-10-15** with the portfolio MCP server: `crates/hkask-portfolio-widget`, the `portfolio` D18 fence language, and the viz-core factory arm are deleted in the same change. **Spreadsheet widget removed 2026-10-15** with the spreadsheet MCP server (Tranche S): `crates/hkask-spreadsheet-widget`, the `spreadsheet` D18 fence language, and the viz-core factory arm are deleted in the same change — ```spreadsheet now falls through to default code block rendering. **rJoule/spend-log removal (2026-09-08, operator budget-deprecation ruling; tombstone retired 2026-10-17):** budgets and rJoules are deprecated project-wide; timeouts and the per-tick runaway call ceiling are the live enforcement mechanisms. The kanban widget's `TaskBody.spend_log` field and `SpendEntryBody` type were deleted, together with spend-log rendering in `view.rs`, the `spend_log: Vec::new()` panel construction, and the kata-kanban set/refill/display surface after its unwired consumption path (`task_consume_rjoules`, `SpendEntry::rjoule_spend`) had already died. This supersedes the gas→rJoule lineage (`1f5fd478ed`, `7839d2a0ef`, `0cdde8942a`, `2a43a24954`, `2268514132` "Remove gas budget tracking, keep rJoule only"). The field was serde-optional and `TaskBody` does not deny unknown fields, so older persisted blocks are expected to remain parseable; no retained test directly supplies a legacy `spend_log` key, so this compatibility is structural inference rather than pinned evidence. The dedicated `rjoule_removal_pin.rs` source-text sweep was deleted on 2026-10-17 under the test-relevance protocol: it had no current expectation boundary or live disturbance channel and created a separate always-green integration-test binary. This row is the durable non-executing decision record; any future budget capability requires a new operator ruling and a current expectation contract. The remaining widget shape is covered by `selects_event_tree_body` / `falls_through_non_graph_bodies`, layout tests, and `parses_full_task_detail_fields` / `full_detail_fields_default_empty_when_absent`. **Posterior engine promotion (2026-10-06, scenario-server redesign PR-08):** `hkask-graph-widget/src/propagate.rs` is now a thin adapter over `hkask_forecast::posterior` — the same engine the scenarios server's `scenario_recompute_posteriors` tool exposes; the widget-local engine (forward marginalizer, polytree union-find, fixpoint posterior loop, conditional-for-parent) is deleted. The promotion also corrects a real divergence: the widget's former multi-group combination was a PRODUCT of per-group marginals, while `scenario_quantify`'s documented, property-tested rule is noisy-OR (`hkask_forecast::combine_independent_channels`) — on multi-group nodes the widget's re-propagation silently disagreed with the server's own emitted marginal. The updated `multi_dep_combines_by_independence` pins the unified noisy-OR value (0.725 for the fixture). **Posterior correctness repair (2026-10-21):** the shared `hkask_forecast::posterior` repeated-belief fixpoint and `conditional_for_parent` are replaced by edge-excluding factor messages. True/false evidence is applied once; fractional overrides retain fixed-marginal semantics or return a typed failure. The graph adapter forwards the same fallible result; the existing header status exposes failures and numeric labels become unavailable rather than stale or zero. What-if marginal products are labeled independence estimates, not posterior joints. Numeric library/independent-enumeration pins, `scenario_recompute_posteriors_matches_finite_bayes`, and `impossible_evidence_clears_numbers_and_reset_recovers` cover the shared/public seams; no upstream renderer dispatch changed. **Scenario evidence display cutover (2026-10-20):** `hkask-scenarios-widget` consumes `CalibrationSummary.evidence_status` instead of raw-loss interpretation labels and keeps absent values visible; the signed diagnostic is now `signed_probability_error` with its sample_count/qualifying_bins/bin_min_count scope, never an overconfidence measurement. Guidance names prospective `scenario_declare` before matching `scenario_score`; legacy observations stay unscored. This changes scenario block/view copy only, not renderer dispatch or other widgets. |

Three edits: (1) `OpenRouterLanguageModel` gains `api_url()`/`api_key()` trait overrides (returning the OpenRouter base URL + API key from provider state) — the trait default returned `None`, forcing callers to resolve credentials from env vars instead of the registry; the kask embedding port already uses this pattern. (2) `open_ai_compatible.rs` gains a process-global `Mutex<Option<Arc<dyn KaskCompletionPort>>>` hook (`set_kask_completion_port`); when wired, `send_custom_server_request` delegates to the port instead of making the HTTP call. (3) `kask_bridge::BridgeEditPredictionPort` implements `KaskCompletionPort` — at construction it resolves `DEFAULT_FALLBACK_MODEL` from the registry via `resolve_model_names`, extracts `api_url()`/`api_key()`, strips the provider prefix, and makes raw `/completions` POSTs via `HttpClient` (same pattern as `LanguageModelEmbeddingPort` but `/completions` instead of `/embeddings`). Wired in `main.rs` from the model-dependent task (fires when the registry resolves a model). `default.json` changes `edit_predictions.provider` from `"zed"` to `"open_ai_compatible_api"` with `prompt_format: "glm"` and `model: "z-ai/glm-5.2"` — the `Glm` FIM format (`fim.rs:189`) formats as bare `{prefix}`, and the `edit_prediction_provider_config_for_settings` router sends `OpenAiCompatibleApi` + non-Zeta format to the `Fim` model path, which calls `send_custom_server_request` (intercepted by the kask port). The `api_url`/`model` in settings are fallbacks for the pre-wiring window. When the port is `None` (before the model resolves), `send_custom_server_request` falls through to the existing HTTP path (upstream behavior). Pinned by `test_kask_completion_port_intercepts_send_custom_server_request` in `open_ai_compatible.rs`. **Test provider override:** `default.json`'s `provider: "open_ai_compatible_api"` changes what `test_settings()` resolves, breaking 21 `edit_prediction_tests` — the test `FakeHttpClient` handlers only serve the Zeta cloud endpoints (`/predict_edits/v3`, `/predict_edits/v4`), but with `OpenAiCompatibleApi` the request routes through `send_custom_server_request` to an unhandled URL, causing deadlocks or 404s. `override_edit_prediction_provider_to_zed` in `edit_prediction_tests.rs` resets the provider to `Zed` in `init_test` + `init_test_with_fake_client_and_legacy_data_collection`, restoring the upstream test path. Tests that need `OpenAiCompatibleApi` (`make_sweep_prompt_test_ep_store`, `test_unauthenticated_without_custom_url_blocks_prediction_impl`) already override the provider explicitly. **2026-10-12 merge:** upstream moved credential resolution onto the `LanguageModelClient` trait — `api_url()`/`api_key()` now live on the trait with the OpenRouter and OpenAI-compatible overrides there, anthropic keeps `api_key` only (matching the fork), and `crates/language_models/src/provider/open_ai_compatible.rs` joins this seam's file list. |
| D25 | Downstream distinction between MaxTokens truncation and cancellation | `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` | Originally implemented in the OpenAI and OpenRouter event mappers because their catch-all mapped output truncation to `EndTurn`. The 2026-09-10 merge adopts upstream's shared `ChatCompletionEventMapper`, whose explicit length arm already preserves `StopReason::MaxTokens`; the old provider mappers are removed, not kept as unused compatibility code. D36 must not drain length-truncated tool input as complete. The shared-core length mapping and `stream_maps_length_finish_reason_to_max_tokens_stop` test are upstream-provided, not a surviving D25 divergence; the fork's `length_with_pending_tool_calls_remains_truncated` pin protects D36's pending-tool drain. The downstream `last_completion_truncated` flag on `KaskThreadState` remains set on MaxTokens, read when flushing pending tool input, and reset per completion; its existing pin is `test_last_completion_truncated_distinguishes_max_tokens_from_cancel`. **Sibling-failure interruption vs user cancellation (2026-10-19, card bd7dbf68):** a parallel tool batch whose streaming tool errors while input is still streaming breaks the event loop (`is_error && is_still_streaming`), abandoning tool calls the model was still emitting — never-started siblings in the pending message. Those flushed as `TOOL_CANCELED_MESSAGE` (a false user-cancellation attribution; live incidents 2026-10-14/15: `terminal` canceled while the operator canceled nothing, and edit_file + grep + curator_semantic_search batches false-halted). `KaskThreadState` gains `interrupted_by_tool_failure` (set at the break site, reset per completion alongside `last_completion_truncated`), and `flush_pending_message` reads it to stamp never-started calls with `TOOL_INTERRUPTED_BY_SIBLING_FAILURE_MESSAGE` — naming the real cause and telling the model to re-issue. The turn does NOT end on the interruption (drain-and-continue): the failing result and the interruption message are delivered in a continuation request, so the false halt (a clean `EndTurn` with no operator action) is replaced by a real continuation the model can act on; the operator-visible label is a `log::warn!` at the break site (the D43 `max_tokens_turn_end_warning` precedent — a silent stop reads as a halt). Pinned by `test_sibling_failure_interrupts_stream_reports_interruption_not_user_cancel` (red on the pre-fix tree: the flag-set reverted reproduces the false user-cancellation message). |
| D26 | Tool-use warnings in system prompt template | `crates/agent/src/templates/system_prompt.hbs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/context_injector.rs` | The upstream Zed agent harness prompt carries general tool-use guidance ("Do not waste tokens by re-reading files...", "send a brief preamble...", a 3-strikes loop rule), and the built-in tools (`read_file`, `edit_file`, `terminal`) are upstream code with known failure modes (`read_file` returning "tool input was not fully received", `edit_file` `old_text` mismatch loops, opaque `terminal` commands). The kask-specific failure-mode warnings (`read_file` re-read-after-success and "tool input was not fully received" fallback to `terminal`; `edit_file` surgical edits and one-retry-on-mismatch; `terminal` preamble requirement and `timeout_ms`) are rendered as `## Tool Use` bullets in `system_prompt.hbs` (a separate section until the 2026-09-26 fold). **Refactored 2026-08-25:** previously delivered via `BridgeContextInjector::inject_static_context` (a runtime injection returning `Some(TOOL_WARNING_PROMPT)`); now baked into the template directly. The `inject_static_context` method, `Thread.static_context` field, and `static_context_loaded` flag were deleted. Thread-scoped memory recall was folded into the per-turn `inject_context` path so memory is fresh at decision time. Pinned by `test_system_prompt_contains_tool_failure_mode_warnings` in `templates.rs`. |
| D27 | Sandboxed terminal non-interactive shell                                                 | `crates/acp_thread/src/acp_thread.rs`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Upstream's `AcpThread::create_terminal` only calls `ShellBuilder::non_interactive()` when `HeadlessTerminal::is_enabled(cx)` is true (eval CLI only). In the GUI, `HeadlessTerminal` is not set, so the sandboxed shell is launched with `-i` (interactive mode). But the sandboxed command has stdin redirected to `/dev/null` (`redirect_stdin_to_dev_null`) and runs in a new session (`setsid()` in the `pre_exec` hook) with no controlling terminal — bwrap's user namespace severs the PTY's controlling-terminal assignment. When an interactive shell (`/bin/sh -i`) starts without a controlling terminal, it tries `tcsetpgrp()`, fails, prints `"Cannot set tty process group (No such process)"` to stderr, and exits with code 2 — even though the actual command ran fine. This made **every** sandboxed terminal command appear to fail (346 instances in a single agent thread), with agents seeing exit code 2 and the tty warning appended to every command output. Zed-kask adds `|| sandbox_wrap.is_some()` to the `non_interactive()` condition on the non-Windows path, matching the Windows sandboxed path (which already always uses `non_interactive()`). The unsandboxed GUI path (when `sandbox_wrap` is `None` and `headless` is false) still uses interactive mode, preserving existing behavior for non-sandboxed terminals. Pinned by `test_sandboxed_terminal_runs_non_interactively` in `acp_thread.rs`. |
| D28 | Standardized Artifact Storage                                                              | `crates/agent/src/db.rs` + `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/tool_permissions.rs` + `crates/agent_skills/agent_skills.rs` + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` + `kask/crates/hkask-types/src/agent_paths.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/settings.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/mcp_servers.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/memory/curator_stores.rs` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-kata-kanban/` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-research/` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-prediction-markets/` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-scenarios/` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-swarm/` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-curator/` | Establishes a single rooted data tree (`{kask_data_dir}/`) with four class subdirs (`agents/`, `mcp/`, `skills/`, `threads/`) for all persistent kask artifacts. **Ownership principle:** an artifact lives under the class subdir of the entity that owns it — agent-owned under `agents/{name}/`, server-owned under `mcp/{server_id}/`, user-owned under flat class dirs. **Archived threads** relocated from `paths::data_dir()/threads/` to `{kask_data_dir}/threads/threads.db` via `THREADS_DB_PATH_OVERRIDE` hook (`agent.rs`), pinned by `test_threads_db_override_hook_round_trips`. **2026-09-10 merge:** retain this storage hook and the error-to-UI `ThreadSaveFailed` event while adopting upstream release-driven session ownership and final-save draining; see the supporting thread-persistence note below. **Skills** relocated from `paths::data_dir()/agents/skills/` to `{kask_data_dir}/skills/` via `GLOBAL_SKILLS_DIR_OVERRIDE` hook (`agent_skills.rs`), pinned by `test_global_skills_dir_override_hook_round_trips` (agent_skills). **MCP server DBs** migrated to `mcp/{server_id}/{purpose}.db` (databases are infrastructure — the ONE artifact class that stays in the hidden internal data dir): kata-kanban (`mcp/kata-kanban/kanban.db`), research (`mcp/research/rss.db`), , prediction-markets (`mcp/prediction-markets/`), scenarios (`mcp/scenarios/`), swarm (`mcp/swarm/ledger.db` + `mcp/swarm/consent.db`). The training server's `mcp/training/` tree was retired with the server (2026-10-17, model training is out of scope for the co-researcher mandate); the dormant database files a prior installation left there were deleted at operator direction (2026-10-18) rather than auto-deleted, so no executable capability survives. The training-only rollout request/response-body capture, body-retention knob, and body-stripping API were removed 2026-10-18; event-store `model_request` rows retain operational telemetry only, and opening an older store strips the two legacy body keys idempotently. The portfolio server's `mcp/portfolio/` tree was removed with the server (2026-10-15, no position tracking). **Artifact files and outputs** of the content servers live in the VISIBLE artifacts dir (`~/Documents/zk-data/`) at `{server}-mcp/{artifact-type}/` via `mcp_artifacts_subdir` + `resolve_under_artifacts_dir`: corpus cache files (`corpus-mcp/cache/`), media generated assets (`media-mcp/generated/`). **Curator DB** renamed from `pod.db` to `curator.db` (the "pod" concept was deprecated); `agent_pod_db()` renamed to `agent_db()` producing `{agent_name}.db`. Stays under `agents/curator/` (agent artifact, not server artifact). **`HKASK_ARTIFACTS_DIR`** always emitted by `mcp_env()` and allowlisted for the servers that resolve artifact routes (corpus, media). **`dirs` crate** removed from the then-present portfolio/companies/swarm crates (replaced by `resolve_under_data_dir`; the portfolio crate has since been removed entirely). **Self-healing posture** (decision 2026-08-28): every artifact/DB write path `create_dir_all`s its parent, so user-deleted/moved directories self-heal on next write or server start; no read-only protection (0444-on-write and OS-snapshot options considered and deferred — see the §0 decision record in the storage doc). Pinned by the in-crate layout tests in `kask/crates/hkask-types/src/agent_paths.rs:236+` (`agent_db_follows_agents_class_layout`, `mcp_server_db_follows_mcp_class_layout`, `mcp_server_subdir_handles_empty_and_nested`, `all_layout_helpers_resolve_under_one_root`). See `kask/docs/architecture/standardized-artifact-storage.md` for the full layout spec. **Per-message thread storage (2026-10-02, the S1 fix — two-symptom research doc §2.1):** messages live one row each in a new `thread_messages` table (`thread_id`, `ix`, `data`); the `threads.data` blob carries only the metadata (a `DbThread` serialized with an empty messages list); `threads.format` (0 = legacy whole-thread blob, 1 = per-message rows, added by PRAGMA-checked ALTER) is the marker. Saves upsert all message rows in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (messages mutate in place after being saved — tool results fill in mid-turn — so append-only writes would leave stale rows) and trim rows at or beyond the new length; the dominant legacy save cost — zstd-compressing the whole thread on every save — is gone. Legacy whole-thread blobs are converged by the startup backfill (below); loads accept only format 1 and fail loudly on any other marker (a backfill-skipped unparseable blob surfaces at open, never silently). A gap in the row indexes fails loudly (saves are transactional, so a gap is corruption). `delete_thread`/`delete_threads` remove the message rows with their threads. Pinned by `test_per_message_round_trip`, `test_save_overwrites_message_rows_in_place`, `test_legacy_format_fails_loudly_on_load`, `test_message_row_gap_fails_loudly`, `test_delete_thread_removes_message_rows` (db.rs). **Legacy backfill (2026-10-05, Route A of the legacy-path deletion):** the lazy first-open migration only reaches threads someone opens — archived threads stay format 0 forever — so a startup backfill on the file-backed database (`backfill_legacy_threads`, spawned in `ThreadsDatabase::new` only when the file branch was taken; tests and ZED_STATELESS never spawn it) walks every format-0 row and converts it through the production parse path: deserialize the blob, serialize the metadata-only blob via the shared `serialize_metadata` (extracted from the save path in the same change), write the message rows via the production row writer, and set `format = 1` — one transaction per thread via the shared `in_transaction` scaffold (extracted from save/migrate in the same change), idempotent and resumable (the walk selects only format-0 rows, so an interrupted backfill resumes next start), throttled 1ms between units so thread opens interleave. Only `data_type`/`data`/`format` are written — the row's own summary, timestamps and folder paths stay untouched. An unparseable blob is logged and skipped (stays format 0, fails on open as before). Pinned by `test_backfill_converts_legacy_threads_without_opening`, `test_backfill_is_idempotent`, `test_backfill_skips_unparseable_blobs` (db.rs). **Legacy read path deleted (2026-10-05, Route A phase 2):** with the operator's database verified converged (1029/1029 threads format 1, 0 format-0 rows, read-only sqlite probe on the rebuilt binary), the lazy first-open migration and its legacy read branch were deleted — `load_thread` accepts only format 1 and bails loudly on any other marker; `migrate_legacy_thread` and `test_legacy_thread_migrates_on_first_load` were removed with the branch. The startup backfill stays as the permanent converter for any pre-D28 database an upgrade encounters. Pinned by `test_legacy_format_fails_loudly_on_load` (db.rs). **Commit-failure rollback + WAL journal mode (2026-10-19, the permanent-wedge fix):** `in_transaction`'s rollback previously ran only on the unit-failure arm; a failed COMMIT (the observed SQLITE_BUSY at 15:53:38, under a concurrent reader in legacy rollback-journal mode where COMMIT's EXCLUSIVE-lock upgrade fails immediately without invoking the busy handler) propagated without rolling back, leaving the transaction OPEN on the shared `Arc<Mutex<Connection>>` — every later save failed with `cannot start a transaction within a transaction` (370+ consecutive save failures, permanent until restart; the operator's message surfaced the data-loss risk). The rollback now runs on BOTH failure arms — unit failure and failed COMMIT — so a transient lock failure degrades to one lost save instead of a wedged database. The file-backed open path (`open_threads_file_connection`, extracted from `new`'s production branch in the same change so tests exercise the real helper over a temp file without the process-global override the test-build branch bypasses) sets `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL` (+ `synchronous = NORMAL`) on the threads database and reads the mode back fail-visibly (a non-WAL read-back warns — `Connection::open_file`'s in-memory fallback must not silently masquerade as persistence); WAL persists in the database header, so pre-existing databases convert once on first open. In-memory test/stateless connections keep the default journal mode. Pinned by `test_commit_failure_rolls_back_and_leaves_connection_reusable` (db.rs — a DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED FK violation forces the exact commit-failure arm; verified RED on the pre-fix tree reproducing `cannot start a transaction within a transaction`, GREEN after) and `test_file_backed_open_uses_wal_journal_mode` (db.rs — the production open helper over a temp file reads back `wal`). |
| D29 | RunPod serverless endpoint provider                                                      | `crates/language_models/src/provider/runpod.rs` + `crates/language_models/src/settings.rs` + `crates/language_models/src/language_models.rs` + `crates/language_models/src/provider.rs` + `crates/settings_content/src/language_model.rs` + `crates/settings_content/src/settings_content.rs` + `kask/crates/hkask-inference/src/model_constants.rs` + `kask/crates/hkask-inference/src/config.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/inference_providers.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/identity.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/kask_bridge.rs` + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-corpus/src/services/convert.rs` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-corpus/src/ocr/pipeline.rs` + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-corpus/src/ocr/config.rs` | Registers RunPod as a dedicated `LanguageModelProvider` (like DeepSeek, not via `openai_compatible`) so the IPC bridge can resolve `RunPod/kask-ocr` for the corpus OCR pipeline. Each endpoint carries its own API URL (`https://api.runpod.io/v2/{endpoint_id}/openai/v1`); endpoint discovery via the RunPod GraphQL API (`{api_url}/graphql`), not `/v1/models`. **Served-id resolution (2026-09-10):** after GraphQL discovery, each endpoint's served model id is resolved from its own OpenAI-compatible `/v1/models` listing (`resolve_served_model`) — vLLM matches the OpenAI `model` field case-sensitively against its served ids, and the RunPod vLLM template can serve a model under an id that differs from the `MODEL_NAME` env var (for example, configured `allenai/olmOCR-2-7B-1025` versus served `allenai/olmocr-2-7b-1025`). OCR failures surface as typed errors; no substitute backend is selected. OCR selection belongs to the Models page; `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page/corpus.rs` documents that the Corpus page carries no OCR routing or threshold controls. The listing's `max_model_len` replaces the 32,768 discovery-default context length when reported. Probe failure falls back to the `MODEL_NAME` env var with a `log::warn!` naming the endpoint (fail-visible). `DEFAULT_OCR_MODEL` changed from `RunPod/allenai/olmocr-2-7b-1025` to `RunPod/kask-ocr` (the endpoint name is the model id). `LanguageModel::name()` returns the endpoint name (the resolution key), NOT the display_name — the IPC server builds `ModelListEntry.name` as `{provider_id}/{model.name()}`, and a display_name suffix like `(OLMOCR-2)` would break `resolve_ocr_model`'s match. `supports_tools`/`supports_streaming_tools` are `false` (OLMOCR-2 is a specialized OCR model, not tool-capable). **Credential bridge (one key, one location, 2026-08-31):** RunPod is in `INFERENCE_PROVIDERS` with `credential_key: "runpod"`; its API key lives at exactly ONE keychain location — the provider `api_url` (`https://api.runpod.io`), the same slot the provider's `ApiKeyState` reads. Every consumer resolves it through `credential_url_for_key("runpod")`: the settings UI Data Services row writes the provider slot and drives the RunPod endpoint provider live via `set_api_key`, so `RunPod/*` models refresh without a restart. No MCP child receives `RUNPOD_API_KEY` after the training server's removal. The startup mirror (`mirror_kask_credentials_to_providers`) is **deleted** — it was one-directional and anti-clobber (kask→api_url only when api_url was empty), so no update path ever refreshed the kask slot, and a key rotated via Settings → AI → LLM Providers left MCP servers authenticating with the dead copy (the 2026-08-31 split-brain, which surfaced as the DeepInfra 401 on the same seam). The former RunPod-only mirror (`mirror_runpod_api_key` in `identity.rs`) and the former env-var mirror (`mirror_env_keys_to_keychain`) are also gone; there is no mirror at all anymore — inference-provider keys never live at `kask://credentials/<key>`. The `.env` file loading has been fully removed — the keychain is the single source of truth for API keys. **`resolve_ocr_model` case-insensitivity fix:** the corpus `resolve_ocr_model` gate used case-sensitive `==` against `ModelEntry.prefixed_name`/`model`, but the IPC server produces lowercase `runpod/...` while `DEFAULT_OCR_MODEL` is `RunPod/...`.
