| D31 | wasmtime `ResourceTable` leak in `WasmExtension` call sites | `crates/extension_host/src/wasm_host.rs` | Upstream's `WasmExtension` impl of `extension::Extension` pushes a resource (worktree, project, kv_store, delegate) into `store.data_mut().table` at 12 call sites (`language_server_command`, `language_server_initialization_options`, `language_server_workspace_configuration`, `language_server_initialization_options_schema`, `language_server_workspace_configuration_schema`, `language_server_additional_initialization_options`, `language_server_additional_workspace_configuration`, `run_slash_command`, `context_server_command`, `context_server_configuration`, `index_docs`, `get_dap_binary`) and **never calls `table.delete`** to reclaim the entry. The wasmtime `ResourceTable` (`wasmtime-48.0.1/src/runtime/component/resource_table.rs`) has a default `max_capacity` of 1,000,000 entries; once exhausted, every subsequent `push` returns `ResourceTableError::Full` ("resource table has no free keys"), which `lsp_store.rs:4111` logs at ERROR via `.log_err()`. The leak manifested as a flood of `getting additional workspace configuration for X from Y: resource table has no free keys` errors because `workspace_configuration_for_adapter` (`lsp_store.rs:4096-4116`) loops over every registered LSP adapter and calls `additional_workspace_configuration` on each — with 6 adapters (rust-analyzer, yaml-language-server, bash-language-server [built-in, no leak] + buf, protobuf-language-server, protols [wasm-backed `proto` extension, leaking]) the cross-product leaked 30 entries per cycle, hitting the cap in ~33,000 cycles and breaking all wasm-backed LSP adapters. The downstream symptoms were rust-analyzer `textDocument/diagnostic` and `textDocument/formatting` timeouts (120s cancellation) and a foreground hang on `app_will_quit`. **Fix:** each call site now captures `resource.rep()` (the `u32` handle) before passing `resource` into the extension call (since `Resource<T>` is not `Copy` — it contains `AtomicU32` state), then calls `store.data_mut().table.delete(Resource::<T>::new_own(rep))` after the call resolves, on both success and error paths. The `delete` runs before the first `?` that propagates the wasmtime error, so it runs regardless of whether the extension call succeeded. **Why not the bridge?** The leak is in the wasm host's `Extension` trait impl, which is upstream code that kask crates don't touch; the bridge is for kask-specific behavior, not for patching upstream trait impls. This is a genuine D-seam: an upstream bug fix that benefits all Zed users, not just kask. Should be reported upstream to `zed-industries/zed`. Pinned by `test_resource_table_push_delete_round_trip_reclaims_entries` (verifies the push-then-delete pattern reclaims entries on a locally constructed `ResourceTable`) and `test_every_resource_push_site_has_a_paired_delete` (source-structure pin, mermaid-precedent style: reads the file's own source, excludes the test module, and asserts every `table.push` site has a paired `delete` — 12 of each; fails if any single `delete` is removed) and `test_resource_table_full_error_message_matches_log_symptom` (documents the failure mode so future maintainers recognize the log symptom). |
| D32 | No-op `update_settings_file` write skips file write + observer notification | `crates/settings/src/settings_store.rs` + `crates/language_models/src/provider/runpod.rs` | Upstream's `SettingsStore::update_settings_file_inner` unconditionally calls `fs.atomic_write` (triggering the settings file watcher) and `cx.update_global::<SettingsStore>` (whose `end_global_lease` pushes `Effect::NotifyGlobalObservers` even when `set_user_settings` returns `Unchanged`) on every call, even when the `new_text` is byte-for-byte identical to `old_text`. This created a self-sustaining feedback loop (historical trigger, since removed): `ensure_openai_compatible_entries` (then wired to a `SettingsStore` observer in `main.rs`) called `update_settings_file` on every settings notification → the file write triggered the file watcher → `cx.update_global` pushed `NotifyGlobalObservers` → the observer re-fired → `ensure_openai_compatible_entries` re-wrote the same file → loop. The RunPod "auto_discover is enabled but no API key is set" warning fired on every iteration because the RunPod `State`'s `SettingsStore` observer called `cx.notify()` unconditionally (unlike its siblings `ollama`, `llama_cpp`, `open_router`, `api_compatible`, which all guard with `if settings_changed`). **Fix 1 (root cause):** `update_settings_file_inner` now compares `new_text` to `old_text` and skips both the `fs.atomic_write` and the `cx.update_global` when they are equal, breaking the loop for all callers. `set_user_settings` already early-returns `Unchanged` on identical content, so skipping the `update_global` entirely is consistent with the in-memory store's own idempotency. **Fix 2 (amplifier):** the RunPod `State` observer now tracks `last_settings` and only calls `cx.notify()` when the settings actually changed, matching the `ollama`/`llama_cpp`/`open_router`/`api_compatible` pattern. This stops the warning spam even if some unrelated settings write fires the observer. **Why not the bridge?** The `update_settings_file_inner` fix is in upstream's `SettingsStore` impl — the bridge is for kask-specific behavior, not for patching upstream settings-store internals. This is a genuine D-seam: an upstream bug fix that benefits all Zed users (any caller that writes the same settings content twice now skips the file write and observer storm. Note: this skip initially defeated `nudge_mcp_servers` (D9) — its file-write path stopped firing observers — so the nudge was re-fixed to call `SettingsStore::notify_observers` directly (`kask_page.rs`), pinned by `test_noop_update_settings_file_does_not_fire_settings_store_observer`). Should be reported upstream to `zed-industries/zed`. Pinned by `test_update_settings_file_skips_noop_write` (verifies that a no-op write does not touch the filesystem or fire observers) and `test_update_settings_file_writes_when_content_differs` (verifies the write still happens when content actually changes). |
| D35 | Media MCP server + MediaRouter + MovieLabs OMC ontology | `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-media/` (28 source files) + `kask/crates/hkask-inference/src/{media_router.rs, provider.rs, media_providers.rs}` + `kask/crates/hkask-bridge-ontology/src/omc.rs` + `kask/crates/hkask-types/src/{voice.rs, ports/inference_port.rs (MediaGenerateParams, MediaFuture, media_generate trait method)}` + `kask/crates/hkask-inference/src/{hkask_inference.rs (LazyInferencePort::media_generate — child-local dispatch), model_constants.rs (DEFAULT_TTS/STT/VISION/IMAGE_GEN_MODEL)}` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/{mcp_servers.rs (media server registration)}` + `.agents/skills/create-skill/SKILL.md` (OMC ontology row) + `Cargo.toml` (workspace member + nom-exif dep) | **Historical recovered media stack (creative execution superseded by the 2026-10-21 research-curation continuation below)** — the media MCP server (domain-grouped tools: gallery management, image/video/audio generation, transcription, and face recognition), the bespoke `MediaRouter` (pluggable `MediaProvider` trait + `ProviderRegistry` for non-chat media APIs that zed's `LanguageModel` cannot represent), and the MovieLabs OMC ontology bridge (9 seed concepts: CreativeWork, Scene, Shot, Sequence, Participant, Capture, Asset, Task, VERSION_INFO + `tool_to_omc` mapping for all registered tools (in `hkask-mcp-media/src/omc.rs`; the concepts + `explain_tool_for` dispatch live in `hkask-bridge-ontology/src/omc.rs`)). **Media generation is child-local (2026-08-31)**: `LazyInferencePort::media_generate` dispatches to a process-local `MediaRouter` (`LOCAL_MEDIA_ROUTER` OnceLock in `hkask_inference.rs`) built from the MCP server process's env-injected keys (`DEEPINFRA_API_KEY` / `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` via the server's credential allowlist) — no IPC round-trip. The former IPC path (`InferenceMethod::MediaGenerate` variant, `InferenceOutcome::Media` outcome, `InferenceParams::media_*` fields, `InferenceIpcClient::call_media_generate`, the zed-side `media_router` field on `LanguageModelInferencePort`, and the `MediaGenerate` dispatch arm) is deleted: the zed-side router was built from `InferenceConfig::from_env()` — the zed process env, which never contains the keys (they are injected only into child processes) — so every IPC-routed media call failed with "no provider configured" even with keys installed, and the IPC branch shadowed the working child-local fallback. Media APIs are not LanguageModel calls, so the bridge bought nothing; vision/chat/embed/list_models still route over the IPC bridge to zed's `LanguageModelRegistry`. **Operator-ratified routing policy (2026-09-06):** resolve `params.model` first, otherwise the per-operation env model; require full `OpenRouter/...` or `DeepInfra/...` qualification (ASCII case-insensitive provider names, nonempty local model, no whitespace). No bare/short aliases, score/order selection, or automatic cross-provider retry on any failure. Dispatch once to the named registered provider with only the local model; preserve typed errors (Model → invalid_argument, NotConfigured/Auth → permission_denied). Fixed DeepInfra background removal/upscale require no model env and reject overrides. Supersedes `d660f3b754` scoring/fallback and `8cc79c797e` order routing; preserves `f86cf19a70` child-local and foreground media behavior. Scorer and the unused MediaRouter chat facade are removed. Pins: `media_routing_tests` (including real loopback adapters and LazyInferencePort with zero IPC calls) and media `error::tests::real_router_http_auth_reaches_media_tool_mapper`. **GalleryStore**: SQLite-backed gallery with image indexing, tag persistence, face registry, generation lineage, and EXIF timeline grouping. Restored to `hkask-storage` with `SqliteDriver::file_pool` re-added for unencrypted file-backed pools, then moved to the media server at `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-media/src/gallery/` (`c85b1e4896`), taking the `file_pool` path with it. **Deprecated rJoule/budget/gas system stripped**: `budget.rs` deleted, all `charge_budget` calls removed from tool handlers, `VerificationStore` and `hkask-templates` dependencies removed. **Server registration**: `BuiltinMcpServer` entry with `HKASK_INFERENCE_SOCKET`, `HKASK_DATA_DIR`, `HKASK_MEDIA_DB`, and media model env vars in `config_env`; `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` + `DEEPINFRA_API_KEY` in `credentials` (child-local `MediaRouter`), plus `HKASK_SERPAPI_API_KEY` (YouTube discovery). Pinned by `media_router::tests::{media_generate_unknown_op_errors, media_generate_no_provider_errors_clearly}` + `media_providers::tests::{detect_audio_format_from_extension, detect_audio_format_defaults_to_mp3}` + `omc::tests` (19 tests in `omc.rs` plus the `tool_names_match_live_router` and `omc_mapping_covers_all_registered_tools` pins in `hkask_mcp_media.rs`) + `gallery.rs::tests` (20 tests including durability across restart) + `levenshtein_tests` (5 tests). |

| D36 | Mid-JSON EOF tool-call fragments are classified truncation in every provider mapper (upstreaming candidate); a stop-finish with accumulated calls drains as defensive compatibility, never observed live | `crates/language_model_core/src/chat_completion.rs` + `crates/language_model_core/src/util.rs` (the shared `tool_call_truncation_error` primitive) + `crates/language_model_core/src/language_model_core.rs` (`ToolCallTruncated`) + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` + `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` + `crates/anthropic/src/completion.rs` + `crates/open_ai/src/completion.rs` (Responses) + `crates/copilot_chat/src/model.rs` (chat + responses) + `crates/language_models/src/provider/mistral.rs` + `crates/language_models/src/provider/deepseek.rs` | Some providers (e.g. GLM via OpenRouter) emit tool deltas but finish with `stop`, not `tool_calls`. The 2026-09-10 merge moves the Kask drain from the deleted OpenAI/OpenRouter mappers into the live shared `ChatCompletionEventMapper`. A stop with pending, non-rejected calls whose arguments ALL parse as complete JSON uses the existing tool-call drain and emits `StopReason::ToolUse`; a stop without calls remains EndTurn. **Truncation classification (2026-09-27, operator ruling "remove the agent truncation behavior at the root", completed same day after log forensics):** a tool-call finish — stop-remapped or a genuine `tool_calls` — with any accumulated call whose arguments end mid-JSON is a stream cut mid-tool-call. The discriminator is serde's error category: `Category::Eof` means the input ended before the JSON was complete — every signature in the 2026-09-27 storm, and all 35 pre-restart log occurrences, were EOF-classified "EOF while parsing …", and the storm finished with `tool_calls`, not `stop` (the pre-restart log holds zero stop-remap drain warns), so a finish-reason test alone cannot catch it. The mapper emits `LanguageModelCompletionError::ToolCallTruncated` (tool name, parse error, raw argument length), dispatches nothing, and emits no stop event, so the turn cannot end cleanly. Every provider mapper door — the shared Chat-Completions mapper, Anthropic native, OpenAI Responses, Copilot chat + responses, Mistral, DeepSeek — classifies through the one shared `tool_call_truncation_error` primitive (`util.rs`), so a cut is named identically regardless of wire dialect; accumulated-drain doors (OpenAI Responses output, Copilot chat, Mistral, DeepSeek) pre-scan the whole batch so a complete sibling never dispatches before the cut is seen, and per-block doors (Anthropic, OpenAI Responses deltas, Copilot responses) convert per call. The thread's stream-error boundary warns uniformly and sets the D25 `last_completion_truncated` flag for the variant (`KaskThreadState::is_tool_call_truncation` → `on_completion_truncated`), so pending partial tool input reports as truncated, not canceled. The fragment never reaches a tool, never produces a tool result, and never counts against the tool-failure cap (the 2026-09-27 live session exhausted `terminal` and `grep` to hard refusal through that counting). The thread's stream-error boundary sets the D25 `last_completion_truncated` flag for the variant (`KaskThreadState::is_tool_call_truncation`), so pending partial tool input reports as truncated, not canceled; `retry_strategy_for` and `is_transient` classify it non-retryable (a retry re-sends the same request and re-cuts at the same output-length boundary). Empty arguments are not a signature (indistinguishable from a genuine no-argument call) and still drain — the tool boundary owns that case (the committed `reg_query` scopeless-reject). The upstream ordered accumulator, signatures, JSON parse-error events and attribution-error guard remain intact; the model-fault path (a tool-call finish with syntax-error arguments — complete input, malformed) still surfaces the per-call parse error inside a normal tool-call turn. **Funnel classification (2026-09-28):** `handle_tool_use_json_parse_error_event` re-checks with the same discriminator (`parse_tool_arguments` over the raw input + `Category::Eof`): an EOF-shaped error that still reaches the funnel — a door that has not classified the cut — surfaces the actionable cut message (nothing executed; resend the whole call with a shorter payload) instead of the bare parse error the 2026-09-27 storm retried identically 10+ times; non-EOF malformed JSON keeps the plain parse error. Pinned by the updated `test_streaming_tool_json_parse_error_is_forwarded_to_running_tool`. Length remains MaxTokens (D25). Pinned by `stream_maps_stop_with_tool_calls_to_tool_use_stop`, `stop_without_tool_calls_ends_turn`, `stop_with_truncated_tool_arguments_is_classified_as_truncated_not_a_clean_turn`, `truncated_tool_arguments_do_not_dispatch_a_defaulted_or_partial_call`, `stop_with_one_truncated_call_truncates_the_whole_batch`, `tool_calls_finish_with_syntax_error_arguments_still_surfaces_parse_error`, `tool_calls_finish_with_eof_fragment_is_classified_as_truncated`, `tool_calls_finish_with_eof_fragment_truncates_the_whole_batch`, `stop_with_syntax_error_arguments_drains_as_model_fault_recovery`, and the strengthened `does_not_emit_tool_calls_after_an_attribution_error`; `stop_with_invalid_tool_arguments_surfaces_parse_error` is superseded by the truncation-classification pins. The door conversions are pinned per provider: anthropic `test_event_mapper_classifies_eof_fragment_as_truncated`, open_ai `responses_stream_classifies_eof_fragment_arguments_as_truncated` + `responses_stream_completed_batch_with_eof_fragment_dispatches_nothing`, copilot `chat_completions_stream_truncated_tool_arguments_emit_error_not_stop` + `responses_stream_truncated_tool_arguments_emit_error_not_stop`, mistral `test_streamed_tool_call_eof_truncated_arguments_reject_the_batch`, deepseek `streamed_tool_call_eof_truncated_arguments_reject_the_batch_without_stop` (each with a syntax-classified control pinning the unchanged model-fault path). The thread wiring is pinned by `stream_truncation_sets_the_d25_flag_at_the_stream_error_boundary` (source-structure, concat! needles) and `is_tool_call_truncation_classifies_only_the_typed_variant` (`kask_thread_state.rs`). **Upstreaming (2026-09-27 essentialist verdict):** the truncation classification is a provider-neutral correctness fix in upstream's own event mappers — report upstream to zed-industries/zed (issue draft prepared for the operator); when upstream adopts it, the fork deletes its copies and the classification half of this seam retires. The stop-remap failed the deletion test on current evidence (zero drain warns in every available log; the 2026-09-03 attribution used the misdiagnosis the 2026-09-27 log forensics exposed) and is kept as demoted defensive compatibility: zero marginal seam cost, guards a silent tool-call-loss mode, and its warn makes any future firing observable. Migrated provider usage-ordering tests are covered by shared `usage_update_precedes_text_and_stop_events_from_the_same_chunk` and `usage_update_precedes_tool_use_and_stop_events_from_the_same_chunk`; reasoning metadata/signatures remain covered by the shared accumulator and mapper tests. **`finish_reason="error"` with accumulated tool calls (2026-10-14, card b186ae67):** OpenRouter emitted complete tool-call arguments in delta chunks (which `map_event` emits with `is_input_complete: false`) and then sent `finish_reason="error"` on the trailing chunk (observed 11× in `Zed-Kask.log` on 2026-10-14). Because only `finish_reason="stop"` was remapped, `map_event` logged `Unexpected chat completion stop_reason: "error"` and emitted `Stop(StopReason::EndTurn)` without draining `self.tool_calls` — leaving every tool call in `Thread.pending_message` at `is_input_complete: false`, which `Thread::flush_pending_message` then flushed as `"Tool canceled by user"`. `ChatCompletionEventMapper::map_event` now applies the same D36 check to `Some("stop" | "error")` when `!self.tool_call_accumulation_failed && !self.tool_calls.calls.is_empty()`: an EOF-truncated argument fragment classifies as `ToolCallTruncated` (which sets `on_completion_truncated()` and flushes `TOOL_TRUNCATED_MESSAGE`), while complete tool calls drain as `Some("tool_calls")`. Pinned by `error_finish_with_complete_tool_calls_drains_as_tool_use_stop` and `error_finish_with_eof_fragment_is_classified_as_truncated` (both observed RED before, GREEN after). |

| D37 | Commit-message and thread-title generation respect reasoning-model requirements (guard + self-healing) | `crates/git_ui/src/git_panel.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` | Upstream's `GitPanel::generate_commit_message` hardcodes `thinking_allowed: false` and `reasoning_effort: None` on the `LanguageModelRequest`. When the resolved commit-message model (which falls back to `default_fast_model` → `default_model` — the user's reasoning model) is a reasoning-only model (gpt-5, o3, claude-opus-5, etc.), the provider rejects the request with a 400 `InvalidRequest` because reasoning cannot be disabled. The error surfaces as "Failed to generate commit message: …" and the generate button appears stuck (the task ends in error) while the rest of the zed-kask session reasons along normally. **Why not kask-side?** Commit-message generation calls `model.stream_completion_text` directly on the `LanguageModel` from `LanguageModelRegistry::commit_message_model` — it does NOT route through `kask_bridge` (D8). There is no kask-side hook in this path; the only interception points are upstream (`registry.rs` model resolution, `git_panel.rs` request building, or routing through the bridge). A kask-side fix would require a strictly larger upstream edit (adding a hook + bridge indirection, or rerouting through the bridge). The minimal divergence is at the call site. **Fix (two layers):** (1) **Guard** — `thinking_allowed = model.supports_thinking()` and `reasoning_effort = thinking_allowed.then(|| "low".to_string())`. Commit messages are simple, so low effort minimizes tokens while satisfying the model's reasoning requirement. (2) **Self-healing retry** — if the initial request fails with a `ProviderRejection` whose category is `InvalidRequest` (400) and thinking was not allowed, retry once with `thinking_allowed = true` and `reasoning_effort = Some("low")`. This catches reasoning-only models whose `supports_thinking()` returns false because the provider doesn't advertise `reasoning_effort` in model metadata (common for OpenAI-compatible providers where `/v1/models` discovery leaves `reasoning_effort: None`). The retry is bounded to one attempt and only fires on `InvalidRequest` (not rate limits, auth, or transport errors). Mid-stream errors do not retry (partial text may already be in the buffer). The `is_reasoning_required_error` helper classifies the error. Pinned by `test_commit_message_guard_enables_thinking_for_reasoning_model`, `test_commit_message_guard_disables_thinking_for_non_reasoning_model`, and `test_is_reasoning_required_error_classifies_invalid_request` in `git_panel.rs`. **Thread-title generation (absorbs retired D38 — same class, same fix pattern):** `Thread::spawn_title_generation` (`crates/agent/src/thread.rs`) carries the same guard + self-healing retry — upstream's `build_thread_title_request` leaves `thinking_allowed` at default `false`, so a reasoning-only summarization model rejects with 400 `InvalidRequest` and the title never generates (recurring `ERROR [agent::thread]` every ~30s, placeholder title). Fix mirrors this seam: guard (`thinking_allowed = model.supports_thinking()`, `reasoning_effort = "low"` when supported) + one bounded retry on `InvalidRequest` via `is_reasoning_required_title_error` — catches reasoning-only models whose `supports_thinking()` returns false because the provider doesn't advertise `reasoning_effort` in metadata (the GLM 5.2 case). 2026-09-10 merge: the request builder now receives `&self.id`; the fork's `reasoning_effort` naming retained (`5269cf00ca`). Pinned by `test_is_reasoning_required_title_error_classifies_invalid_request` and `test_thread_title_guard_preserves_thread_id_and_reasoning_effort`. |

| D39 | PaymentRequired errors name the rejecting provider | `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view/thread_view.rs` | Upstream now reserves the Zed-Pro upgrade copy for `ZED_CLOUD_PROVIDER_NAME`, but funnels third-party `ProviderErrorCategory::PaymentRequired` into a generic, provider-less `ProviderRejection` (`upstream/main` merge parent `7fc2cf9b84`, `conversation_view.rs`). A 402/billing rejection from OpenRouter, Anthropic, or OpenAI therefore loses which account needs attention; it must not be presented as a Zed subscription problem. Historically, an older upstream mapping showed "Free Usage Exceeded / Upgrade to Zed Pro" for an OpenRouter account out of credit even for a logged-in Pro subscriber. The current fork retains provider-specific payment guidance rather than relying on that older reproduction. **Why not kask-side?** The missing provider attribution lives in zed's error-to-UI mapping (`ThreadError::From<anyhow::Error>` + `render_payment_required_error` in `agent_ui`); there is no kask-side hook for agent-panel error copy. **Fix:** `ThreadError::PaymentRequired` now carries `provider: SharedString` (populated from the `ProviderRejection` provider in the `From` impl); `payment_required_message(provider)` returns the Zed-Pro upgrade copy only for `ZED_CLOUD_PROVIDER_NAME`, and "{provider} rejected the request because your {provider} account is out of credit or over its spend limit…" for every other provider; the upgrade button renders only for Zed-cloud rejections (title switches from "Free Usage Exceeded" to "Payment Required"); telemetry carries the same provider-aware message. Pinned by `test_payment_required_preserves_provider` (conversation_view.rs — From-impl preserves the rejecting provider's name) and `payment_required_message_tests::{zed_cloud_provider_keeps_upgrade_copy, third_party_provider_names_its_credit_balance}` (thread_view.rs — Zed copy unchanged; third-party copy names the provider, never offers a Zed upgrade). |
| D40 | Division of Responsibilities — the four-moves interaction loop | `crates/agent/src/templates/system_prompt.hbs` (`## Division of Responsibilities (kask)` section + amended autonomy bullet in `## Task Execution` + functional-first bullet in `## Final Message`) + `crates/agent/src/templates.rs` (role, authority and prompt-corpus pin tests) | Implements Phase A of the functional-interaction spec ([`kask/docs/architecture/functional-interaction-spec.md`](kask/docs/architecture/functional-interaction-spec.md)): the user is the product manager (keeper of functional requirements, judge of what work is for); the agent fits the technical implementation to those requirements. The section carries the four moves — point at the same target (intake interpretation, submitted for user correction; the agent interprets the functional requirement, never revises it), bring choices to the user as experiences (options + recommendation, user decides), report outcomes not artifacts (functional outcome before technical detail), bank the learning. **Framing discipline:** the text is preference-framed (evaluation axis: "the user evaluates this work by what it lets them do") per the spec's gradient-over-constraint principle — no prohibition walls, which habituate and provoke escape-seeking. The autonomy bullet's ask-trigger is extended from "information unavailable / risky" to include "a choice changes what the user will experience" — without this, the bullet licenses unilateral functional decisions (the agent has the information; the question is authority, not information). The same bullet's "rather than coming back to the user prematurely" is reframed to "for information you can find yourself" — "prematurely" framed user-contact as cost and biased against interpretation rounds and choice-surfacing. The `## Ambition vs. Precision` bullet's "high-value, creative touches when scope is vague" is replaced with "high-value additions when they serve the functional goal" + "When scope is vague, resolve the vagueness with the user (see Division of Responsibilities) rather than filling it with initiative" — the upstream phrase licensed unilateral product decisions in exactly the situation (vague scope) that is the four-moves' ask-trigger. The Final Message gains a functional-outcome-first bullet — the prior bullet alone rehearsed a file-list summary format every turn. **Goal wiring cutover (2026-10-20):** the create-availability conditional records confirmed source/specification and worker progress without routine probability. Native close and correction instructions each have their own tool-availability conditional. The model may submit evidence, never human outcome fields; actual ActionChoice records closure. Canonical history is retained; blinded findings and optional HumanAcceptance forecasts stay separate under the D58 contract. Prompt/corpus pins in `templates.rs` cover retired-symbol absence, authority/admission guidance, full-tool and missing-native-tool rendering. **Skill rubrics (2026-09-04, operator-ratified):** a paragraph after the four moves names each side's operational rubric — the `program-manager` skill (the agent's side: spec recovery, design-before-coding, actual verification and honest reporting; the former self-scored closure ledger was removed on 2026-10-21) and the `product-manager` skill (the user's side: the intake contract — requirement, spec provenance, acceptance criteria, constraints, decisions, confirmation) — and directs underspecified intake to the product-manager skill rather than improvisation. Pinned by `test_system_prompt_contains_division_of_responsibilities` (section + all four moves + authority boundary + both skill references + the no-improvisation directive), `test_division_skill_references_resolve_on_disk` (both named skills exist on disk — a prompt reference to a missing skill is a ghost instruction), `test_system_prompt_autonomy_bullet_includes_experience_trigger`, `test_system_prompt_autonomy_bullet_reframes_prematurely` (also asserts "prematurely" absent), `test_system_prompt_vagueness_resolved_with_user` (also asserts the upstream "creative touches when scope is vague" phrase absent), and `test_system_prompt_final_message_leads_with_functional_outcome` (templates.rs). **Evolution (operator ruling 2026-09-10):** the mid-prompt section underperformed — agents read it as an overlay, kept asking technical questions at intake and ignoring functional requirements (observed live in the companies-fix session: technical questions where functional ones were required, three times). Roles now live in the OPENING of the prompt, not the section: the first line renames the agent ("You are the Z-K agent running inside the Zed-Kask fork of Zed.dev") and fixes the roles (user = product manager owning functional requirements; agent = technical program manager / VP of engineering owning technical implementation), followed by a role paragraph carrying the question-class rule (functional questions are the user's to answer, asked in functional terms; technical questions are the agent's to decide — "do not route technical decisions to the user as questions"). The section at `:304` is now the working loop: it opens "The roles are fixed in the opening of this prompt" and move 2 is **Decide by class** — functional decisions surfaced as experiences with a recommendation, technical decisions decided and presented with their functional consequence, "never route one to the user as a question". Pinned by the updated `test_system_prompt_contains_division_of_responsibilities` (opening name + roles linkage + decide-by-class + both question-class rules) and `test_system_prompt_template` (the Z-K name). **Identity frame (2026-10-17, operator request):** the opening gains the co-research-environment identity sentence immediately before the two-role division — the human is the principal investigator (question, judgment, veto), the agent holds the instruments, the methods, and the memory, with the thesis-charter pointer `kask/docs/architecture/core/co-research-environment.md` — nesting the division as the operating contract within the co-research environment; the program-manager role language is retained (the identity adds, the contract stays; `hypothesis-testing` is the inquiry-cycle method and `experimental-design` its design deep module, not the identity). The Curator overlay's `CURATOR_STATIC_CONTEXT` (`crates/agent/src/curator_agent_server.rs`) gains the parallel `## Identity` block carrying the collaboration test ("does this change deepen the human's seat at the bench, or automate the human out of it?"), preceding the Role block. Pinned by `test_system_prompt_opening_carries_co_research_identity` (identity + charter pointer + the non-replacement pin: `technical program manager` still present) and `curator_static_context_carries_co_research_identity` (identity block, collaboration test, and block order). |
| D41 | Config-file watchers are directory-anchored, not file-anchored | `crates/settings/src/settings_file.rs` (`watch_config_file`) | Upstream's `watch_config_file` watches the config file's own path. On Linux, `RealFs::watch` translates that to an inotify watch on the file's inode — and `RealFs::atomic_write` (tempfile + `persist()`, i.e. rename) replaces the inode on every zed-driven config write. The kernel drops the inode-anchored watch at that moment and nothing re-arms it, so **every later external change to the file is invisible for the rest of the session**. Live root cause of the settings-reactivity defect (2026-08-30): zed's own startup settings write — the kask namespace guard (D3) cleaning `context_servers` — renamed a new inode over `settings.json` one second after launch; from then on "Settings file reloaded by watcher" never fired, `SettingsStore` observers registered in `main()` (including `sync_kask_mcp_servers` and `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers`) never fired on external changes, and the operator's live settings edits were ignored until restart. Verified live: the settings file's inode was absent from the app's inotify watch set (133 surviving watches, ~1947 removed since launch), and an in-place inode-preserving modify produced no watcher line. **Fix:** `watch_config_file` now watches the file's containing directory (mirroring `watch_config_dir`'s existing directory-anchored design) and filters event batches to the file's own path (plus `Rescan` markers), so rename-overs and in-place writes both survive while sibling activity in the config directory does not trigger reloads. Benefits every `watch_config_file` consumer: user/global settings, keymap, `.editorconfig`, `AGENTS.md`, recent-projects, remote-server, and project local settings. Should be reported upstream — any atomic-saving editor (sed -i, jq > tmp && mv) breaks upstream's file-anchored watch the same way. Pinned by `test_watch_config_file_is_directory_anchored_and_ignores_siblings` (asserts the watch root is the parent directory via `watched_paths` — the line that fails if the watch reverts to file-anchored — that an inode-replacing `atomic_write` is still delivered, and that sibling-file events do not reload the config file). **2026-09-27 advisory audit:** upstreaming candidate, retain; local `upstream/main@2c4bc2d7b2c5` still watches the file path, not its directory (remote freshness unverified). The named FakeFs pin ran 1 test, 1 passed; it checks routing and filtering, not actual Linux inotify longevity. No controlled current live-fire confirmation. Issue draft and oracle limits: `kask/docs/research/d-seam-audit.md` D41; report upstream only after operator review. **2026-10-27 follow-up (probe-verified):** the 2026-10-27 sync brought upstream's reworked `fs_watcher` (folded subscriptions, +566 lines); under it, a file-creation event is NOT delivered to a child watch whose directory is created after watch-time when a parent-anchored watch overlaps (the event routes only to the ancestor subscription; the child receives just the directory event, which the D41 filter correctly drops). Reproduced with a probe test over the FakeFs (deleted after diagnosis); the live shape at risk is an ancestor subscription overlapping a descendant-dir watch created later — first-run config-tree creation is the closest live case. `test_disable_ai_menu_update_with_malformed_keymap` (`crates/zed/src/zed.rs`) pins the broken shape and is `#[ignore]`d with the reason until upstream fixes the fan-out; upstream-reportable per this row's precedent. |

The former hardcoded 4096-token reasoning cap silently truncated reasoning-heavy turns; reasoning depth is the model's business, not a hidden client budget. **2026-09-10 merge:** preserve this operator requirement while carrying upstream supported/default efforts, supports-max-tokens and mandatory-reasoning metadata. Explicit user Thinking budgets still pass through; Adaptive settings use upstream typed `ReasoningEffort` with the fork's `reasoning_effort` field. Recovered policy from `5f939570bb` and `a2134949e2` remains authoritative: explicit user disable sends `effort: none` regardless of mandatory-reasoning metadata; the removed `supports_disabling_thinking` and Responses API `supports_none_reasoning_effort` gates are not restored. OpenRouter Thinking requests retain `exclude: false` and the existing parallel-tool-call policy. **QA non-thinking wire pin (2026-09-11):** `test_explicit_reasoning_disable_overrides_mode_and_mandatory_metadata` now also serializes the actual OpenRouter request and asserts `reasoning.effort = "none"` and `enabled = false`; `exclude` alone is not a disable. Synchronous direct HTTP already emits effort none; no live probe is claimed. Pinned by strengthened `reasoning_models_get_uncapped_thinking_budget`, `test_discovered_thinking_is_uncapped_in_request` (uncapped discovery and explicit budget passthrough), `test_explicit_reasoning_disable_overrides_mode_and_mandatory_metadata`, and upstream Adaptive request tests. Related Anthropic/Bedrock/cloud hidden caps were removed in the same ruling (absorbs retired D49): the D13 budget removal (`ce9cb47c6c`) left hardcoded `budget_tokens: Some(4_096)` enforcement in three provider mode constructors — the same defect class as the open_router 4096 that silently killed reasoning-heavy turns. Removed 2026-09-04 under the operator's budget-deprecation ruling ("the budget concept is deprecated and should have been removed; timeouts are the enforcement/kill mechanism for runaway or looping processes") — `budget_tokens: None`, thinking stays enabled, reasoning depth is the model's business. The user-facing `budget_tokens` Option in settings remains the operator's explicit knob (the passthroughs in `crates/language_models/src/provider/{anthropic,anthropic_compatible,bedrock,open_router}.rs` carry the user's setting, not a hidden cap — untouched). Pinned by the updated bedrock `thinking_mode` assertions (`Some(4096)` → `None`) and the strengthened anthropic `from_listed` mode assert; the cloud site had no prior pin. Residual same-class sites swept 2026-09-28 under the operator's D83-thread ruling (see D83): the `max_output_tokens.unwrap_or(4_096)` custom-model output-cap fabrications are gone — output-limit metadata is truthful `Option` passthrough everywhere, with one documented mandatory-wire fallback in `into_anthropic`. **2026-10-12 merge:** upstream dropped the `supports_none_reasoning_effort` gate — `into_open_ai_response` is 8→7 args at every call site, the fork's no-hidden-budget policy unchanged. **2026-10-27 merge (naming-cost measurement):** upstream renamed the request field and Thread field to `thinking_effort`/`thinking_enabled` across its tree; the fork's `reasoning_effort` naming was retained and ~12 upstream rename sites were flipped back (request.rs, provider/{google,opencode}.rs, x_ai_subscribed, thread_view, agent tests). This is a recurring per-merge cost while upstream actively renames around this surface — retiring the naming divergence (one large mechanical sweep of the ~428 fork refs to upstream's names, keeping the uncapped-thinking/explicit-disable SUBSTANCE) would eliminate the class; operator decision pending. **Executed 2026-10-27 (operator ruling, commits `3d29142aab` + `73ab8cc198` + `7f257307fe`):** the naming retirement is done — the request field, the Thread/DbThread fields, the `reasoning_effort()`/`set_reasoning_effort()` accessors and all fork call sites now carry upstream's `thinking_effort` name. `DbThread` keeps `#[serde(alias = "reasoning_effort")]` so existing serialized threads still load. The SUBSTANCE is unchanged and still pinned (uncapped thinking, explicit-disable `effort: "none"` passthrough, no hidden budgets). Upstream-aligned names that were NEVER the fork's rename — the OpenAI/DeepSeek/Mistral wire-body fields, model-metadata fields and accessor methods, the settings-content fields — were verified against upstream and kept as `reasoning_effort`. The rename-flip merge-conflict class is gone. |
| D43 | Turn-limit diagnostics and native compaction recovery control | `crates/language_model_core/src/util.rs` (`is_context_window_exceeded_message`) + `crates/language_model_core/src/language_model_core.rs` (classification regression test) + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view/thread_view.rs` (`render_compact_control`) + `crates/open_router/src/open_router.rs` (`OpenRouterErrorBody::message_with_details`) + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` (turn-end `CompletionError::MaxTokens` arm + turn-failure `Err` arm) + `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` (`max_tokens_turn_end_warning` + `provider_rejection_turn_end_warning` + tests) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/inference_chat.rs` (`completion_error_detail` + both conversion sites + tests — kask-side, no D-seam) | **Compaction rejection corrections (2026-09-11, `494e61550e`):** `build_compaction_request` applies D38's helper-request rule: `thinking_allowed` follows the selected compaction model's `supports_thinking`, rather than defaulting to false and rejecting mandatory-reasoning endpoints. `test_manual_compact_forces_summary` now pins reasoning as well as precompression and native summary storage. The shared context-overflow recognizer also accepts the upstream plain-text form "maximum context length ... but the request requires ..." without a `context_length_exceeded` code; `plain_provider_context_overflow_is_not_a_generic_invalid_request` pins the incident message and an unrelated-invalid-request counterexample. Both regressions were observed red before correction and green afterward. This correction changes request settings and classification only; the subsequent two-half summarization design is recorded under D8. **Native recovery control (2026-09-10, `b8d8f6b064`):** a Compact context icon follows the reasoning control in the composer's existing wrapping group. It uses the existing native command-turn dispatch, preserving the unsent draft; native command availability controls visibility, and busy/loading/empty state disables activation with a click-time recheck. No new compaction lifecycle or persistence format. `test_compact_command_gate_requires_native_category` pins native-command recognition; rendered click/draft and narrow-layout behavior remain unverified. **OpenRouter rejection detail (2026-09-10, `4cdde2c6e5`):** both HTTP and SSE error conversion retain `metadata.provider_name` and `metadata.raw` in the message instead of dropping the upstream reason behind "Provider returned error". Other metadata is not appended. A live synthetic 720,023-token prompt reproduced the reported 400: provider context 1,048,576, combined requested tokens 1,303,727, raw code `context_length_exceeded`. Retaining that code also enables the existing `PromptTooLarge` classification and Context Too Large UI; no new retry, output-limit or compaction policy. Pinned through the HTTP transport and SSE decoder by `provider_error_details_survive_http_and_streaming_responses`, including metadata-free responses. Upstream sends `acp::StopReason::MaxTokens` with no operator-visible signal — no error, no warn, no UI message. Combined with D42's root cause (a provider-side reasoning cap), this was the silence that made the 2026-08-30 silent-turn-stop incident diagnosable only by inference: the log showed prompt → silence → partial ingest with nothing explaining the stop. **Why not kask-side?** The stop is emitted in `Thread`'s turn-completion handler; there is no kask hook on that path. **Fix:** the `MaxTokens` arm now warns via `log::warn!` (target `agent.thread`) with the thread id, the model, and `KaskThreadState::max_tokens_turn_end_warning(model, produced_content)` — which distinguishes the zero-content signature (prompt → silence → nothing) from partial-content truncation. `produced_content` is computed from the messages appended after the turn's `message_ix` boundary (the same boundary the Refusal arm truncates to). **Extension (2026-09-10, same match block):** the turn-failure `Err` arm had the mirror-image gap for provider rejections — `log::error!("Turn execution failed: {:?}", error)` prints the anyhow Display, and `ProviderRejection`'s Display is the provider's wire message only, so the status, provider code, category and retry-after that the variant deliberately preserves for diagnostics reached no log. Made live by the 2026-09-10 incident: five turn failures across three sessions in three minutes, all logging only `Provider returned error` (OpenRouter's generic upstream-failure wrapper for GLM 5.3) — credit exhaustion (402), rate limiting (429), an upstream outage (502, which `retry_strategy_for` retries four times over ~75 s before the turn fails) and a statusless mid-stream rejection (classified permanent, fails sub-second with no retry) are indistinguishable in the log yet demand different operator action. The `Err` arm now downcasts to `LanguageModelCompletionError` and routes `ProviderRejection`s through `KaskThreadState::provider_rejection_turn_end_warning` — one `log::error!` (target `agent.thread`) carrying provider, status, code, category, retry_after, the wire message, and the `is_transient` verdict (the exact predicate `retry_strategy_for` gates on, so the line states whether the failure followed retry exhaustion or happened on the first attempt). **Why not kask-side (extension):** the same handler, the same reason — the main-thread completion path (`Thread::send` → `model.stream_completion` on the registry model) never routes through `kask_bridge` (D8 serves MCP-server inference over IPC only), so no bridge-side code can observe these errors; the helper lives in `KaskThreadState` (the kask-side home inside the agent crate, per the D2/D6 pattern), keeping the zed-side footprint to one log block + its pin. **Bridge-path fix (same session, kask-side — no D-seam):** the bridge had the mirror-image gap — `StreamAccumulator::process_event` flattened every mid-stream error to `InferenceError::Generation(e.to_string())` and the stream-establishment `map_err` to `Connection(error.to_string())`, so MCP-server inference (corpus QA, condenser) lost the same wire fields. `completion_error_detail` (kask_bridge `inference_chat.rs`) now enriches both strings at the boundary — the one place that sees both worlds; `InferenceError` stays string-carrying because hkask-types must not depend on zed-side types (`StatusCode`, `ProviderErrorCategory`), so the variant remains the programmatic classification and the string carries the detail. The classification verdict states the class only (`transient`/`permanent`) — retry behavior is the caller's policy, not the bridge's to claim. Pinned by `max_tokens_warning_names_the_stop_reason_and_content_state` (kask_thread_state.rs — asserts the warning names `StopReason::MaxTokens`, the model, and the zero-content vs partial-content distinction), `provider_rejection_detail_names_wire_fields_and_transient_verdict` + `provider_rejection_detail_marks_statusless_permanent_rejections` + `provider_rejection_detail_is_none_for_non_rejections` (kask_thread_state.rs — wire fields, the statusless/permanent signature, and the non-rejection pass-through), and `turn_failure_surfaces_provider_rejection_detail` (thread.rs — source-structure pin; the needle is assembled via `concat!` so the test's own source cannot satisfy it). Bridge-path pins: `completion_error_detail_carries_rejection_wire_fields` + `completion_error_detail_marks_statusless_rejections_permanent` + `completion_error_detail_passes_non_rejections_through` (formatter), `mid_stream_rejection_surfaces_wire_detail_through_the_port` (behavioral — full port → `InferenceError::Generation` carries the fields), and `establishment_errors_route_through_the_detail_helper` (source-structure pin — the fake cannot inject a ProviderRejection at establishment). |
| D44 | Full MCP tool surface + visibility marker + discovery meta-tool (LazyToolRouter removed) | `crates/agent/src/tools/list_mcp_tools_tool.rs` (new `list_mcp_tools` tool) + `crates/agent/src/tools.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` (`count_hidden_mcp_tools`, `render_system_prompt`, `add_default_tools`) + `crates/agent/src/templates.rs` + `crates/agent/src/templates/system_prompt.hbs` + `assets/settings/default.json` (profile allowlists) + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/tool_permissions_setup.rs` (EXCLUDED_TOOLS) | **2026-09-10 merge:** `build_completion_request` retains Kask's UTF-8-safe, 200-byte description cutoff while consuming upstream's no-argument `input_schema() -> Value`; it no longer calls the removed format adapter or treats schema retrieval as fallible. The eligible-tool surface, visibility marker and cached conditional-rules prompt remain. The `LazyToolRouter` — a per-turn keyword-scoring filter that retained ≤ 40 of ~361 registered MCP tools, scored against the last user message only — was **removed** (2026-08-30) after a live incident exposed its failure mode: the model read the pruned list as the complete toolset and reported registered tools as "unavailable" (an agent denied `web_ping` existed; the operator caught the false report). The removal case: (1) six consumer escape hatches (built-ins, skills, scoped threads, exact names) plus three fail-open gates — every special consumer bypassed it and every uncertainty path surrendered to full surface, so it acted only in the narrow band "complex message, confident keyword match, no skill, no scope" — exactly where main sessions live; (2) its recall-1.00 eval was self-graded; (3) per-turn selection changes the tools array every turn, busting provider prefix caching (a stable full list caches; a changing 40-tool subset may not); (4) within-domain sampling gave sessions incoherent slices (8 of media's 68 tools — enough to look covered, not enough to complete a workflow). **What replaced it (three parts):** (a) the per-turn keyword router no longer prunes `enabled_tools`; profile and delegation restrictions still apply; (b) the D44 visibility marker: `render_system_prompt` counts registered-but-hidden MCP tools (`count_hidden_mcp_tools`) and the system prompt renders, only when non-zero, a Tool Use bullet naming the count, forbidding absence claims, and pointing at the recovery path — the current turn's available list can still omit registered tools (for example, through profile restrictions), and the marker keeps those absences legible; (c) the `list_mcp_tools` meta-tool: the model pulls the registered surface on demand (grouped by server, name + description, optional case-insensitive substring filter over server id / tool name / description) — discovery by pull instead of pre-selection by push, the pattern that scales in the tool-retrieval literature. The `kask.tool_router` settings section, its UI toggle, and the `set_tool_router` hook were removed with the router; existing settings files with a `kask.tool_router` key ignore it (no `deny_unknown_fields`). If the fleet grows to where schema tokens are a material context fraction (~2x today), the revisit design is whole-server (domain) selection per user message — never within-domain sampling. Pinned by `test_list_mcp_tools_enumerates_and_filters` (tests/mod.rs — real path: source → registry surface-change signal → listing; grouping + all three filter axes + empty-on-no-match; the registry re-reads the source on the process-global change notification `notify_kask_tool_surface_changed` instead of the former 2s poll, deleted 2026-09-22 with the poll loops in main.rs and `ContextServerRegistry::new` — discovery, connection-loss, stop and shutdown events (`McpRuntime::tool_surface_changes`) drive both refresh layers now), `test_system_prompt_names_tools_hidden_by_profile` (tests/mod.rs — integration: profile filters a tool, marker names the count), `test_system_prompt_names_registered_mcp_tools_hidden_this_turn` + `test_system_prompt_omits_hidden_mcp_section_when_nothing_hidden` (templates.rs — marker render states), and `count_hidden_mcp_tools_excludes_visible_and_counts_hidden` (thread.rs). |
| D45 | Kask managed servers in Settings → AI → MCP Servers + runtime load/unload | `crates/settings_ui/Cargo.toml` + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/mcp_servers_page.rs` + `crates/settings_ui/src/settings_ui.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/context_server_registry.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` (test impl) + `crates/agent/src/tests/mod.rs` (test impl) + `crates/ui/src/components/ai/ai_setting_item.rs` (`AiSettingItemSource::BuiltIn`) + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (`sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` + launch-pass latch) + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page/mcp_servers.rs` + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page.rs` (stale-count copy) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/settings.rs` (stale-count doc) + `kask/docs/reference/kask-settings.md` | The Settings → AI → MCP Servers page reads only the per-project `ContextServerStore`, so the built-in kask servers (single spawn authority, D3) were invisible there — their only management surface was Settings → Kask → MCP Servers (load toggles). **Part 1 — UI:** a "Kask Managed Servers" section on the AI MCP page renders every `BUILT_IN_MCP_SERVERS` entry as an `AiSettingItem` with a new `AiSettingItemSource::BuiltIn` variant (Kask icon; "is a built-in kask MCP server, managed by the kask runtime" tooltip), row status derived from the `kask.mcp` load set + discovered tools + non-blocking live-connection reading through the process-global `KaskToolSource` (`Running` only for connected servers with tools / neutral `Unknown` for loaded but disconnected, unreadable, or tool-less servers / `Stopped` for unloaded), the registry description as the row detail, and a toggle writing `kask.mcp.overrides` — the same write the Kask page performs. The section renders without a project (kask servers are app-global). No configure/uninstall buttons: the command/env are managed (`build_mcp_server_env`), not user-editable. **Part 2 — runtime load/unload:** the `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` observer previously only restarted servers whose *env* changed — a load toggle does not change env (`kask_server_env` excludes the override), so toggling a server OFF left it running and toggling a never-launched server ON never started it. The observer now also computes the load set (the same expression as the launch loop: `load_default && overrides.get(id).unwrap_or(true)`) and stops/starts explicitly through the runtime's own primitives, preserving the self-healing semantics: `stop_server` is idempotent and drops the launch spec (a deliberately unloaded server is not resurrected by the health supervisor or the on-demand reconnect); `start_server_with_env` carries startup retry/backoff, launch-spec recording, and health-supervisor wiring, and is idempotent for a live connection. Discovery publishes metadata with the connection; no empty preregistration or public metadata-mutation API is needed. The start path is gated on a **launch-pass-complete `AtomicBool` latch** (set at the end of the deferred launch loop, unconditionally — an empty `servers_to_start` is still a completed pass): before the latch, "no baseline" means the launch pass hasn't decided, and an observer-side start could race the launch loop with an env computed before the inference socket exists (the socketless-server defect class). The latch also makes the launch loop's "on start failure the baseline is dropped so a later settings change retries" comment true — previously the observer skipped no-baseline servers, so nothing retried a failed start until a tool call hit the recorded launch spec. The latch threads through `wire_kask_inference_stack` (its post-socket re-sync uses the same observer). **Baseline at initiation (2026-10-02, P7h):** the observer records a server's new env baseline in the decision loop at restart/start initiation (removed again on failure), matching the deferred launch loop's record-before-start discipline — recording only after the async start completed let overlapping sync passes (socket-set re-sync + settings observer + wiring re-sync) each re-restart the same server mid-flight (2026-10-02: portfolio 4× in 13 s). Pinned by `kask_restart_baseline_is_recorded_at_initiation` (main.rs source pin). **Stale copy fixed in the same pass:** hand-written counts were removed; the registry currently has 8 servers (companies removed 2026-10-18; swarm removed 2026-10-28). Pinned by `kask_managed_server_status_requires_liveness_and_discovery`, `kask_tool_counts_group_descriptors_by_server`, `kask_server_loaded_follows_master_toggle_and_overrides` (settings_ui `mcp_servers_page.rs` tests), `builtin_source_tooltip_names_the_kask_runtime` (ui `ai_setting_item.rs`), and the updated `kask_wiring_symbols_exist` fn-pointer pins for `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` + `wire_kask_inference_stack` (zed `main.rs` — the latch parameter). **Truthful zero-tool fallback:** the shared `AiSettingItemStatus::Unknown` has a neutral dot, no startup animation, and a status-not-confirmed tooltip. A loaded built-in with no registered tools or no confirmed live connection maps to `Unknown`, not `Starting` or fabricated `Error`; actual per-project `ContextServerStatus::Starting` still maps to `Starting`. Pinned by `kask_managed_server_status_requires_liveness_and_discovery` and `unknown_server_status_is_neutral_and_not_animated`. The open settings window subscribes to runtime tool/liveness changes through the existing agent surface signal and re-renders; a synchronous liveness read that contends with a runtime write conservatively shows `Unknown`. Tool registration alone does not certify runtime health. |
| D46 | Build profile seam + self-observing install | root `Cargo.toml` (`[profile.release-mcp]` + `[profile.release.package]`) + `kask/scripts/build/install.sh` + `kask/scripts/build/build-monitor.sh` + `kask/scripts/build/check-build-profile.sh` (CI: `kask-invariants.yml`) | **The `zed` binary builds with the `release` profile** (thin LTO; its `[profile.release.package]` override uses `codegen-units = 16`, also present upstream). Kask MCP servers build on `[profile.release-mcp]` (inherits release, `lto = false`, `codegen-units = 16`): they are I/O daemons, LTO buys them nothing, and building them on `release` pinned one core per crate for minutes — the install CPU-burn defect (an install pegged all 24 cores / ~25 GB RSS on the dev box and crashed the machine). `install.sh` splits the build into two invocations (`--release --package zed`, then `--profile release-mcp` + the `mcp-servers.txt` packages), caps both with `--jobs min(nproc, 16)` (override: `HKASK_BUILD_JOBS`; raised 8→16 2026-09-02 — the 8 cap left the 24-core dev box at 1/3 capacity, and CI runs no cargo so only local machines observe the cap), and copies servers from `target/release-mcp`. **Every install observes itself**: `build-monitor.sh` samples the compile processes (count, total CPU%, total RSS, busiest) into `target/build-cpu-trace.log` (override: `HKASK_BUILD_TRACE`) — one CSV row per sample; the trace file is the burn record (peaks are the max of the CPU/RSS columns). The sampler self-terminates on parent death, and install.sh stops it with SIGTERM at build end so the trace covers exactly the build. **Peak-summary epilogue removed 2026-09-27 (function-first audit):** the former exit-time summary (`wall_secs/samples/peak_cpu_pct/peak_rss_mb`) never executed on any completed install — the SIGTERM kills the sampler mid-loop — and only ran when install.sh itself died, the opposite of its stated “a burn leaves a number” purpose (a burn is a completed install). Verified by bounded probe: the exact install.sh stop protocol (kill + wait) left zero summary lines; parent death wrote them. The CSV rows were always the delivered record; the dead epilogue and its private peak/sample accumulators are deleted rather than preserved behind a new signal-trapping protocol. The sampler loop, CSV trace, jobs cap, split build and sccache wiring are unchanged; `check-build-profile.sh` still pins the trace wiring. `script/clippy` carries the same jobs cap. **sccache wiring (2026-09-02; wrapper relocated 2026-09-07):** `install.sh` and `script/clippy` export `RUSTC_WRAPPER` when the binary is present and log a warning naming the enable path when it isn't — a missing wrapper must not break the build, but "not configured" must be distinguishable from "configured but broken". `script/setup-sccache` installs the wrapper to `~/.local/lib/kask-sccache/` (`HKASK_SCCACHE_DIR` overrides) — OUTSIDE `target/`, because `cargo clean` deletes the target tree and on 2026-09-07 a clean deleted the wrapper so a full install ran uncached with a healthy 4 GiB disk cache (the silent-uncache defect); `check-build-profile.sh` fails if any of the three scripts references `target/sccache`. `script/setup-sccache` defaults the server cache to 20 GiB (`SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE` override; the 10 GiB sccache default evicts before covering one full profile set of ~800 deps × 5 profiles) and restarts the server so the size applies even when the binary was already cached. The workspace-wide `.cargo/config.toml` `[build] jobs` cap was raised 8→16 in the same pass. Pinned by `check-build-profile.sh` (profile exists with cheap settings, install.sh uses the split + cap + trace + sccache wiring, jobs = 16 in `.cargo/config.toml`) wired into `kask-invariants.yml`. **Release dependency codegen memory bound (absorbs retired D50; operator-authorized 2026-09-07, applied 2026-09-08):** `[profile.release.package]` carries `"*" = { codegen-units = 16 }` — external dependencies no longer inherit single-unit release codegen (concurrent builds and large cgu=1 jobs caused swap thrashing). Thin LTO and the explicit `zed` override unchanged; the wildcard applies to non-workspace dependencies, not workspace crates. Pinned by the TOML-parsed check in `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-corpus/tests/build_profile.rs` (moved out of the gate script's former `tomllib` heredoc by the zero-Python operator ruling 2026-10-13): observed RED without the override, GREEN with it. One build at a time remains required. **typos spell-check removed from the local gate (operator ruling 2026-09-10):** the `typos --config typos.toml` invocation in `script/clippy`'s local-only branch (upstream `d25da9728b`) is deleted, along with `typos.toml` and `typos-audit.toml` (orphan sweep). The gate never ran in CI — only locally — and it blocked the build gate on domain proper nouns (`DuPont`, the standard name for the ROE decomposition used throughout the companies tooling), taxing real work for allowlist curation with no CI enforcement behind it. The `cargo machete` and `buf` checks remain. On upstream merges, `typos.toml` shows delete/modify conflicts — resolve by re-deleting, same as the D7/D16 surfaces. **`--fast` + dev-profile route removal (2026-10-02):** the installer offers release-class builds only — `--fast` builds the zed binary on `[profile.release-fast]` (lto=false, codegen-units=16, debug=full; servers still on release-mcp via the shared `build_mcp_servers` helper) and installs from `target/release-fast`; the `--debug` dev-profile route is deleted (a day of dev-profile daily-drivers installed via `--debug` read as a performance regression — debug_assertions tax on every hot path, hang detector blind below 5s; two-symptom latency findings §7.9) and `HKASK_BUILD_TYPE` is validated to `release\|release-fast`. | D51 | Stop the managed MCP runtime on session quit | `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (`wire_kask_mcp_shutdown`) | Operator-authorized T19 (2026-09-07). The quit future drives `McpRuntime::shutdown_all` on the tokio handle before process exit, which otherwise skips Rust destructors. Lifecycle serialization is retained, with a terminal shutdown latch rejecting queued starts. A real-process RED exposed a second defect: rmcp's detached/graceful cleanup let a child survive after maps were cleared. `McpRuntime` now owns each child before handshake, gives rmcp only its pipes, and tracks kill-and-reap tasks that stop/shutdown await. The added duplicate shutdown definition was removed after the operator observed E0592. Fn-pointer pin: `kask_wiring_symbols_exist`; runtime regression: `shutdown_all_clears_every_reconnect_path`. Validated: 17 runtime unit + 14 real-process integration tests, including `shutdown_all_kills_live_and_pending_children` (connected child, blocked discovery, repeated shutdown, rejected late startup). Observed RED for the leaked child and late-start resurrection before the repairs. After the initial timeout, the full Zed check and scoped lint passed in the operator-approved incremental window. No live application-quit smoke test is claimed; that remains an operator checkpoint. |

Model classifiers emit raw candidate terms only; the shared canonicalizer preserves them and derives namespaces/URIs, stamped as `published-term-resolution-v2`; v1 or mismatched records require re-tagging, not a compatibility upgrade. Pinned by `term_resolution::tests` in the bridge, `downstream_readers_reject_stale_and_inconsistent_tags` in corpus, the corpus/hMem classifier contract tests, and the wrapper's resolution and tier tests. **Compile repair (2026-09-10, `47ffbee7e6`):** `thread.rs` imports the registered `OntoAnchorTool`; output text fields own their strings to satisfy `AgentTool::Output`'s lifetime-independent deserialization contract and retain dynamically formatted input errors. The ladder and JSON shape are unchanged. Regression pins: `output_round_trips_through_owned_json` (every then-supported rung through `serde_json::from_value`) and `invalid_input_returns_an_owned_error` (actual tool execution with malformed input, error text retained after deserialization). **Relation-traversal extension (2026-09-22, operator-directed):** `onto_anchor` retains its resolution-only JSON contract and accepts optional `relation_query` for bounded directed concept-neighbor/path queries. `hkask-bridge-ontology/src/ontology_graph.rs` builds a read-only graph from recorded derived constituents (only distinctly resolved concepts) and the verified schema.org inverse-property pair; the schema.org pinned release supplies the published declaration (the old `schema-org-relations.tsv` fixture is removed). The graph reads directly stated `has_parent` and `inverse_of` edges; the old `sumo-terms.txt`, `schema-org-terms.txt` and `schema-org-relations.tsv` fixtures are removed. | D55 | Deterministic test-profile load in the agent test harness `setup()` | `crates/agent/src/tests/mod.rs` (`setup` — `cx.run_until_parked()` after the `watch_settings` spawn) | `Thread::new` fixes `profile_id` from `AgentSettings.default_profile` at construction, and `setup()`'s settings load is async (`watch_settings` spawns a watcher task; the file content reaches the store only when the foreground executor polls it). When the thread is constructed before the watcher delivers the test profile, it is permanently on the default `write` profile, whose tool allowlist (`assets/settings/default.json`) omits every test-only tool — `enabled_tools` filters them all out (`is_tool_enabled` is must-be-listed: `tools.get(name) == Some(&true)`), and every tool call fails with "No tool named X exists". The race's resolution tracked the test prologue: the `init_test(cx)` + `always_allow_tools(cx)`-before-`setup` prologue lost it (every failing test had it), bare `setup` won it. Observed 2026-09-11 running the agent suite for the first time after the onto_anchor arc: five streaming/partial-input tests failed — `test_streaming_tool_completes_when_llm_stream_ends_without_final_input`, `test_non_streaming_tool_partial_input_then_retryable_error_flushes_canceled_message`, `test_streaming_tool_json_parse_error_is_forwarded_to_running_tool`, `test_streaming_tool_error_breaks_stream_loop_immediately` (upstream origin, f69ab1d04e), `test_streaming_tool_error_waits_for_prior_tools_to_complete` — while `test_non_streaming_tool_partial_input_then_max_tokens_gets_canceled_message` and both queued-message tests passed only because they never assert on tool results: they ran with a silently empty tool surface (probe-verified: `profile_id="write"`, `Request includes 0 tools`, "No tool named echo exists" tool results inside passing tests). **Fix:** `setup()` drives the watcher to completion before constructing the thread, so `profile_id` is deterministically `test-profile` and the test tools are always enabled. Pinned by the five tests above (all RED before, GREEN after) plus the previously-silent tests now exercising the real tool surface. |

Current mechanism: when no escalation sink is wired — a reachable production state, because the review board wires asynchronously at editor start (`Tokio::spawn` → `set_alert_escalation_sink`, `crates/zed/src/main.rs:2278-2286`), so every editor start runs sinkless until that spawn lands, and the regulation crate's sink is optional by design — an unchanged escalation disposition archived another `reg.variety.algedonic_alert` every tick. A process-local fallback-condition latch arms only after successful archive persistence (`alert_escalation_sink.is_none()` branch, `cycle.rs`), suppresses exact persistent conditions, clears as soon as the corresponding escalation disposition disappears, and therefore permits a later recurrence. The heap tie-break now reverses the end comparison so the shorter equal-start layer is emitted first; overlapping injections and the invariant remain intact. Should be reported upstream.
