The named production-shape regression ran 1 test, 1 passed; no current live panic was observed in available logs. Issue draft and limits: `kask/docs/research/d-seam-audit.md` D66; report upstream only after operator review. |
| D67 | Double-lease panic names the offending call site | `crates/gpui/src/app/entity_map.rs` + `crates/gpui/src/app.rs` + `crates/gpui/src/app/context.rs` + `crates/gpui/src/app/async_context.rs` + `crates/gpui/src/gpui.rs` | Operator-observed 2026-09-19: a live prompt send panicked with `cannot read agent::thread::Thread while it is already being updated` at `entity_map.rs:164` and a release-build backtrace of only `<unknown>` frames, so the re-entrant read could not be located. GPUI reported its own panic helper as the caller because `EntityMap::read`/`lease` raised through `unwrap_or_else(|| double_lease_panic::<T>(...))` — the closure breaks `#[track_caller]` propagation — and no entry point carried `#[track_caller]`. The read/update/lease entry points now propagate the caller location: `EntityMap::read`/`lease` call `double_lease_panic` directly (no closure) and are `#[track_caller]`; `Entity::{read,read_with,update,update_in}` and `WeakEntity::{read_with,update,update_in}` are `#[track_caller]`; the `AppContext::{read_entity,update_entity}` trait declarations and the production impls (`App`, `Context<T>`, `AsyncApp`, `AsyncWindowContext`) are `#[track_caller]`; and `double_lease_panic` embeds `std::panic::Location::caller()` in the message, so a release panic names the offending `file:line:col` even with no debug symbols. Message text keeps the `cannot <op> <Type> while it is already being updated` prefix (`should_panic` compatibility; no test pinned the old suffix). Test-only/bench contexts are not annotated — propagation simply stops at that impl frame, which only lowers diagnostic quality, never correctness. Pinned by `double_lease_panic_names_the_offending_call_site`, which asserts both the direct (`Entity::read`) and `AppContext` (`read_with`) paths report the test file, not the panic helper. |
| D68 | Tool-call calling actor captured at stream construction (double-lease fix) | `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/context_server_registry.rs` | Live incident 2026-09-19 (BH-001): a prompt send panicked with `cannot read agent::thread::Thread while it is already being updated` at `crates/agent/src/thread.rs:7103` — the exact site D67's self-locating panic was built to expose. `ToolCallEventStream::calling_actor` read the owning `Thread` through its `WeakEntity` handle on demand (`thread.read(cx)`), and its only production caller is the MCP tool proxy's `run` (`context_server_registry.rs`), which `Thread::run_tool` invokes synchronously — reached from `handle_completion_event` → `handle_tool_use_event` inside `this.update(cx, ...)`, so the Thread entity is leased and the handle read double-lease-panics. The actor is now captured once at stream construction: `ToolCallEventStream` carries a `calling_actor: Option<WebID>` field initialized from `native_thread_calling_actor(self.id())` at both production constructor sites (`run_tool`, `replay_tool_call` — `Thread::id()` reads the already-borrowed `&Thread`, no entity access); `calling_actor()` returns the cached value with no `App` and no entity read, so it is safe inside the tool-dispatch update. Provenance unchanged: host-derived from the owning thread's session id only (`WebID::for_agent_name`, `native-thread:{session_id}`), never from model-supplied tool arguments; `None` for streams not tied to a live thread (test constructors pass `None`), with callers keeping the documented process-level fallback. Pinned by `calling_actor_is_readable_from_inside_a_thread_update` (observed RED under the old on-demand read with the exact live signature — `cannot read agent::thread::Thread while it is already being updated (at crates/agent/src/thread.rs:7125:33)` — GREEN under the cached design; pins the class, not the instance: any future re-introduction of an entity read on this path fails there) plus the adapted `test_calling_actor_is_distinct_per_thread_and_deterministic` (distinct per thread, deterministic across calls, `None` for un-tied streams). Verification: `./script/clippy -p agent` clean; full agent suite 914 passed / 0 failed / 11 ignored. |
| D69 | `openai_compatible` LLM-provider pre-seeding (DeepInfra, KiloCode) | `assets/settings/default.json` (`language_models.openai_compatible`) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/inference_providers.rs` (`INFERENCE_PROVIDERS` + tests) + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page.rs` (nudge-guard test) + `kask/crates/hkask-inference/src/hkask_inference.rs` (`DIRECT_EMBEDDING_PROVIDERS` comment) | The shipped `default.json` pre-seeds `language_models.openai_compatible` entries so providers appear in Settings → AI → LLM Providers without user edits (upstream ships the map empty): `DeepInfra` (`https://api.deepinfra.com/v1/openai` — pre-existing, seam-recorded here; the embedding default's provider until `55a366a30c` moved the default to `ollama/qwen3-embedding:0.6b`; moved again to `OpenRouter/qwen/qwen3-embedding-8b` on 2026-09-28) and `KiloCode` (`https://api.kilo.ai/api/gateway` — Kilo Code's OpenAI-compatible chat gateway), each with `available_models: []` + `auto_discover: true` (discovery via `GET {api_url}/models` — unauthenticated on Kilo). KiloCode is also in `INFERENCE_PROVIDERS` (`credential_key: "kilocode"`, `env_var: "KILOCODE_API_KEY"`): one key, one location — its key lives at the `api_url` keychain slot (D5); the active editor credential path reads the keychain at the provider `api_url` (D5). The old D12 Kask-specific env-name normalization was retired; the `KILOCODE_API_KEY` descriptor does not inject a key into MCP children. `credential_urls_for_mcp` does not include `KILOCODE_API_KEY`: no current MCP server consumes it, so rotating the native provider key does not restart child processes. The native provider still reads the same `api_url` keychain slot. No future-only MCP credential fan-out or compatibility path is retained. `resolve_embedding_credentials` resolves only `serves_embeddings` providers (2026-09-27, implementing the D69 audit's advisory): the descriptor table carries an explicit embeddings-endpoint flag, so chat-only KiloCode and endpoint-discovery RunPod (its base `api_url` is the account API, not an OpenAI-compatible root; each serverless endpoint has its own `https://api.runpod.io/v2/{endpoint_id}/openai/v1`) are rejected in `embedding_provider_descriptor` before credential resolution — the startup "semantic recall enabled" announcement can no longer fire for a route that would fail on every call (`LanguageModelEmbeddingPort` POSTs to `{api_url}/embeddings`). No default change in that pass (the embedding default was `ollama/qwen3-embedding:0.6b` at the time; it moved to `OpenRouter/qwen/qwen3-embedding-8b` on 2026-09-28); KiloCode also has NO `DIRECT_EMBEDDING_PROVIDERS` row in hkask-inference: the gateway is chat-only (no embeddings endpoint, no embedding models in its catalog; RunPod is the precedent for an `INFERENCE_PROVIDERS` entry deliberately absent from that table). Kilo model ids are nested (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5`, `kilo-auto/efficient`); `resolve_model_names` splits on the first `/`, so `KiloCode/anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5` overrides resolve by exact id (the OpenRouter nested-slug pin covers the class). Gateway specifics recorded for operators: upstream payment failures surface as 503 (not 402) — billing exhaustion masquerades as "service unavailable"; tool-call repair (dedup, orphan cleanup, ID normalization) is gateway-side. Pinned by `inference_provider_keys_resolve_to_the_provider_slot`, `credential_urls_for_mcp_injects_only_provider_keys_with_child_consumers` (OpenRouter/DeepInfra child consumers only; native KiloCode/RunPod absent), `nudge_guard_covers_provider_api_urls_and_excludes_legacy_slots` (native-only slots do not restart children), `embedding_eligibility_rejects_providers_without_an_embeddings_endpoint`, and `embedding_credentials_reject_ineligible_routes_with_keys_present`. |
| D70 | Opt-in native on-demand tool trace | `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` + `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tool_trace.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view/thread_view.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs` (UI submission pin) | The native `/trace <prompt>` command starts a private, single-turn capture before sending the stripped prompt; the completed turn finishes the capture and exports it under `agent-traces/` in the visible artifacts directory. The trace stays off for ordinary turns. Compile repair (2026-09-23): `Entity::update` returns its closure output directly, so the prompt closure returns `Result<Receiver>` without unwrapping it, and trace finalization returns `Option<ToolTrace>` which `.context(...)` converts into an error when absent. Pinned by `trace_prompt_starts_capture_and_sends_only_the_prompt` (native routing), `traced_turn_exports_final_answer_and_normal_turn_does_not_export` (actual `onto_anchor` two-hop call, real exported input/output/timing and final answer, and no export on the following ordinary turn), `captured_tool_result_and_missing_result_are_distinct` (pending versus completed output), and `private_export_round_trips_and_refuses_a_permissive_directory` (parseable 0600 export in a 0700 directory, no overwrite, fail-closed on permissive directories), and `oversize_export_fails_without_leaving_a_partial_file` (8 MiB guard; rejects rather than truncating trace evidence). Test-only trace-directory override is thread-local, leaving the production artifact path unchanged. **UI argument preservation (2026-10-20):** the idle-send path splits a native command from trailing text only when its `AvailableCommand.input` is absent. Input-taking native commands such as `/trace` receive the complete prompt in one submission; `/compact` still runs alone and queues its remainder. The former category-only test sent bare `/trace` and queued its required prompt, causing `/trace requires a prompt or attachment` before model execution. Pinned by `test_native_command_submission_respects_declared_input` (captured first submission through `ThreadView::send`, inline/newline/bare input, another input-taking native command, and the compact-with-follow-up control; observed red before the fix). Capture does not supply a global evaluation rubric. |

| D71 | Sidebar observes draft editors only for draft threads | `crates/sidebar/src/sidebar.rs` + `crates/sidebar/src/sidebar_tests.rs` | The sidebar's `refresh_draft_editor_observations` rebuilt subscriptions for every open conversation on every entries refresh, including promoted threads whose titles come from metadata rather than the message editor. It now subscribes to editor edits only when the thread's existing metadata identifies it as a draft; it retains each conversation's `StateChange` subscription so a Loading → Connected draft can still acquire its editor observation. This removes one unnecessary foreground event subscription per loaded non-draft without changing status/title updates or adding mirrored sidebar state. Pinned by `test_non_draft_editor_does_not_subscribe_for_draft_title_updates` (fails before the change, passes after) and `test_draft_title_updates_from_editor_text` (draft title preservation). This bounded cleanup does not establish the cause of the live ~one-core foreground load. |
| D72 | Retired edit-tool eval scenarios and unused git-log CLI wrapper | `crates/agent/src/tools/evals/edit_file.rs` + four `fixtures/` subdirectories + `crates/edit_prediction_context/src/git_log_context.rs` | The optional `unit-eval` suite previously replayed historical `git blame` code (including an instruction to delete `run_git_blame` without its live callers), a removed `compile_parser_to_wasm` implementation, and a Python Zode project whose 50-iteration scoring checked only leading whitespace/backticks rather than the requested application. This row is the rebase obligation, not a claim that a deleted function still runs. Retire only if upstream removes the same material or the operator changes the evaluation policy. **2026-10-27 retirement attempted and REVERSED (operator direction, same day):** the minimization audit recommended retiring this deletion (upstream's fixtures returned untouched = zero merge friction, no kask impact — the evals are opt-in `unit-eval` the fork never runs). The restoration was executed, then reversed within the day at the operator's explicit direction: seeing the deleted material return (including the `disable_cursor_blinking` fixtures — inert eval input data, NOT the retired D15 code, which never left its upstream-identical state) outweighed the marginal merge-friction saving. The deletion stands; this row remains the live rebase obligation; the original misleading-evaluation rationale stands. |

| D74 | Approved saved-file Lean check in native chat | `crates/agent/src/tools/lean_check_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/{tools.rs,thread.rs}` + `crates/agent/Cargo.toml` + `assets/settings/default.json` + `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/tool_permissions_setup.rs` | `lean_check` is a built-in, restricted-mode-disabled tool. It rejects non-local, non-saved, private/excluded, traversal and any symlink-aliased paths; it always prompts before invoking the project's pinned Lake environment. The Lake launcher is resolved from `ELAN_HOME/bin/lake` or `$HOME/.elan/bin/lake` before PATH, so GUI launches find the durable version manager installed by the supported source/binary installers (`kask/scripts/build/install-common.sh`; pinned Elan release digest + Lean v4.34.0; plain cargo build remains offline). Argument-based `lake env lean --json` returns structured diagnostics and a Lean version with bounded runtime/output; on Unix the Lake/Lean process group is killed on timeout, output overflow or cancellation; optional validated `#print axioms` audits one named theorem from the saved source in project context. Successful exit without an audit is `checked_not_axiom_audited`, never axiom-free; sorry/native-axiom dependencies are surfaced separately. The write profile enables it, the ask profile disables it. The UI excludes it from configurable permission rules because approval is mandatory for every call. No interactive tactic streaming. Tests and verification evidence belong to this change's closeout; local execution remains capable of running arbitrary project code after approval. |

| D73 | Defer offscreen historical entry content | `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view/thread_view.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view/thread_search_bar.rs` + `crates/agent_ui/src/entry_view_state.rs` | On opening saved root-thread history, `ConversationView` previously called `EntryViewState::sync_entry` for every entry before displaying the list, constructing terminal and diff editor entities for tool calls even when offscreen. Root history now creates a cheap `ToolCallEntry` shell with its focus handle for every old tool row; the list processor materializes terminal and diff views only as that row renders. `EntryUpdated` leaves untouched offscreen shells deferred, while materialized entries, newly appended entries, and subagent preview entries retain eager update behavior. Saved user-message editor entities retain their focus handles, but defer `set_message` until the row is rendered. A thread-search query hydrates any remaining user editors before scanning, shifting that cost from opening to first search while keeping all saved messages searchable; newly appended messages and subagent previews remain eager. Pinned by `entry_view_state::tests::test_historical_user_messages_hydrate_at_entry_creation` (deferred content, stable editor identity, view/search hydration), `entry_view_state::tests::test_diff_sync` (focusable shell with no offscreen diff editor; offscreen update remains deferred; diff editor and content appear when materialized) and `conversation_view::tests::test_thread_search_includes_expanded_tool_call_content` (new tool content remains searchable). The visible opening-time benefit has not been measured on a deployed build. **2026-10-12 merge:** re-homed onto upstream's `synced_source_version` entry sync; `materialize_user_messages` deleted with upstream's new entry pipeline; the pin was renamed `test_historical_user_messages_hydrate_at_entry_creation` with the re-home. |

| D77 | Repository agent instructions | `.rules` + `AGENTS.md` | Upstream ships `.rules` with its own content and `AGENTS.md` as a symlink to it (`099226e50c`). zed-kask replaces `.rules` with the fork's trap list (auto-loaded: `.rules` wins in `RULES_FILE_NAMES`, `crates/prompt_store/src/prompts.rs`) and made `AGENTS.md` a regular file (`71920e5aa2`, 2026-07-23): a manual-read orientation guide that is not auto-loaded. | D78 | Failed multi-edit `edit_file` calls change nothing | `crates/agent/src/tools/edit_session.rs` (`run_session` failure arm, `EditSession::restore_original_text`) + `crates/agent/src/tools/edit_file_tool.rs` tests | Upstream streams each resolved edit into the buffer and, when a later edit fails (no match or multiple matches), saves the buffer with the earlier edits applied while the tool reports an error — the agent then believes nothing changed, or retries and double-applies. The fork restores the text the session opened with before saving, so a failed call leaves the file byte-identical, and the error states "No edits were applied; the file is unchanged." Successful calls are untouched. Motivated by 22 recorded curator incidents (2026-09-05..26) of half-applied multi-edits. Pinned by `test_streaming_edit_failure_leaves_file_unchanged` and the adapted upstream `test_streaming_edit_failure_mid_stream` (which asserted upstream's partial-apply behavior). **2026-10-12 merge:** the rollback condition is refined from the buffer diff to the pipeline's own edits-applied record (`EditPipeline::edits_applied`, `WritePipeline::content_written`): a buffer changed externally mid-call is not a tool edit — it is never reverted, and an external change alone never claims a rollback happened. Pinned additionally by upstream's `test_streaming_edit_detects_new_ambiguous_match` (external mid-call change + ambiguous match → bare error, external change preserved). |
| D79 | Curator status separates new alerts from the board worklist | `crates/agent/src/tools/curator_tools.rs` + `crates/agent/src/curator_agent_server.rs` + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/metacognition_bridge.rs` | The current-cycle new-alert count is distinct from the number of Algedonic review board cards not Done (including proposal cards). The latter is read on demand through the governed panel invoker, not a queue or a cached alert count; an unreadable board reports unknown, never 0. The earlier queue-backed D79 implementation landed in `fe2c18b99f` and was replaced by the Phase D board migration. Pinned by `status_labels_cycle_alerts_and_review_backlog_separately` and `status_counts_all_non_done_cards_including_proposals`. **Truthful `unobserved` label + cycle evidence (2026-10-08):** `status_line` mapped the Fermi `LoopView` `unobserved` composite to "partial (loop or memory health not monitored)" — a false statement in every ordinary session: `unobserved` implies liveness `Live` (`compute_reading` returns `WiringClosed` for `NeverRun` first), and the loop IS monitored (heartbeat, cycle count, deviations recorded in `reg.outcome.loop_quality`); what is absent is verified impact — the only production submitter of before/after rollout checks is the harness regression monitor (`rollout_event_bridge.rs`), which fires on harness pass-rate regressions only, so a homeostatic session records `(0,0,0)` verdicts every tick and `acceptance_rate()` is honestly `None` (observed live: 720 cycles at zero deviations read as "not monitored"). The label now reads "partial (loop turning; impact unverified)", with "not monitored" reserved for genuinely absent readings (`loop_reading.is_none()` / memory probe unwired). `CuratorStatusOutput` gains `loop_cycles` (read from the snapshot's `regulation_health.total_cycles`), rendered as "Loop Cycles", so the operator sees the loop turning behind the verdict. Pinned by `status_line_labels_unobserved_as_turning_not_unmonitored` (observed RED on the pre-fix tree) and `status_output_carries_the_loops_cycle_count`; the Goodhart pin `status_line_never_calls_a_broken_or_unobserved_loop_healthy` is unchanged. **Native-first readout (2026-10-23, the instrument-affordance spec D's operator-facing leg):** `curator_status` and the session-start state block render the native-first-rate sensor's rolling reading end-to-end — `RegulationLedger::native_first_rate` → `HealthSnapshot.native_first_rate` (`metacognition.rs` tick, captured before the ledger guard drops) → `BridgeMetacognitionProvider::health_snapshot_json` keys `native_first_rate`/`native_first_samples` → `CuratorStatusOutput` ("Native First Rate: 62% of 26 substantive turns") and `format_state_block` ("- Native first rate: …"). An empty window renders `not available`/`unavailable`, never a fabricated "0%" (the `unwrap_or(0)` trap). Pinned by `snapshot_carries_the_native_first_drift_reading` (metacognition tests), `status_output_carries_native_first_rate_and_samples` + `status_renders_missing_native_first_as_not_available_never_zero` (curator_tools status_snapshot_tests), and the two state-block pins. |
| D76 | Model discovery lifecycle and failure diagnostics | `crates/language_models/src/provider/open_ai_compatible.rs` + `crates/language_models/src/provider/runpod.rs` | The kask-added discovery (`DiscoveryState`, from `bd2799171c`; RunPod mirrors it) restarted its fetch whenever the shared provider state notified, and on success notified that same state so the `LanguageModelRegistry` would re-read `provided_models`. Each success therefore started the next fetch: a continuous `/v1/models` polling loop for every authenticated auto-discover provider (DeepInfra, KiloCode, RunPod). Measured live 2026-09-24 in one process: about 12 discovery completions per 5 s, about 1,000 model-picker notifies per 5 s, and a foreground thread pinned at about 100% of one core while idle. **Fix:** discovery success notifies only its own entity, and each provider overrides `LanguageModelProviderState::subscribe` to observe both the shared state and the discovery entity, so the registry still sees discovered models without discovery observing itself. No backward-compatibility shim. Failure warnings in the same fork-added fetch path use anyhow's `{e:#}` source-chain display (formerly D48, which was not an upstream warning fix); the RunPod-side counterpart remains under D29. Pinned by `successful_discovery_does_not_restart_itself` in `open_ai_compatible.rs` (exactly one `/models` request after storing a key and settling; the registry subscription fires on discovery completion without another request) and `discovery_failure_warn_includes_error_source_chain` (a split source needle that cannot be satisfied by its own test literal). |
| D75 | Prospective expectation checkpoint in the shared agent prompt | `crates/agent/src/templates/system_prompt.hbs` + `crates/agent/src/templates.rs` | The `gradient-hunter` skill body was read only on invocation; in a read-only ordinary code inquiry an independently dispatched agent produced a source-grounded answer without a visible prospective expectation. The base prompt, rendered for every agent thread, now asks for one goal-relevant, falsifiable expected observation before substantive inquiry (or an honest `unknown` followed by a scoping probe), then uses `gradient-hunter` for consequential mismatches. Agreement, operator-declared irrelevance, and trivial mechanical work do not open a hunt. Pinned by `test_system_prompt_establishes_expectation_before_substantive_inquiry` over the rendered prompt, including placement before Tool Use; observed RED without the instruction. Prompt presence is not evidence that agent behavior improved: an independent post-rebuild task is still required. |
| D80 | remote_connection test-support pairing for eval_cli's normal graph | `crates/eval_cli/Cargo.toml` | The structural trap: `remote`'s `test-support` compiles `RemoteConnectionOptions::Mock` into the enum (`crates/remote/src/remote_client.rs`, `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]`), while the match arm for it in `remote_connection` is gated on `remote_connection`'s OWN `test-support` (`crates/remote_connection/src/remote_connection.rs:256`). An invocation where a dev closure unifies `remote/test-support` on while `remote_connection` is built from a default-features normal edge fails E0004 (non-exhaustive patterns). In this fork that fires for scoped all-features/all-targets selections containing both `remote_server` (dev-deps `workspace`/`project` with `test-support` unify `remote/test-support` on) and `eval_cli` (its normal `agent_ui` edge builds `remote_connection` default-only) — e.g. `./script/clippy -p remote_server -p eval_cli -p edit_prediction_cli -p xtask`. **Fix (2026-09-28, operator option 1):** a feature-propagation-only NORMAL dep `remote_connection = { workspace = true, features = ["test-support"] }` in eval_cli — not `use`d in source; a dev-dep request does not reach the normal-dependency unit (the swarm_panel precedent), so the request must ride a normal dep. Existing pairings for the same trap: `agent`/`agent_ui` (dev-dep), `git_ui`/`recent_projects` (test-support feature forwarding), `swarm_panel` (normal dep), `kask_bridge` (dev-dep, 2026-10-22: its dev closure requests `agent`/`acp_thread`/`workspace` test-support, which forwards `remote/test-support` on for the shared graph — the paired `remote_connection = { workspace = true, features = ["test-support"] }` dev-dep keeps the arm visible in multi-crate `cargo test` selections like `-p hkask-mcp-swarm -p hkask-mcp-kata-kanban -p swarm_panel -p kask_bridge`, observed failing E0004 in remote_connection before the pairing; cargo-machete-ignored per the swarm_panel precedent). Upstream carries the same variant/arm gate split, so the class is upstream-reportable; this row protects the eval_cli instance. Pinned red-green by the originally-failing invocation: `HKASK_BUILD_JOBS=2 ./script/clippy --offline --locked -p remote_server -p eval_cli -p edit_prediction_cli -p xtask` — E0004 observed before the pairing, clean after; `cargo check -p remote_server --all-targets --all-features` green before and after (single-package selections never build the mismatched pair). **Extension (2026-10-22, workspace unification):** the upstream merge `8e32d2a127` added more consumer-side Mock-gated arms (`From<RemoteConnectionOptions>` in `project/src/trusted_worktrees.rs`; `RemoteConnectionIdentity` in `workspace/src/persistence.rs`; the project-title match in `title_bar/src/title_bar.rs`; `recent_projects`' connection/servers matches), and once eval_cli's D80 pairing turns `remote/test-support` on for its normal graph, `cargo check --workspace` builds those consumers' units default-featured — variant visible, arms hidden, E0004 at each in turn (observed live: project → workspace → title_bar, each failing in sequence). Closed with two more propagation-only normal deps in eval_cli: `recent_projects = { workspace = true, features = ["test-support"] }` — upstream's own forwarding hub (`test-support = ["remote/…", "remote_connection/…", "project/…", "workspace/…"]` in upstream's `recent_projects/Cargo.toml`), one request pairing four consumers — plus `title_bar = { workspace = true, features = ["test-support"] }` (not covered by the hub). The consumer census is exhaustive (`grep -rn 'RemoteConnection\(Options\|Identity\)::Mock' crates/*/src/*.rs`): `remote` itself (its own unit carries the feature), `remote_connection`, `project`, `workspace`, `recent_projects`, `title_bar`, plus test-gated uses only (agent_ui/sidebar/workspace test modules). Pinned red-green: `cargo check --workspace` — E0004 at `project`→`workspace`→`title_bar` observed before each pairing, `Finished` clean after; the D80 clippy invocation above remains green. |
| D81 | Wrong-root path-pattern warning in `grep` and `find_path` | `crates/agent/src/tools.rs` (`orphaned_path_pattern_note`) + `crates/agent/src/tools/grep_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/find_path_tool.rs` | Both tools match path patterns against full paths that start with a project root directory, so a pattern whose first literal segment names no root (`kask/docs/**` in a `zed-kask`-rooted project) can only match zero files — and the resulting "No matches found" is byte-identical to a genuine empty search. Observed live 3× 2026-09-27/28 (a docs grep, a call-site grep, a registry-template glob), each read as absence while the files existed; one produced a false "zero matches anywhere" claim. The fork appends a warning when the provable orphan condition holds (first segment a literal naming no project root; wildcard-leading patterns are left to glob semantics, and correctly rooted patterns that find nothing stay clean — the warning never fires on correct output): `grep` computes it from the worktree root names beside the include matcher; `find_path` corroborates the exact zero-match count. The `.rules` agent-discipline entry (patterns must start with `**/` or the root name) prevents the wasted call; this seam makes the failure visible when it happens anyway. Pinned by `orphaned_path_pattern_tests` (5 unit cases in tools.rs), `test_grep_tool_orphaned_include_pattern_warns` (fires with the corrected form; a rooted-but-empty search stays exactly "No matches found"), and `test_find_path_tool_orphaned_glob_warns` (same two directions through the model-visible From impl). |
| D82 | Diff point mapping clips patch-translated columns | `crates/buffer_diff/src/buffer_diff.rs` (`base_text_range_for_buffer_range`, `patch_for_buffer_range` start/end clamps, the four `buffer_point_to_base_text_*` / `base_text_point_to_buffer_*` mappers) + tests in the same file | Upstream's `Patch::compose` / `old_to_new` / `edit_for_old_position` translate points by row delta while preserving the source column, so a point valid in one snapshot's layout (a 35-char table row) can overshoot the row it lands on in the other (a blank line). `BufferDiffSnapshot` consumed those translated points unclipped: `base_text_range_for_buffer_range` clamped only the rope end (`.min(max_point())`), `patch_for_buffer_range` fed `old_to_new` results straight into `anchor_before` (`ToOffset` never clips rows), and the point mappers returned raw `edit.new` and converted caller points without clipping. Debug builds panicked at `crates/rope/src/chunk.rs:429` (`point Point(1:35) extends beyond row`, observed 2026-09-28 02:54:57 while staging SKILL.md hunks right after commit `0626d634`; the panicked task died and the stage click silently failed); release builds silently resolve to the row end — a wrong staging footprint. The fork clips every patch-translated point to its target snapshot's row layout (`clip_point`) at each consumption site — mapper inputs and outputs, the anchor lookups, and the stage footprint — so debug builds match the release row-end fallback instead of panicking and both modes are explicit. Pinned by `test_point_mappers_clip_overshooting_columns` (deterministic overshoot column; red on the unfixed tree at the observed panic site via `base_text_point_to_buffer_point` → `patch_for_base_text_range` → `point_to_offset`) and `test_random_staging_point_mapping_stays_within_rows` (500 seeded layouts asserting mapped points stay within their rows through the staging entry points). Upstream carries the same defect; upstream-reportable. |
| D83 | Requests carry the model's discovered/configured output cap (`max_tokens`), never a fabricated client-side default | `crates/open_router/src/open_router.rs` (`Model.max_output_tokens`, `ModelEntry.max_completion_tokens`/`top_provider`, `ModelTopProvider`, `model_from_entry`) + `crates/language_models/src/provider/open_router.rs` (`provided_models` settings literal) + the 2026-09-28 same-class sweep: `crates/anthropic/src/anthropic.rs` (`Model.max_output_tokens: Option<u64>`, `from_listed`) + `crates/anthropic/src/completion.rs` (`UNCONFIGURED_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, `into_anthropic`) + `crates/bedrock/src/models.rs` (Converse + Mantle accessors) + `crates/bedrock/src/bedrock.rs` (`Request.max_tokens: Option<u64>`, `set_max_tokens`) + `crates/language_models/src/provider/{anthropic,anthropic_compatible,bedrock,opencode}.rs` + `crates/copilot_chat/src/model.rs` + `crates/language_models_cloud/src/language_models_cloud.rs` | Upstream's `open_router::Model::max_output_tokens()` is a hardcoded `None` stub and `ModelEntry` parses no output-limit field, so `into_open_router` → `effective_max_output_tokens(None)` → the request JSON omits `max_tokens` entirely — OpenRouter then applies its own default output limit, and GLM 5.3 at `effort: max` with thinking hits it mid-tool-call: the stream dies with the tool_use input still open (four `ToolInput::recv` warns 2026-09-28 08:36–08:50, zero `push_truncation` classifications — abrupt stream end, not a finish-chunk fragment; the 2026-09-27 storm was the same missing cap surfacing as EOF-classified fragments through finish chunks). D36 made the cut safe (typed error, nothing dispatched) but not absent; this seam removes the cause. The discovery mirrors the open_ai crate's `resolve_provider_fallbacks` fix for the same disease class (its own comment names the `ToolInput::recv` warn path): promote top-level `max_completion_tokens`, else OpenRouter's `top_provider.max_completion_tokens` nesting; cap the promoted budget at half the discovered context window (or half the existing 2M fallback) so oversized catalog caps cannot consume the whole request window. The guard restores the earlier half-context invariant from `c95999cdcd`, lost when D13 was retired and not reinstated by D83; the 2026-09-28 GLM 5.3 rejection requested 943,718 completion tokens alongside 585,195 input tokens against a 1,310,720-token endpoint. This fixed reservation does not guarantee fit if actual input exceeds its half-window allowance; compaction still has to enforce the input capacity. `None` only when the catalog reports no limit, and the wire then omits the field exactly as before. Settings-configured models carry no discovered cap (unchanged). A genuine limit hit now surfaces as `StopReason::MaxTokens` (D25/D43), visible and named, instead of a silent mid-tool-call cut. **Same-class sweep (2026-09-28, same operator ruling):** every remaining fabricated output cap is removed — the bedrock `unwrap_or(4_096)` fallbacks (Converse + Mantle accessors, now `Option<u64>` passthrough), the anthropic-protocol mappings (`provider/anthropic.rs` + `anthropic_compatible.rs`), and opencode's `unwrap_or(8192)`. Bedrock Converse omits `maxTokens` when unconfigured (`set_max_tokens(None)`) so AWS's own model default binds — the same shape as the OpenRouter seam; Mantle passes the `Option` through to `into_open_ai`/`into_open_ai_response`. The Anthropic Messages API makes `max_tokens` a mandatory wire parameter, so `anthropic::Model.max_output_tokens` is truthful `Option<u64>` metadata and `into_anthropic` resolves the unconfigured case with the single named documented fallback `UNCONFIGURED_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (65_536 — the largest documented Anthropic-class output cap: a provider accepts, clamps, or rejects visibly — never a silent small cut); copilot keeps its visible no-limit error and cloud wraps its catalog value in `Some`. Pinned by `discovered_output_cap_parses_and_promotes` (open_router crate: top-level, nested promotion, half-context cap, absent → None), `into_open_router_sends_the_discovered_output_cap` (provider: serialized wire carries `"max_tokens":131072`; no cap → field omitted), and `discovered_oversized_cap_is_bounded_on_the_wire` (oversized catalog cap serializes as the guarded value); the 2026-09-28 sweep pins `unconfigured_output_cap_falls_back_to_the_documented_wire_value` (anthropic crate: fallback value, request∩model intersection, the mandatory parameter still serialized), `unset_max_output_tokens_is_not_fabricated` (both anthropic-protocol provider mappings), `test_custom_max_output_tokens_is_not_fabricated` (bedrock Converse + Mantle), and `into_bedrock_without_any_configured_cap_omits_max_tokens` (Converse omits `maxTokens` when unconfigured; caps intersect); the D36 guard's dispatch-safety invariant is additionally pinned by the machine-checked spec model `kask/lean/truncation_guard_d83.lean` (Lean 4.34.0 via `kask/lean/lean-toolchain`, axioms `[propext, Quot.sound]`, paired `negative_control.lean` failing as designed). Upstream carries the same hardcoded `None`; upstream-reportable. **2026-10-12 merge:** the bedrock effort enum rename `BedrockAdaptiveReasoningEffort` carried (5 refs), `into_bedrock` takes `Option` args, and bedrock `models.rs` tests migrated to truthful `Option<u64>` caps (`Some(128_000)`, `ConverseModel::Custom` with `supports_tool_use`/`supports_images`/`thinking: None`). |
| D84 | Idle agent-thread retention raised to 16; per-window frame-health observability (looping-animation redraw caps RETIRED 2026-10-02) | `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (`agent_ui::MaxIdleRetainedThreads(16)` global after `agent_ui::init`) + `crates/gpui/src/profiler.rs` (`record_draw_duration`/`take_draw_stats`, feature-gated) + `crates/zed/src/reliability.rs` (the `ui frame health` lines) + `crates/gpui/src/window.rs` (the per-window reliability-monitor feed) | **Retention half (2026-09-28):** upstream's `MaxIdleRetainedThreads` defaults to 5: switching to an idle thread past the cap re-loads it from the DB and replays every entry through the live event path on the foreground (P7c: 635 ms load for a 963-entry thread), which the user experiences as a freeze on open/switch. The fork sets the GPUI global to 16 at startup — upstream's designed override point, no upstream file edited for this half; running threads are retained regardless, the cap only bounds idle eviction. Pinned by `kask_idle_thread_retention_override_is_set_after_agent_ui_init` (zed: source-order pin that the global is set after `agent_ui::init`). **Frame-health observability (2026-09-29):** `gpui::profiler::{record_draw_duration, take_draw_stats}` (feature-gated, recorded from `Window::draw`'s existing profiler block, keyed by `WindowId`) feed a per-window `ui frame health: w<id> draws=N avg_ms=A max_ms=M` line into the zed reliability monitor's existing 30 s log cadence (`crates/zed/src/reliability.rs`), with take semantics so each report covers exactly one interval. Per-window because the operator runs several agent windows concurrently and process-wide aggregates cannot attribute cost to a window. `draws ~ 0` while idle is the idle floor; high draws with low max is rate-driven lag; low draws with high max is cost-driven lag. Pinned by `draw_stats_accumulate_per_window_and_reset_on_take` (gpui). A standing observability surface, not a temporary probe: the next UI-lag investigation starts from logged data. **Redraw caps RETIRED (2026-10-02, `3f6f844703` — the redraw-cluster A/B):** the `DEFAULT_REPEAT_MAX_FPS` 20 fps default on `repeat()`/`repeat_synced()`, `Animation::uncapped()`, and the shared `gpui::frame_grid` facility (with D14's grid half) are deleted; `crates/gpui/src/elements/animation.rs` is restored byte-identical to the upstream sync base. The A/B measured the cluster as the dominant per-draw cost: with the cluster, draws averaged 22-35 ms early-session and 54-115 ms by ~1 h of session age (accumulation, S2-H1); with the cluster reverted (one restart, same active-streaming workload class), draws averaged 5.9-8.3 ms at high draw rates (2200-2800 draws/30 s) — the pre-cluster P7c band (2.3-7.5 ms) restored. The uncapped upstream redraw rate returns with the retirement (upstream carries the same uncapped loops; upstream-reportable); the age trajectory on the reverted build answered 2026-10-02 (61-minute read): no accumulation — the band read 3.1-12.4 ms avg across minutes 0-61 (3-4 ms at minute 54, where the clustered build read 114.5), tracking workload (draws 1600-4300/30 s), not age. |
| D86 | Experimentation MCP server — the experiment registry for the sharded experimentation program | `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-experimentation/` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/mcp_servers.rs` (registration) + root `Cargo.toml` (workspace member + dep) + `kask/scripts/build/mcp-servers.txt` (install package) + `kask/docs/reference/mcp-servers/README.md` (registry) + `kask/docs/research/experimentation-reference-model.md` (design authority: pattern, protocol, operator rulings — promoted from the repair plan §P8, 2026-10-05) + `kask/docs/plans/hkask-core-mcp-repair-improvement-plan.md` §P8 (server schemas, program and execution records) | The experimentation server joined as the 13th managed MCP server in 2026 and is one of the 10 remaining servers. Eight tools over one SQLCipher registry: `experiment_propose` (hypothesis, artifact layer — six layers incl. `scientific_hypothesis`, the general lab notebook, operator ruling D2 2026-10-15 — genotype refs, eval set, fitness fn, noise band, typed rubric bundle (P1: ≥1 metric + ≥1 implementation/grounded_report rubric, stable snake_case ids, verification in the swarm evaluator vocabulary — contains/not_contains/regex/report_ref), pre-registered prediction with confidence in [0,1], energy budget), `variant_register` (genotype config, lineage parent, role (P4: baseline|challenger|ablation — exactly one baseline per experiment; ablations name their parent)), `experiment_seal` (P2: SHA-256 fingerprint over (eval_set, rubrics, fitness_fn, noise_band) + headroom attestation (baseline variant, fitness evidence, measured pass rate < 1.0 — saturation is the typed no-headroom void, §5 Q6); UNIQUE(experiment_id) replay convergence — identical retries return the existing seal, different attempts are typed AlreadySealed refusals; pre-seal only the baseline records fitness, post-seal every record cites seal_id), `fitness_record` (recorded report refs only — never simulated; immutable; run_count summed against budget.max_runs), `final_evaluation_record` (P3: the leading variant re-scored on a run-ref-disjoint selection set — overlaps are typed refusals naming them; the run count joins the budget atomically), `selection_record` (selected/rejected fossils, both retained; the first selection resolves the experiment — further selection is failed_precondition), `lineage_read` (cycle-guarded ancestry; the experiment kind also reads the seal and final evaluations), `population_query` (layer/status/window filters). Replay convergence: the identity-minting inserts take optional caller keys whose UNIQUE index + INSERT OR IGNORE converge a retried interrupted call onto the existing record (the kata-kanban idempotency shape, D3). DB: `mcp/experimentation/experimentation.db` under the D28 layout (`HKASK_EXPERIMENTATION_DB` overrides), opened through the canonical `resolve_db_passphrase` chain — a missing `HKASK_DB_PASSPHRASE` fails startup as `Infrastructure`, never an empty-key open. Registration: credentials `["HKASK_DB_PASSPHRASE"]`, config_env `["HKASK_DATA_DIR", "HKASK_EXPERIMENTATION_DB"]` — aligned with the crate's actual env reads. No backward compatibility (§P8.7-Q5): the registry is the only record path from day one, no legacy-import surface; the GEPA per-run log retention and the per-skill experiment record formats are replaced by it in the same program (§P8.5 Phase 1 routing edits). Pinned by the crate's unit and tool-behavior suites (lifecycle propose→running→seal→fitness→final-evaluation→selection→resolved-refuses-work; experiment/variant key convergence; cross-experiment parent rejection; verdict validation; rubric, role, seal, disjointness, and budget refusals; seal fingerprint determinism; ancestry walk; population filters), `tool_names_match_live_router` + `tool_surface_is_exactly_8_registered_tools` (build.rs-generated TOOL_NAMES vs the live router), and `experimentation_registration_entry_is_pinned` (kask_bridge: id/binary/credentials/config_env). Launch-verified over stdio 2026-09-30: initialize + tools/list advertises all six tools. |
| D87 | Experimentation health source — the registry's Layer-A afferent pathway into the cybernetics loop (§P8.9 steps 1–2, 5) | `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (deferred wiring after the memory health source) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/experimentation_health_bridge.rs` + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/kask_bridge.rs` (module + re-export) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/Cargo.toml` (hkask-mcp-experimentation dep) + `kask/crates/hkask-regulation/src/loops/signals.rs` (`ExperimentationStuckExperiments` metric: variant, `as_str`, `from_str_name`, impact direction Some(false)) + `kask/crates/hkask-regulation/src/sensor_provider.rs` (`ExperimentationHealthSource` trait + `ExperimentationHealthSensor` + `DEFAULT_EXPERIMENTATION_STALE_DAYS = 7`, D-3) + `kask/crates/hkask-regulation/src/cybernetics_loop.rs` (`set_experimentation_health_source`) + `kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-experimentation/src/{types,store,server}.rs` (budget accounting + health snapshot) | The experimentation registry becomes a regulated domain (repair plan §P8.9, operator rulings D-1/D-2/D-3 2026-09-30). **Server-side budget enforcement (D-1: enforce):** `experiment_propose` requires `budget.max_runs` (a positive integer — typed `BudgetMissingMaxRuns` refusal otherwise); `fitness_record` carries `run_count` (the rollouts its reports cover) and the store sums recorded run counts against the ceiling, refusing with typed `BudgetExhausted` (failed_precondition) naming the experiment, the would-be total, and the ceiling — never a silent overshoot; exactly-at-ceiling passes. Schema declared in full at creation — the migration surface and the pre-rule NULL/0 accommodations were removed 2026-10-15 (operator no-backward-compatibility ruling, applied to data as well as code; the pre-contract registry database at the per-agent default — holding the E-cycle rows — was deleted 2026-10-16 under the same ruling, and a fresh full-schema registry is created on the next server open). **Goal-loop join (step 5):** `linked_goal_id` round-trips through the declaration and population query. **Layer-A afferent (step 1):** `ExperimentationStore::health_snapshot()` (per-experiment status, recorded runs, prediction confidence, verdict, reject reasons) + `ExperimentationHealthSnapshot::{stuck_running(stale_days), resolved_claim_brier()}` (no-headroom voids excluded from the calibration record via the literal "no-headroom" marker in the first reject reason); the bridge opens the same SQLCipher registry the MCP child serves (`HKASK_EXPERIMENTATION_DB` override → the D28 per-agent default — path parity with the server's `run()`); the sensor emits `experimentation_stuck_experiments` (set-point 0; any positive count is a deviation; impact direction: fewer is better). **Stale set point (D-3):** 7 days. A broken source warns and emits no signal — never a fabricated zero (the `.rules` failure-signal rule). Pinned by: the experimentation crate's budget-boundary, run-count, linked-goal, snapshot, and migration tests (store + tool seam); the regulation crate's three sensor tests (stuck → deviation, healthy → homeostatic zero, broken source → None) + the metric round-trip and impact-direction tests; the bridge's `bridge_senses_budget_spent_running_experiments`. |

| D89 | Durable hang-incident record — append-only JSONL beside the task traces | `crates/zed/src/reliability/hang_detection.rs` + `crates/hang_telemetry/src/hang_telemetry.rs` + `crates/zed/src/reliability/hang_detection/incident_log.rs` (new) | Upstream serializes `SerializedHangIncident`s into `telemetry.log`, but that file is an upload spool: `Client::start` recreates it with `File::create` at every app launch (`crates/client/src/telemetry.rs`), truncating the previous session's events — a bad session's hang evidence is destroyed by the restart that follows it (observed live 2026-10-03: 6 "Hang Incidents" events / 487 incidents in one release session, all erasable by the next launch; the two-symptom round-2 "zero incidents in telemetry.log" reading was this truncation, not a routing failure). The fork appends every non-empty send batch — wall-clock `recorded_at` plus the same payload the telemetry event carries — to `hang_traces/hang-incidents.jsonl`: append-only, surviving restarts; the `.jsonl` extension sits outside the task-trace cleanup's `json`/`miniprof` filter so the 3-file cap never sweeps it; empty 30-minute windows are not recorded. Failures log a warning and never take the detection loop down (the in-session telemetry channel still carries the payload). Pinned by `batches_append_without_truncating` (two batches → two JSONL lines, each parses, `recorded_at` present). **2026-10-12 merge:** upstream re-homed the telemetry module into the new `crates/hang_telemetry` crate — the seam now lives at `crates/hang_telemetry/src/hang_telemetry.rs` as `HangTelemetry::with_incident_sink` (typed batch + threshold/budget counts summed across triggers), and `incident_log` gains `HangTrigger::LateFrame` + `during_active_use`. |
