| D91 | Fork-owned dependency licenses declared — `ffmpeg` WTFPL accepted, `nom-exif` clarified | `script/licenses/zed-licenses.toml` (`accepted` list + `[nom-exif.clarify]` block) | Two dependencies the fork adds — neither present in upstream Zed — could not be resolved by `script/generate-licenses`, so the generator exited 1 and, because it truncates its output file first (`echo -n "" >"$OUTPUT_FILE"`), left `assets/licenses.md` empty. That file is a compile-time asset input (`crates/assets/src/assets.rs` `fs_embed!` includes `*.md`), so the failure breaks the release build's attribution asset, not just the report. (1) `ffmpeg-next` 8.1.0 / `ffmpeg-sys-next` 8.1.0 (the `hkask-media-widget` video decoder, default `vendored` feature) declare `license = "WTFPL"` — a maximally permissive, no-copyleft, no-attribution license that upstream's list never had to carry because upstream does not decode video. Accepted by name rather than hidden or dropped: dropping it means dropping video decode. (2) `nom-exif` 3.8.0 (a `hkask-mcp-media` dependency) declares `license-file = "LICENSE"` with no `license` field, so cargo-about cannot resolve it; the LICENSE is plain MIT, declared here with its sha256 checksum (the cargo-about clarify convention, verified against the existing `fuchsia-cprng` entry). Gate: `script/generate-licenses` (the xtask `check_licenses()` CI job runs it alongside `script/check-licenses`) now exits 0 under `--fail` with no warnings, and `assets/licenses.md` carries no removed crates. Upstream-identical before this change; upstream-reportable only in the sense that a fork adding these deps must make the same two declarations. |
| D92 | FIBO ontology rung removed — the financial-research vocabulary retires with the companies capability | DELETED: `kask/crates/hkask-bridge-ontology/src/fibo.rs`, `fibo-release-modules.tsv`, `sources/fibo/` (159 RDF + LICENSE, 6.9M), the `index_fibo` codegen and its `build.rs` pin checks, 159 `SOURCES.lock` rows, the `Fibo` arm of `OntologyNamespace` (variant, `FromStr`, `Display`, density weight 1.3) and its `select_ontology_anchor` finance branch, `fibo` from `ONTOLOGY_TERM_PATTERN` + the `fibo:Corporation` allowlist entry, the FIBO ladder assertions, the condenser's FIBO graph nodes/`HasProperty` variant, and the fabricated `fibo-fbc-fct-ra:*` widget anchors. REPLACED: `onto_anchor("corporation")` now resolves `sumo:Corporation` at the `upper` tier (`sumo::CORPORATION`, guard-covered); the six prediction-markets FIBO concept URIs are dropped rather than re-homed (they were computed-and-discarded at both call sites — `fibo_concept()` had zero callers — so no observable behavior changes); the scenarios widget carries derived-rung labels (`calibrated_forecast`/`brier_score`/`scenario_planning`); the PS-1 gate's `fibo_anchored` flag is renamed `domain_anchored` across the skill, three templates, and the lisp form check, with the conflict-resolution rank table re-ordered over the surviving supplements. Also removed in the same pass: the unwired `corpus_discover_company` tool and its `CompanySourceManifest` consumer. | **Operator ruling 2026-10-18: the platform is a general co-researcher, not a financial-research tool.** The FIBO rung was removed only after the replacement anchors were live-verified (Phase D gated Phase E). Two decisions carry the design: (1) **the six prediction-markets anchors were dropped, not re-homed** — the plan recommended `qb:` with SUMO fallback, but `qb:` is purely statistical-data-structure (36 terms: DataSet, Observation, Dimension, Measure) and carries no economic concepts, so re-homing would have been a substitution-repair onto a vocabulary with no place for them; the measured fact that the URIs were dead at every call site made dropping the honest choice. (2) **`corporation` re-homes to SUMO, not to a `qb:` term** — SUMO's `FinancialOntology.kif` publishes the class with a real definition, verified live before FIBO was removed. Gate: `onto_anchor` never returns a dangling `fibo:` concept; `named_concepts_are_published` covers `sumo:Corporation`; the ladder test asserts `corporation` → `upper`/SUMO. |
| D93 | Cloud provider test credential seam | `crates/language_models/src/provider/cloud.rs` (`init_test`: the fake-credentials injection) | The fork's default cloud provider resolves credentials through the OS keychain (the D9 credential chain), whose I/O runs outside the GPUI test scheduler; the fork-added injection `crate::tests::set_fake_credentials_provider(cx)` (commit `6d13e5f3b3d`, 2026-09-24) keeps sign-in inside the test. Pinned by the cloud provider tests that exercise sign-in and credential flows under the fake provider. |
| D94 | Per-turn instrument-affordance injection — spec B v1: task-matched skill candidates at the request tail | `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` (`AffordanceInjector` trait + `AFFORDANCE_INJECTOR` OnceLock hook + `set_affordance_injector`/`affordance_injector` + `affordance_catalog`) + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` (`run_turn_internal` call site: reads `kask.affordance.enabled` per turn, hoists the user-prompt extraction once for both injectors, appends the block at the request END) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/affordance.rs` (new: the injector — embedding KNN over the catalog, description-vector cache keyed by (name, description), per-prompt memo for continuation rounds, gates and degradation) + `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/kask_bridge.rs` (module + re-export) + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (deferred-task wiring beside the context injector; clones taken before the `RealMemoryPort::new` move) + `crates/settings_content/src/settings_content.rs` (`kask.affordance.enabled`) + `kask/docs/reference/kask-settings.md` (Affordance section) + `kask/docs/research/instrument-affordance-experiment.md` §5 (the spec) + `kask/docs/research/native-first-denominator-sample.md` (the measured discovery failure it targets) | The measured problem: skill activation is 8/8 when the operator names the skill and 0/3 for primary-contract discovery otherwise — the broken arrow is catalog MATCH, not tool willingness. The fix converts discovery from recall into perception: per substantive turn (≥ 20 words — the sensor's own `SUBSTANTIVE_MIN_WORDS` boundary), the user prompt is embedded, matched against the fresh skill-catalog descriptions by cosine similarity, and the top-3 matches above a 0.30 floor are surfaced with their EXACT invocation handles (`{"name": …, "task": …}`), the deterministic quartet (`lisp_eval`/`onto_anchor`/`render_template`), and the decline line ("instruments serve fit, not ritual") in a bounded block appended at the request tail — the highest-attention position, immediately before generation (the affordance experiment's floor tasks moved exactly this way; its t5 specimen is the exact-handle design rule). Placement contract: the block rides its own OnceLock hook with END placement, never the early splice memory recall uses, and never touches the digest-cached system prompt (prefix cache preserved). Settings: `kask.affordance.enabled`, default **false** — the operator enables it after reading the native-first-rate baseline so the pre/post comparison stays clean; read per turn at the call site, so a flip applies to the next turn without a restart. The catalog is read fresh per turn from the published `SkillIndex` (`affordance_catalog`), excluding `disable_model_invocation` skills; description vectors are cached by (name, description) so unchanged skills never re-embed, and the per-prompt memo makes continuation rounds free (known staleness window: a mid-session catalog edit serves the memoized result for that turn's remaining rounds only). Degradation contract: an embedding failure surfaces a one-line degradation note IN the block slot (never a silent absence) plus a `reg.affordance` warn, memoized per prompt; a prompt with no floor match injects nothing (no fabricated relevance); short prompts are skipped at zero embed cost. NOT pinned (documented limitation, the InertInjector precedent): the turn-loop END-placement splice itself — the dispatch hook, the catalog extraction, and the bridge matcher are pinned instead. Pinned by `matching_surfaces_exact_names_and_invocation_shape`, `short_prompts_are_not_affordance_work`, `no_floor_match_injects_nothing_and_memoizes`, `embed_failure_surfaces_the_degradation_note`, `continuation_rounds_reuse_the_match_without_re_embedding`, `changed_descriptions_re_embed_only_the_new_entry` (kask_bridge affordance tests), and `affordance_injector_dispatch_returns_the_wired_instance` + `affordance_catalog_reads_published_skills_and_excludes_user_only` (agent internal_tests). **Gate v1 (observing, landed after the injection):** at turn completion, substantive native-first turns — the drift population the sensor counts — are scanned by the pure classifier `kask_thread_state::claim_gate_findings` (deterministic, no model cooperation): a percentage/computed figure delivered in the final assistant text without `lisp_eval` anywhere in the turn fires `percent-without-deterministic-verification`; a delivered http(s) citation without the research ledger's tools fires `citation-without-research-ledger`. Thinking segments are scratch, never gated; only the final delivered agent message is scanned; at most one finding per class, each with a bounded excerpt. Findings flow through the `CLAIM_GATE_RECORDER` hook (the `RecorderHook` family, `ClaimGateFinding` re-exported on the agent surface for the zed-side sink) wired in `main.rs` to a `reg.gate` warn — the v1 sink, the running log where the operator reads regulation output; the durable `reg_query` surface and the corrective re-prompt (the affordance doc's gate) are v2, armed once these observations calibrate the checker's precision (sense before act). Pinned by the detection suite in `kask_thread_state` (`percent_in_delivered_text_fires_with_excerpt`, `citation_in_delivered_text_fires_with_excerpt`, `clean_delivered_text_fires_nothing`, `thinking_scratch_is_not_gated`, `only_the_final_agent_message_is_gated`, `both_classes_fire_together`) and `claim_gate_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable` (agent internal_tests). |
| D95 | Finished terminals release PTY resources at task finish; closed-channel resize logs at debug | `crates/terminal/src/terminal.rs` (`register_task_finished` + `release_pty_resources`) + `crates/terminal/src/alacritty.rs` (`PtySender::resize`) | The alacritty event loop thread ends with the PTY child — it breaks at child exit and drops the channel receiver (alacritty_terminal@4c12966 `event_loop.rs`), so the `EventLoopSender` can never deliver again — but `Terminal` kept `PtyResources::Active` until `Drop` or the ACP caller (upstream #62978/#64405; task terminals are retained past their command because task panes never emit `CloseTerminal` under `HideStrategy::Never`, and #64405 documents exactly this retention while fixing only the parse buffer). Every later layout resize (`TerminalElement::prepaint` → `set_size` → `InternalEvent::Resize` → the `Active` guard → `pty_tx.resize`) sent `Msg::Resize` on the closed channel and logged `failed to resize alacritty pty: sending on a closed channel` at ERROR (live 2026-10-27 20:31, five hits in interactive drag-resize bursts). Root fix: `register_task_finished` — the single point where the terminal learns the child exited (`TerminalBackendEvent::Exit`/`ChildExit`) — releases the PTY resources, so the `Active` guard no longer claims a live PTY and `has_active_pty_resources()` is truthful for retained terminals; `release_pty_resources` drops the parse buffer before the already-released early-return so a buffer rebuilt by later `write_output` calls still dies (required because auto-release now precedes upstream's parse-buffer test's manual call). Belt: `PtySender::resize` treats a closed-channel send like its `shutdown` sibling — debug-level, matching alacritty's own `Notifier` (closed-channel input writes are silently dropped) — which covers the race window between the loop thread breaking and the foreground processing the exit events. An upstream bug (upstream main still logs ERROR and never releases at task finish); upstream-reportable per the D31/D32/D41 precedent, not filed per the 2026-09-30 operator policy. Pinned by `test_task_finish_releases_pty_resources` (terminal.rs — finished task terminal must not claim live PTY resources, captured output still retained) and `resize_logs_a_closed_channel_at_debug_not_error` (alacritty.rs — source pin on the log level). |
| D96 | Built-in keymap loads surface failures and degrade — never a startup crash loop | `crates/zed/src/zed.rs` (`load_default_keymap` + `bind_built_in_keymap_asset` + `built_in_keymap_bindings_from_result`) + `crates/settings/src/keymap_file.rs` (`load_asset_result`) | Upstream's `load_default_keymap` unwraps its four built-in keymap asset loads, and `KeymapFile::load_asset` bails on ANY `SomeFailedToLoad` — discarding every loadable binding because one is stale — so a single binding to an action whose defining crate was removed panic-crash-loops the app at startup. Observed live twice: 2026-10-06 (collab removal left collab_panel/channel_modal bindings; the D90 pin `test_keymap_assets_only_bind_registered_actions` was added then) and 2026-10-28 (swarm removal left `ctrl-shift-alt-s` → `swarm_panel::Toggle` in `keymaps/default-linux.json`; two failed starts at `bc06cc7` before `780ba40` removed the binding). **Fix:** the new `KeymapFile::load_asset_result` returns the full `KeymapFileLoadResult` — the existing `load_asset` discards the loadable bindings on partial failure and `load_asset_allow_partial_failure` discards the error message; neither lets a caller surface a partial failure honestly. All four loads route through `bind_built_in_keymap_asset` → `built_in_keymap_bindings_from_result`: a partial failure binds the loadable subset and `log::error!`s the reason (a stale binding costs its own hotkey, not the app); a parse failure logs and binds nothing. The app always starts; the failure is loud. Asset cleanliness stays pinned at test time by `test_keymap_assets_only_bind_registered_actions` (D90) so stale bindings fail before any rebuild. Pinned by `built_in_keymap_partial_failure_degrades_instead_of_panicking` (the incident shape — one loadable plus one stale binding — yields the loadable binding, no panic) and `built_in_keymap_parse_failure_degrades_instead_of_panicking` (an unparseable keymap binds nothing, no panic). |

### Per-seam follow-up rulings

Dated per-seam follow-ups and corrections; each entry carries its date and pins.

**Swarm capability removal (2026-10-28, operator order — supersedes the swarm clauses of D1 F3, D2, D3, D6, D18, D28, D45, D54, D80, D86):** the local swarm capability is retired whole (see the retired D33 entry for the removal inventory). Row-level supersessions: D1's F3 delegated `host/*` skill access is removed with `host_skill_tools.rs` and the IPC `ToolInvoke`/`ToolDefinition` methods (`agent::activate_delegated_skill` + `DELEGATED_SKILL_INVOKER` deleted); D2's Steer overlay set is two (kanban, media) and `crates/hkask-steer`'s doc no longer names swarm_panel; D3's `kask-panel` persona is now the PANEL accounting identity (renamed from `swarm-panel`, cap seed `KASK_PANEL_CALL_CAP` in `main.rs`, `PanelToolInvoker` retyped to `hkask_tool_invoker::ToolInvoker` — the leaf dep now direct in `crates/zed/Cargo.toml`); D6's swarm-memory paragraph is historical (the swarm server, `swarm_recall_local`, and the `store=swarm` backfill arm on `curator_memory_backfill_embeddings` are gone — the tool now sweeps the curator store only); D18 admits `media`/`graph`/`kanban`/`scenarios` fences (the `swarm_delegate_results` widget + factory arm deleted); D28's managed-DB set is curator/kata-kanban/research (`managed_database_layout` is `[…; 3]`); D45's registry has 8 servers; D54's delegated `host/onto_anchor` parity is historical (no host tools remain); D80's kask_bridge dev-dep pairing example now reads `-p hkask-mcp-kata-kanban -p kask_bridge` (swarm removed from the example); D86's layer set is five (`agent_card` removed from `LAYERS` — old experiment rows keep reading, the removed filter value fails `UnknownLayer`). Pinned by: kata-kanban's `tool_surface_is_exactly_26_registered_tools`, the bridge's `all_mcp_servers_allowlists_match_source_env_reads` (curator no longer reads `HKASK_SWARM_MEMORY_DB`), the swap-mechanics pins (`backend_slot_swap_replaces_serving_ports_in_place` two-port form), viz-core's admitted-fence list pin, and the markdown D18 gate literal.

**D1 Lisp H0–H8 / C5 (2026-10-21):** Strong captures survive evaluation; budgeted scope teardown clears cycles. Raw/pure APIs remain unchanged. [Boundary reference](kask/docs/reference/lisp-eval-dialect.md#numeric-admission-and-limits): provisional source/env 65536/1048576 bytes, steps/depth 2000000/8192; defaults 100000/1024 (corpus 100000/100), zero/aliases unchanged. H4B bounds the exact pretty JSON document to 1 MiB, depth 128, not total heap. H5 titles read only 80 form characters (`thread.rs:7197@AgentTool::raw_initial_title`, `:7347@Erased::initial_title`); native decode/evaluation run in the background, copying/display remain foreground. H6 adds one monotonic cancellation flag, cooperative checkpoints, mandatory teardown and native cleanup receipts/turn join (`lisp_eval_tool.rs:374@LispEvalTool::run`, `:418@lisp_worker`; `thread.rs:7448@ThreadEventStream::join_lisp_completions`), with publication/weak pending-owner guards. Bounded serde/std/conversion/destruction phases remain nonpreemptible. H7 offloads only authorized IPC host Lisp decode/eval through existing Tokio blocking execution and public shared `evaluate_lisp_controlled` (`lisp_eval_tool.rs:487@evaluate_lisp_controlled`; `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/host_skill_tools.rs:119-190@run_lisp`). The invocation guard precedes first poll/spawn; actual IPC EOF winning dispatch drops its owner and signals the flag (`host_skill_tools.rs:107-137`, `:221-231`; `kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/inference_ipc_server.rs:827-840`, `:1093-1124`). Eventual worker exit/cleanup is tested, not immediate join, hard deadline or simultaneous EOF/result priority. H8 direct/routed corpus queries share one body; only Lisp options are offloaded and observe propagated request cancellation during evaluation/join, leaving downstream inference/retrieval/generation unchanged (`kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-corpus/src/tools/storage.rs:108-243`, `:605-723`). Selector/defaults/fixed 100000/100 and strict `true`/`t`/`false` remain; `nil` is refused with corrected guidance. Explicit rmcp cancel reaches the pending worker; plain EOF does not immediately signal it (tested context cancellation follows service-owner teardown after drain). Promise handler-owner drop / propagated context, not immediate disconnect or service-shutdown-worker-join. Held-worker adapter tests are not independent mid-core scope-reclamation proof. H4C measured operating envelope remains deferred; H6 native Stop validation was not performed. Installed live build `1c247be309` predates H7/H8, so source gates do not establish deployment. No hard time/memory/concurrency guarantee or new runtime, service, dependency, cache or configuration. This docs-only update does not rerun production gates; hash-bound C5 evidence remains external.

**D1 provider-neutral arbitrary JSON arguments (2026-10-20):** native `render_template.context` and `lisp_eval.env` advertise JSON text in a string and decode it before rendering/evaluation, using the same codec as MCP `AnyJsonValue` payloads (`kask/crates/hkask-types/src/tool_schema.rs`). This replaces the permissive-object schema workaround: open-map decoding was provider-dependent, and an OpenRouter-only strictness override would not establish a portable contract. Ordinary typed arguments, required fields and tool-side validation are unchanged; malformed JSON and non-object native contexts/environments are refused. MCP callers must JSON-encode arbitrary payload fields; literal string values require quoted JSON text, while omitted optional fields retain their existing meaning. No provider parser/router exceptions or dual live wire formats are added. **Saved-call replay correction (2026-10-20):** `AgentTool::deserialize_replay_input` in `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` separates persisted arguments from live input decoding. The two native map tools convert pre-change object arguments to JSON text on a replay copy through `tools.rs::deserialize_saved_json_map`; other tools retain strict decoding by default. This replaces unconditional current-wire decoding in erased-tool replay, which made valid pre-change history warn on every replay after `fd79beb00d`. Stored history, live schemas/decoders and execution are unchanged. Pinned by `saved_json_map_tools_replay_without_execution` (actual erased replay, old/current shapes, malformed-input refusals, invalid form/missing-template fixtures proving no execution) and `saved_env_migration_preserves_bindings_without_relaxing_live_inputs` (scalar arithmetic, nested values, JSON-looking strings, omitted env and strict live-map rejection). Zed-side files: `crates/agent/src/tools.rs`, `tools/{render_template_tool,lisp_eval_tool,lisp_eval_emission_matrix,lisp_eval_conformance}.rs`, and `crates/language_models/src/language_models.rs` (wire-serializer conformance test only). Pins cover lossless structured/scalar/string/null round trips, invalid input rejection, native evaluation and required-input checks, delegated host schemas, curator insert/embedding/recall, and nine provider wire types. These are local protocol/consumer checks, not a claim that every remote model was live-tested. **H0 specimen removal (2026-10-22):** the retired ED-1 scientific-validity gate specimen and its two h0 fixtures were removed from `lisp_eval_conformance.rs` (the source form no longer exists in any skill or template), together with the stale pointer to the deleted packet-study C1 evidence file; the remaining H0 specimens — MCDA weighted sum, grounding-claim gate, recursive walker, boundary, corpus query — and all success/error/audit conformance coverage are unchanged.

**D43 context-overflow follow-up (2026-09-28, `c706f1e635`):** `language_model_core::is_context_window_exceeded_message` now recognizes the Cloudflare-wrapped `Requested token count exceeds ... maximum context length` 400, so the existing `PromptTooLarge` UI and token-limit indicator receive the failure instead of `InvalidRequest`. `Thread::compaction_message_target_ix` retains billed-token cost thresholds but also checks gross tokens against input capacity: cached input is cheap, not free of context. The existing `Context Too Large` callout now names the existing native Compact context control when available and explains that unchanged retry is unlikely to help and that compaction may fail; non-native sessions are not promised a control they lack. No hidden automatic retry or new settings. Pins: `cloudflare_wrapped_context_overflow_is_prompt_too_large`, `cloudflare_wrapped_context_overflow_reaches_compaction_category`, `test_compaction_ignores_cache_read_tokens`, `context_overflow_message_names_available_recovery_without_promising_retry`.

**D1 corpus-audit handoff pins (2026-10-17):** the 61-skill semantic audit found template outputs that looked plausible while breaking their invoking skill's deterministic contract. `corpus_audit_repairs_are_pinned_at_template_handoffs` renders the live registry and pins three retained seams: product-manager complete intake emits `missing_fields: []` while sparse intake emits the five ordered questions; Sankey CSV is serialized directly from the sourced `edges` array with each row exactly once; therapy counts resolution/reification only from successful execution records, never approved-but-unexecuted proposals. **Program-manager cleanup (2026-10-21):** the authorized removal of its three rendered-record templates also removes only their sections from `crates/agent/src/tools/render_template_tool.rs::corpus_audit_repairs_are_pinned_at_template_handoffs`; the remaining intake, Sankey and therapy assertions stay. No production renderer behavior changes. PM-1 and closure-ledger self-certification are retired; the skill now directs actual implementation, deletion, tests and honest reporting with human judgment preserved. `optional_cross_skill_inputs_are_actually_optional` pins the three repaired optional-contract seams (falsifiability domain/context, task-breakdown skill catalog, skill-discovery recommendation limit/epistemic state). **Approved finalizer handoffs (2026-10-20):** `finalizer_handoff_tests::{prompt_enhance_preserves_critic_findings_at_delivery, task_breakdown_preserves_open_questions_at_finalization}` reuse the registry renderer to pin required `grill_findings` in both human delivery and structured change log, unchanged verdicts, and required `open_questions` in the writer prompt. Explicit empty arrays remain valid; omitted fields fail admission; non-array values surface input errors, with invalid question handoffs suppressing normal file-writing instructions. The tests cover all prompt delivery modes and retain the render-only/no-written-path contract. These C1 repairs replace incomplete caller contexts and implicit reconstruction; no arithmetic rule or production Rust behavior is added by those handoff repairs. **Arithmetic followups (2026-10-20):** PE-State replaces PE-2/PE-3 with one revision-bound `critique_runs` attempt log, counting pending and failed attempts within the shared three-attempt medium/high budget. The companion `raw-shape.jq` validates the original JSON before the authored Lisp form; `selected_attempt_id` is a scalar selector into that same raw log, never a review echo, so ratings/findings reach the finalizer unchanged and `delivery_verdict` cannot certify a stale revision. TB-COUNT supplies task facts before one normal critic, which emits base scores and semantic directives under a rendered count mandate; TB-2 alone applies the penalty once and emits the raw/effective gate object with required-reference coverage. `check_skill_lisp_forms` tests execute the authored PE/TB forms rather than Rust copies, and `task_count_facts_drive_the_critic_directive_mandate` pins real critic-template inputs/mandates. The existing thresholds/weights and C1 fields are retained; numeric and directive-reference checks do not establish semantic rating/text quality. These tests exercise computation, rendering and handoff shape, not the model's semantic quality.

**D1 forecasting handoffs (2026-10-21):** `superforecasting_record_and_admission_preserve_source_ownership` reuses the authored-jq runner and real renderer to pin raw evidence → narrative → caller-owned record → quality prompt. The skill's single assembly filter binds exact quantify arguments/events and the selected outcome marginal to the final estimate, rejects narrative envelope injection, and preserves null versus matching declaration receipts. `superforecasting_quality_uses_raw_bounded_criteria` executes authored SF-3 against its weight vectors, inclusive .60 boundary and malformed scores. These replace model-typed aggregates/metadata, not semantic judgment; no production Rust controller or durable live forecast probe is added.

**D1 grill-me handoffs (2026-10-20, simplified):** renderer tests load the canonical skill's jq filters and exercise the real grade/ask/final renders. One answer ledger stores latest grades and a monotonic failed flag; it replaces grading-event history, joined record copies, running assessments and recursive Lisp gates. Regrades cannot add an attempt. Delivery/evidence checks preserve retired explanations and Unassessed areas. These are finite workflow checks, not learner-outcome evidence; no production controller or interpreter builtin is added.

**D1 MCP-review artifact handoff (2026-10-20):** `mcp_review_artifact_serializes_supplied_evidence_without_reconstruction` renders the canonical findings template directly into parseable JSON and pins exact tool/finding/reconciliation and receipt-object preservation, including incomplete, blocked and not-run states. Missing required evidence inputs refuse rendering. Authored MTR-1 tests derive exact-name differences from raw lists; existing real-server router pins supply registration evidence, without a second fixture server. The template replaces a probabilistic JSON-reconstruction prompt; no production renderer/interpreter behavior or MCP tool contract changes. These are artifact/discovery mechanics, not semantic review or deployment assurance.

**D1 template metadata contract (2026-09-28; supersedes the 2026-09-24 skill-verdict note, whose `algedonic-review/record-skill-verdicts.j2` and `skill_verdict_recorder_requires_operator_decisions` pin were removed in 0b523558de):** a `.j2` header is a single `[inference]` block holding `contract.input`/`contract.output`, terminated by a lone `---`. `crates/agent/src/tools/render_template_tool.rs::validate_contract_inputs` reads `contract.input`; `strip_frontmatter` removes the header; nothing reads any other key. The body `[inference]` parameter stanza (temperature/work_effort/verbosity/thinking_budget, 214 templates) and the header `visibility:` key (251 templates) had no consumer and were deleted corpus-wide (0626d6348b); the stanza-stripping branch is deleted with them. Pinned by `all_registry_templates_conform`: a header key other than `contract` or a body parameter line is RED.

**D1 expectation-inquiry admission (2026-09-23; tightened 2026-09-24):** `crates/agent/src/tools/render_template_tool.rs::expectation_inquiry_requires_active_user_goal` pins the shipped `gradient-hunter/expectation-inquiry` contract at the actual render-input validator: missing, blank, whitespace-only, null, or non-string `active_goal` must fail visibly before prompt rendering; a nonblank goal admits it. The template opts into this check with `non_blank: true`, leaving other required inputs' presence-only semantics unchanged. The skill passes the user-stated goal explicitly with nullable operator feedback, so the template cannot quietly ask an ungrounded model to infer user interest from surprise. A live blank-goal render succeeded before this tightening; the original missing-goal correction and the nonblank check pin input admission, not the model's semantic judgment or improvement across tasks.

**D1/D28 skill-index publication ordering (2026-09-22):** `crates/zed/src/main.rs` seeds globals for the Settings page on a detached startup task; `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` publishes disk-loaded global and project skills after an agent-panel scan. The startup task may complete *after* the agent publication and previously replaced `SkillIndex` with `project_skills: []`, hiding project skills in Settings until another refresh (and potentially replacing fresher globals). `crates/agent_skills/agent_skills.rs::SkillIndex` now marks agent-published catalogs; the startup result publishes globals if the agent has not published, fills an empty global list, or adds missing shipped **non-core** skills to a nonempty partial agent catalog. It never resurrects non-shipped globals or replaces an agent-published non-core entry. A verified core entry may replace an older core entry; unverified disk snapshots cannot: `seed_shipped_skills` warns on write failure, so a readable core file alone is not proof of canonical bytes. Project groups are retained. Both seeding triggers, the global path, and disk edits remain unchanged. Pinned by `startup_skill_publication_respects_agent_refresh_order`, `partial_agent_catalog_receives_missing_shipped_startup_skills`, `startup_does_not_promote_unverified_core_over_agent_edits`, and the existing Settings scope and global-skill reload tests. The index update is synchronous on the GPUI foreground app context, so the ordering decision is made against the index *at publication time*, not against a pre-I/O snapshot.

**D1/D28 startup convergence follow-up (2026-09-23; partially included in concurrent `ea2ddc6725`/`d82ed4bf52`, remaining edits uncommitted):** installed-build startup now checks loaded core SKILL.md bytes against the bundled seed before publishing a core entry to Settings or replacing an existing agent-published core entry; development builds treat the authored checkout file as the source. A denied overwrite with a readable stale core file is not a receipt. `SkillIndex` labels startup-origin publications, and each live `NativeAgent` observes that signal and sends its existing per-project context refresh notification; an agent-origin publication is not echoed back, avoiding a refresh loop. This fills Settings and re-reads the active project model catalog on a startup publication while preserving user globals and project groups. The scoped-miss prompt test now installs a colliding MCP prompt and asserts that it is discoverable before testing the named miss. Pins: `failed_core_seed_write_does_not_promote_readable_stale_content`, `verified_core_seed_promotes_and_signals_refresh`, `startup_publication_refreshes_active_project_without_filesystem_event`, `missing_scoped_skill_does_not_fall_through_to_other_prompts`. The installed project loader also rejects parsed core files whose bytes cannot be verified against the bundled source, reporting a skill load error rather than passing stale core instructions to the model (`stale_installed_core_is_excluded_from_active_project`). A later change to the file after verification remains subject to the existing disk-body read path; this is not an atomic content-locking guarantee.

**D1/D28 developer-only skills (2026-09-24; superseded 2026-09-26):** the 2026-09-24 `shipped: false` tier is removed by operator ruling 2026-09-26 — every skill in `.agents/skills/` is available to every install and every user, and skills and templates carry no release state (they are evolving drafts; "shipped" survives only as Rust identifiers). `SkillMetadata::shipped` / `Skill::shipped`, the `build.rs` payload exclusion and the checkout-loader filter (`load_global_skills_from_source`, `is_development_shipped_skill`) are deleted, and the key is stripped from the 10 marked SKILL.md files. `shipped_skill_seed_all_parse_without_errors` now pins that the embedded payload equals the authored `.agents/skills/` tree; `development_shipped_skill_has_one_live_source` pins that an authored skill (even with a legacy `shipped: false` key) loads as a global skill.

**D2/D40/D59 learning-loop routing (2026-09-26):** operator rulings 2026-09-26 — learning capture is always on and covers every agent's skill use, not only the Curator's; proposal authority is the operator's by default and may be granted to the Curator; accepted work and schema-evolution requests are executed by a spawned agent with clear instructions; nothing is done until verified unless the operator or Curator explicitly skips verification. (1) `SkillOutcomeRecorder` now receives the invoker: `SkillTool::with_invoker` stamps the session's agent id at `register_session` (`Curator` or `Zed Agent`), and `activate_delegated_skill` stamps `DELEGATED_SKILL_INVOKER` for inference-IPC `host/skill` runs; both `main.rs` recorders write `invoker` into the `reg.skill.<id>.outcome` payload that `reg_query` and the gemba walk read. Pinned by `test_skill_tool_records_outcome` (default `Zed Agent`, Curator-configured tool `Curator`) and `skill_outcome_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable`. (2) `CURATOR_STATIC_CONTEXT` gains a `### Learning loop` block routing quality signals to `curator_report_skill_use_issue`, `memory_insert`, `therapy` and `skill-maintenance`, and hands `evolve_mcp_tool_schema` requests to a delegated kanban task; `test_curator_overlay_advertises_only_registered_tool_names` now also checks curator-MCP names against `hkask_mcp_curator.rs` (observed RED on a renamed token). (3) D40: the goal-lifecycle sentence in `system_prompt.hbs` now states the D58 acknowledgment lifecycle. (4) `algedonic-review` gains "Proposal authority and done"; `skill-maintenance` Act step defers to it.

**D59 mechanical skill-use issue capture (2026-09-26):** closes the learning loop's observe stage without relying on the model to report. `KaskThreadState::active_skill` holds the last skill a thread activated successfully; in `Thread::run_tool`, a non-`skill` tool failure while it is set (authorization failures excluded, as for the retry tracker) calls `agent::record_skill_tool_failure(skill, invoker, tool, error≤500B)`. `main.rs` wires the recorder to `kask_bridge::persist_skill_tool_failure` → durable `reg.skill.<id>.tool_failure` (warns on failure), which the `algedonic-review` gemba walk reads with outcomes via `reg_query`. Records are unclassified evidence; `curator_report_skill_use_issue` remains the classified (`failure_origin`) channel. Pinned by `test_tool_failure_under_active_skill_is_recorded` (no attribution before activation; one record after, with skill/invoker/tool/error; observed RED with the hook disabled) and `skill_tool_failure_survives_archive_restart`.

**D59 recorder consolidation (2026-09-26, P5.5 removal pass):** the four process-global regulation recorders (`MCP_TOOL_OUTCOME_RECORDER`, `SKILL_OUTCOME_RECORDER`, `SKILL_TOOL_FAILURE_RECORDER`, `OPERATOR_FEEDBACK_RECORDER`) were four ~57-line copies of one static + test thread-local + setter + scoped-test helper; they are now one `agent::RecorderHook<T>` (via `recorder_hook!`) with `scoped_for_test`, and the per-hook `scoped_*_recorder_for_test` functions are deleted. `main.rs` wires one skill-outcome recorder at startup instead of two: it always feeds the ledger and persists `reg.skill.<id>.outcome` once the deferred task sets the shared `OnceLock<kask_bridge::CuratorRegulationArchive>`. Behavior is unchanged; existing pins (`scoped_skill_outcome_recorder_is_thread_local_and_panic_safe`, `skill_outcome_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable`, `operator_feedback_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable`, `test_skill_tool_records_outcome`, `test_tool_failure_under_active_skill_is_recorded`) cover it. Prompt removals in the same pass: the Curator overlay's unimplemented "metacognitive self-calibration" bullet and prose `### Methodology` block (replaced by one line naming the four skills); D26's separate `## Tool failure-mode warnings (kask)` section folded into `## Tool Use` bullets (pin `test_system_prompt_contains_tool_failure_mode_warnings` now asserts the rules sit in Tool Use and the old heading is gone).

**D1/D28 development skill identity (2026-09-22):** the checked-out `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` is the only live body for each authored skill while developing from this source tree. `agent_skills::seed_shipped_skills` removes old copied global directories for shipped names and retired names (`remove_retired_skill_copies` / `RETIRED_SKILL_NAMES` — see D1); it never creates links that an older editor could write through into source. Both the agent-panel loader and Settings startup loader call `load_authoritative_global_skills`, which reads shipped bodies directly from the checkout and global-only user skills from disk while ignoring stale global shipped entries. Project discovery skips the same checkout entries, leaving one catalog entry and one `/name` body. Installed binaries without a checkout still seed the bundled body to disk; unrelated global-only and project skills remain untouched. Pins: `development_shipped_skill_has_one_live_source` (removal, direct body, one catalog entry, edit-through, repeat call) and `checkout_shipped_skills_have_one_catalog_identity`; a live `skill` tool read requires editor rebuild/restart.

**D1 core-skill slash and load gate (2026-09-22):** `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` now applies the same `candidate_overrides_skill` rule to model-catalog deduplication, conflict diagnostics, and unqualified `/skill-name` selection. The old unqualified slash route chose only by source precedence; a project-local non-core collision could execute different instructions from the catalog's unshadowable global core entry. The slash path still filters by the typed name and clones only its winner (no all-skills clone on each prompt); first-on-ties, explicitly source-qualified `/:name` / `/<worktree>:name`, MCP-prompt priority, and manual invocation of `disable-model-invocation` skills are preserved. `crates/agent_skills/agent_skills.rs::parse_skill_frontmatter` also rejects a project-local `core: true` claim even if its name is reserved: frontmatter alone cannot mint core authority. This rejection is surfaced as a skill load error; a project-local non-core skill with the same name remains available by explicit worktree scope. RED/GREEN pins: `project_skill_cannot_claim_core_status` and `unqualified_skill_winner_matches_core_aware_catalog`; full slash-to-model pin: `unqualified_core_slash_invokes_global_body`. `project_skill_with_core_name_without_core_status_remains_loadable` pins that a non-core collision remains loadable; the existing `test_skill_source_scope_prefix_and_matches_scope` pins its scope matching, but this change adds no scoped slash-to-model end-to-end test. `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/skill_creator.rs` corrects the stale assertion that the loader rejects every reserved-name collision (the creator still refuses such names on save). Existing `test_apply_skill_overrides_*` pins retain ordinary project precedence and first-on-ties.

**D1 scoped-skill miss (2026-09-22):** `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` now returns a named skill-not-found error when an explicitly scoped `/:name` or `/<worktree>:name` cannot resolve. Previously the scoped match's `if let` fell through to `find_prompt(None, name)` and then ordinary chat; a missing skill could invoke an unrelated MCP prompt. A successful scope match still invokes the specified skill, while unqualified commands retain MCP-prompt priority. Pinned by `missing_scoped_skill_does_not_fall_through_to_other_prompts` through `NativeAgentConnection::prompt` (RED: sent the model a chat turn; GREEN: no model request, named error).

**D44 tool-source startup order (2026-09-22):** `crates/zed/src/main.rs` installs `KaskToolSource` before spawning the Tokio task that rebuilds its initial cache and calls `notify_kask_tool_surface_changed`. Previously the task could notify while registries still saw an unwired source; `reload_kask_tools` returned without loading descriptors and no second event was guaranteed when servers were already registered. The event-driven loop and server membership semantics are unchanged. `kask_tool_source_is_installed_before_initial_refresh` pins the order at the production composition root (RED before the move, GREEN after). The registry's existing late-registration tests pin subsequent updates.



**2026-09-18 maintenance (D3/D7):** rustfmt-only normalization of
`test_calling_actor_is_distinct_per_thread_and_deterministic` in
`crates/agent/src/thread.rs` and the caller-identity fallback in
`crates/zed/src/main.rs`; attribution behavior and its existing test are unchanged.
The D7 isolation script no longer greps the deleted updater source: its existing
`crates/auto_update*` directory-absence checks enforce removal, while host
initialization checks remain active. Verification: workspace `cargo fmt --check`
and `kask/scripts/build/check-zed-isolation.sh`.

**D2 Kanban Steer goal-lifecycle correction (2026-09-27):**
`crates/kanban_panel/src/kanban_panel.rs::steer_system_prompt` no longer
calls goals ephemeral: the kanban server retains a scored goal until its
outcome reaches curator memory and is acknowledged. This replaces stale
Steer guidance without changing goal storage or upstream behavior. Pinned by
`steer_prompt_describes_durable_goal_acknowledgment` in `kanban_panel` (red
against the old prompt, green with the corrected one).

**D9/D2 Media Steer extension and credential-page helpers (2026-09-23; Reduct credential seam removed 2026-10-22):**
The shared `write_credential`/`delete_credential` helpers in `kask_page.rs`
mark a credential configured (or removed) and nudge the MCP runtime only
after the keychain operation succeeds; a failure logs the URL, never the
key — this half remains live for every Data Services row. The Reduct-specific
half of this seam is removed with the media server's Reduct.cloud connection
(the `REDUCT_API_KEY` slot display in `data_services.rs`, the media-only
credential grant, the panel's `reduct_*` group labeling, and the operator-
supplied v3 API verification history are gone; the plan artifact is deleted on
execution 2026-10-25 per the house docs lifecycle — git history holds it).
The keychain slot is
purged at startup (`kask_bridge::purge_removed_reduct_api_key`, D5).
Pins: `reduct_key_reaches_no_child` (kask_bridge absence pin), settings_ui
`removed_reduct_cloud_row_is_absent`, media `tool_names_match_live_router`
and `tool_surface_is_exactly_71_registered_tools`, Media-panel
`server_tools_are_all_advertised`, and the schema-compliance retired-tool
list (which carries the 20 removed `reduct_*` names).

**D35/D9/D2 research-curation scope (2026-10-21, operator-approved):**
Media Steer keeps cross-domain tools but guides only ingestion, organization,
research annotations, retrieval, transcription, and source-linked excerpts/reels.
Its generated inventory target is 71 (91 − 20 Reduct cloud tools, removed
2026-10-22); running deployment and passing integration are separate observations.
The D35 recovery/routing account above is historical where it describes creative
image/video/TTS or workflow execution; those tools and settings are retired.
`KaskMediaSettingsContent` / `KaskMediaSettings` and the Media settings page retain
only STT/vision, including settings-layer defaults and explicit-empty fail-visible
behavior. Historical lineage/OMC remains inspectable, not executable replay.
Gallery annotations use stable `gallery_asset_id`; transcript annotations use
immutable-source word ranges. Lexical transcript search returns bounded excerpts
with source/timing citations and continuation, never a semantic-search claim.
Research jobs retain transcription and audio/video trim/concat. Face ID is opt-in.
Existing rollback, gallery identity, transcript alignment, and accepted
DNS-rebinding risk/revisit boundaries remain. The retired creative skill and its
exclusive logo templates are removed; installed skill retirement bookkeeping must
also remove copied retired skills without deleting user-adopted content. Pins: `steer_prompt_carries_research_scope_and_boundaries`,
`historical_creative_hint_remains_readable`,
`media_models_are_research_only_and_round_trip_to_child`, and
`media_settings_default_carries_stt_and_vision_models`. These are test targets,
not a claim of validation or deployment.

### Retired seams (numbers are never reused)

**D4** — guard layer (`hkask-guard` crate removed in b89a28359e; the `RoleOverride` scanner's false positives blocked legitimate template rendering);

**D33** — swarm panel + swarm server (retired 2026-10-28 with the entire local swarm capability, operator order): the `hkask-mcp-swarm` MCP server (37 tools, the A2A protocol surface, the local agent registry, the shared swarm memory store), `crates/swarm_panel`, `crates/hkask-swarm-widget` (and its D18 `swarm_delegate_results` fence language + viz-core factory arm), the `swarm-intelligence` and `local-research-swarm` skills with their 8 templates, the `kask.swarm` settings section + settings page, the swarm `BuiltinMcpServer` registration, the `swarm_memory` managed-DB row, the `a2a` (a2a-lf) workspace dep, the child→parent `ToolInvoke`/`ToolDefinition` inference-IPC methods with `ToolDispatchPort`/`host_skill_tools` (their only consumer was the swarm local delegate loop; `CreateWorktreeThread` and the delegation-grant machinery remain for kata-kanban), and kata-kanban's registry-coupled surface (`LocalAgentRegistry` reuse, `Task.swarm_id`/`delegate_result`/`deterministic_verdict`, the `kanban_task_delegate_result` tool — write-orphaned since the in-server executor died; the worktree-spawn path reports via task comments). `kanban_task_spawn` survives: it executes via zed-native worktree agent threads, now always building the in-memory task-agent card (the registry's former fallback). The panel persona was renamed `swarm-panel` → `kask-panel` (`KASK_PANEL_CALL_CAP`). If multi-agent swarms are wanted again, that is a new product decision under a new number. **D10** — kask panel (redundant with the agent panel + curator threads; standalone viz views replaced by the D18 inline widgets); **D12** — retired 2026-09-27 by operator ruling: the Kask-specific API-compatible credential env-name path was deprecated months earlier, so preserving that path does not justify a fork edit. The alphanumeric normalization helper, its pin, and its marker were removed; the ordinary upstream formatting and crate dependency were restored. Provider keychain credentials and MCP child credential injection are separate current functions and remain. The initial audit mistakenly promoted a passing obsolete-behavior pin to functional necessity, then questioned the operator's explicit deprecation decision; both failures are recorded in `kask/docs/research/d-seam-audit.md`. **D17, D19** — retired (kask-seam-audit); **D30** — local skill marketplace (deleted; skill sharing moved to the ABW cloud); **D38** — folded into D37 (same bug class — reasoning-model request guards — same fix pattern, two surfaces); **D49** — folded into D42 (D13's residual budget removal belongs under the uncapped-thinking ruling); **D50** — folded into D46 (explicitly "extends D46"); **D53** — folded into D54 (one anchoring feature, one seam — the prompt identity, the tool, and the skill). **D47** — tool-schema property-annotation stripping (retired 2026-09-24 by operator decision: upstream no longer deletes properties named `description`, so the original bug is gone; the remaining fork behavior removed per-input `description`/`default` from every tool schema. Measured across 390 built-in and MCP tools: 205,712 bytes with annotations vs 90,606 stripped (~29k tokens, bytes÷4, upper bound per turn). The operator chose giving the model per-input guidance over the size saving; `tool_schema.rs` now matches upstream and the three fork pins were removed). **D11** — `time::format_description::parse` deprecation allow (retired 2026-09-24: its premise was `time` 0.3.54+, but the workspace resolves 0.3.47, where `parse` is not deprecated; `git_graph.rs` now matches upstream). **D13** — model-derived OpenRouter output budget and Ollama `num_predict` cap (retired 2026-09-24 under the operator ruling that max-token budgets are deprecated; requests again omit an output limit unless the caller sets upstream's per-request `LanguageModelRequest::max_output_tokens`, and the model-derived `open_router::Model::max_output_tokens` field, `TopProvider`, half-context cap, Ollama `max_output_tokens` override, and their five pins were removed). **D15** — bounded cursor-blink timers (retired 2026-09-24 under the no-backward-compatibility, take-away-first ruling: `crates/editor/src/blink_manager.rs` is byte-identical to upstream again. Its fork-only settings handler re-enabled blinking in every editor that had lost focus, which live probes measured at 420–730 blink ticks per 5 s. A retirement probe on the upstream code (2026-09-24, removed after use) measured 10 blink notifies per 5 s steady, 10 after five settings updates, 11 after 20 rapid pauses, and 0 for a blurred editor after settings updates: upstream epoch checks already prevent the timer stacking D15 claimed; upstream `test_only_focused_editor_blinks_across_window_activation` is the retained behavior pin. The obsolete `eval_disable_cursor_blinking` scenario and fixtures remain removed under D72.) **D22** — two compile-only pin syntax repairs are folded into D3/D8's existing `kask_wiring_symbols_exist` guard; its test-support-only check and live production wiring remain. **D48** — the fork-added discovery warning's source-chain formatting is part of D76, not a distinct upstream bug fix; its behavioral pin remains under D76. **D62** — retired 2026-09-27: its Kask-owned advice queue/receipt/tools had already been removed by the board migration (D8); the remaining board and independent rollout-impact queue (D57) have their own pins. The consumer-free `EscalationKind`/`EscalationID` declaration and re-export were deleted with this retirement. The negative advice-tool/receipt regression checks remain; numbers are never reused. **D21** — widget→agent compose-back seam (the "I disagree" gesture), retired 2026-09-28 with the operator-directed seam removal `fa95c2b8c7`: the `hkask-conversation-injector` leaf crate, the agent_ui injector wiring, and the widget-only `from_label`/`disagree_draft` affordances are deleted. That removal shipped without its coupled cleanup — a dangling `swarm_panel` dev-dep declaration broke the whole workspace manifest until the owning stream pruned it, a stale `hkask-types` doc example named the deleted crate, and this row still described the seam as live. The row removal, the doc correction, the ledger count, and this retirement record land together in the residue-completion commit (removal hash: `fa95c2b8c7`; the owning stream's manifest/lock repair is theirs). If a widget→agent compose-back is wanted again, that is a new product decision under a new number. **D34** — provider-batch API bridge (retired with the batch API: its provider-batch formatter was removed in `3443af0ba4`, no `generate_batch` bridge exists, and the D42 row records the removal; also noted in `kask/docs/architecture/zed-host-architecture-plan.md` §3). **D88** — reverted 2026-10-02 (operator correction, same day it landed): the boot-time Zed-account sign-in gate and the title-bar sign-in default-off were unrequested divergence around upstream behavior. The sign-in flow is upstream Zed function (P12 expects the Zed account as the host identity source), the project's governing goal is minimal divergence from Zed, and the "accountless fork" framing used to justify the changes was invented, not derived from any ruling. The underlying worker stall (`client.rs:1510` — tiny_http `recv_timeout`, a blocking call inside an async task) is an upstream defect, upstream-reportable per the D31/D32 pattern, not fork-patched. **D66** — retired onto upstream at the 2026-10-27 sync: upstream resolved the equal-start injection-order contradiction with the opposite canonical order (longer end pops first at equal start) and flipped `check_invariants` to match; their design delivers the seam's purpose (deterministic equal-start ordering, no `layers out of order` panic), so the fork's shorter-first tie-break, its cmp line, and its pin test were replaced by upstream's version (`test_markdown_injections_with_same_start_and_different_ends`). The upstream `ParseStep` cmp also adds a language-id tiebreak the fork's lacked. **D27** — the upstream-adopted linux-gated non-interactive sandboxed shell replaces the fork's copy at the 2026-10-27 sync; the failure rationale stays in this row. Numbers are never reused. **D85** — mimalloc default (retired 2026-09-30 for upstream parity: the default landed unmeasured and the draw-cost regression appeared only after it; mimalloc stays opt-in exactly as upstream; the arena-ratchet memory finding remains real — address it via MALLOC_ARENA_MAX in the launcher, never a code divergence).

## Other zed-kask-modified files (supporting the numbered D-seams)

These files carry `// zed-kask:` comments but are supporting edits, not
primary divergence seams:

- `crates/cli/src/main.rs` — `zed-kask://` added to `URL_PREFIX` array.
- `crates/zed_actions/src/lib.rs` — `OpenApplicationUrl` action doc references `zed-kask://`.
- `crates/gpui/src/app.rs` — `register_url_scheme` doc example uses `zed-kask`.
- `crates/terminal/src/alacritty/hyperlinks.rs` — `zed-kask://` added to URL regex (hardcoded — terminal doesn't depend on `paths` crate).
- `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` — **thread persistence (D28, 2026-09-10 merge):** `Session` holds a weak ACP thread and retains child ACP threads; release observers replace manual reference counts. `release_session` checks the released entity id, enqueues its final draft before removing project state, and detaches the coalescing save worker. The worker drains the last payload after its watch sender closes and retains only a `WeakEntity<NativeAgent>` for Kask's `ThreadSaveFailed` event on database-connect/write errors; failed delivery is logged, not silently dropped. The existing `ThreadView` subscription remains the UI sink. Quit flushing is retained. Added `test_save_worker_reports_database_failure_after_sender_closes`; retained upstream release/subagent-leak, save-burst, last-owner and quit-flush tests. The final-save failure test and both release/session-subagent tests passed during merge recovery. No live application-shutdown smoke test is claimed.
- `crates/agent/src/tools/list_mcp_tools_tool.rs` — the D44 discovery meta-tool (`list_mcp_tools`): enumerates the registered MCP surface on demand, the pull mechanism that replaced the removed LazyToolRouter's per-turn push.
- `crates/agent/src/kask_thread_state.rs` — **KaskThreadState**: all kask-specific per-thread state (D2/D6/D25) grouped into one struct so upstream `Thread`'s struct definition stays clean and upstream rebases touch one field init instead of nine. Methods on this struct encapsulate the kask-specific turn-loop behaviors that were previously inline in `Thread`'s methods (`render_system_prompt`, `enabled_tools`, `handle_tool_use_event`, `handle_completion_event`, `flush_pending_message`, `cancel`, `run_turn_internal`, `new_subagent`). See `kask/docs/plans/thread-hooks-refactor-2026-08-26.md` for the full refactoring plan. Pinned by `test_last_completion_truncated_distinguishes_max_tokens_from_cancel`, `test_system_prompt_renders_session_context_without_rules_or_agents_md`, `test_cached_filtered_context_reuses_on_unchanged_inputs`, `test_tool_retry_tracker_integration_in_thread` (the Phase 0 pinning tests that survive the D6 curator-gate removal — `test_curator_memory_edit_tool_classification` and `test_curator_memory_edit_tools_gated_to_curator_threads` were deleted with the gate), plus `context_injector_dispatches_by_agent_id` (D6 dispatch-by-agent_id), `test_context_server_tools_remain_cross_domain` (D2 cross-domain Steer surface), and `test_system_prompt_names_tools_hidden_by_profile` (D44 visibility marker).
- `crates/agent/src/templates.rs` (and `system_prompt.hbs`) — **full structure + divergence catalogue: [`kask/docs/architecture/skills-and-composition.md`](kask/docs/architecture/skills-and-composition.md) Part I** (includes the rebase procedure for this file and the four prompt surfaces — base template plus the Curator/swarm/kanban overlays; formerly `AGENT_SYSTEM_PROMPT.md`, folded 2026-08-28). The Division of Responsibilities section and its bullet amendments are D40, designed against [`kask/docs/architecture/functional-interaction-spec.md`](kask/docs/architecture/functional-interaction-spec.md). Agent Skills system-prompt section follows upstream wording (body retrieval via `skill` tool). Additional `system_prompt.hbs` divergences in shared upstream sections: (1) a loop-termination guardrail added to `## Task Execution` ("If a tool loop repeats without measurable progress… stop, summarize what you tried, and ask the user rather than continuing indefinitely"); (2) the mermaid diagram-type list names the exact directives merman requires (`sankey-beta`, `xychart-beta`, `architecture-beta`, `radar-beta`) rather than bare forms the renderer drops, keeps `kanban` (it **is** a supported mermaid directive — see `markdown::mermaid::SUPPORTED_PREFIXES`), and separately notes that the `graph`/`media`/`portfolio`/`scenarios` fenced blocks are kask viz widgets, plus that a ```kanban fenced block is a widget while `kanban` inside a ```mermaid block is a diagram. The prompt is pinned against the renderer allowlist by `test_system_prompt_advertises_every_supported_diagram_type` in `crates/markdown/src/mermaid.rs`, which replaces the former "if updating this list, also update the system prompt!" manual-sync comment; (3) the `## Session Context` (`static_context`) block is a sibling of the `{{#if (or user_agents_md has_rules)}}` custom-instructions guard, not nested inside it (see D2). Pinned by `templates::tests::test_system_prompt_contains_loop_termination_guardrail` (which also asserts the guardrail states a concrete iteration count, not a vague quantifier), `..._mermaid_list_uses_renderer_directives`, and `..._renders_session_context_without_rules_or_agents_md`.
- `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view/thread_view.rs` — `render_skill_loading_issues` surfaces `LoadFailed`, `DescriptionTooLong`, and `CatalogBudgetExceeded` (the former disabling of description-length + catalog-budget issues was deliberately reversed in d2f8d76b3a, 2026-08-20, "Restore skill loading warnings" — the disabled code paths were removed).
- `crates/agent_skills/agent_skills.rs` — `global_skills_dir()` relocated to the kask data root via `set_global_skills_dir_override` hook (D28 — Standardized Artifact Storage). Choosing a source writes the opt-in plus exactly one ID; choosing memory-only clears both. `BridgeContextInjector` rejects legacy multi-ID selections for automatic injection with a surfaced unavailable notice (explicit tool search remains multi-source). The explicit MCP search remains independent; external passages are framed and labelled as evidence rather than Curator memory.
