- `crates/agent/src/tools/context_server_registry.rs` + `crates/agent/src/agent.rs` + `crates/zed/src/main.rs` + `crates/project/src/context_server_store.rs` (the store half: non-auth transport-death state moves + the public self-heal trigger) — (existing, previously undocumented here) on-demand server self-healing restart in `ContextServerTool::run` + timeout-vs-transport-death retry classification. **Kask merge removal semantics (2026-09-08):** `reload_kask_tools` now REMOVES previously-kask-sourced servers the source no longer lists (tracked via `kask_server_ids` on the registry; the merge previously only ever inserted, so an unloaded kask server — D45 toggle → `McpRuntime::stop_server` → dropped from the runtime's registered set — kept ghost tools on the agent surface forever). Store-registered servers are untouched; only ids the previous pass sourced from kask are eligible. The merge is extracted into `merge_kask_tool_descriptors` (testable without the store machinery) and emits `ToolsChanged` only when the visible surface changed (no subscriber churn on steady-state poll ticks). Pinned by `kask_merge_removes_servers_absent_from_the_source` (context_server_registry.rs — insert, removal, store-server survival, steady-state no-op). **Agent-path MCP tool outcome recording (2026-08-28).** `ContextServerTool::run` wraps `run_inner` to record every agent-path MCP tool outcome (server name, tool name, success, error kind) via the process-global `agent::record_mcp_tool_outcome` hook (re-settable `Mutex` slot, `set_mcp_tool_outcome_recorder`), wired in `main.rs` to the shared `RegulationLedger::record_outcome` on the GPUI-global tokio runtime. **Error-path collapse (2026-08-28, second pass):** all kask MCP servers now return `Result<String, McpToolError>` and rmcp sets `is_error` natively with the typed kind in `structured_content`; `run_inner`'s `is_error` branch carries that value in the error output's `raw_output` so outcome recording and the retry tracker (`is_authorization_error`) classify structurally — the in-band envelope sniffing and `[kind] ` prefix parsing are deleted. **Invalid-JSON classification (2026-09-09):** `run_inner` consumes the `ToolInput` payload stream directly (`ToolInput::next`) instead of `recv`, so the typed `InvalidJson` payload — the model's tool-call arguments were unparseable (the truncation signature, see `Thread::handle_tool_use_json_parse_error_event`) — returns a typed `invalid_argument` envelope (`invalid_json_tool_output`) instead of raw error text; `mcp_run_outcome` classifies it structurally and the ledger excludes it from the tool-reliability success rate (`hkask_types::tool_response::is_not_tool_fault_kind`) while keeping it in the per-kind breakdown. The LLM-visible parse-error message is unchanged (pinned end-to-end by `test_streaming_tool_json_parse_error_is_forwarded_to_running_tool`). **rmcp deserialization-rejection classification (2026-09-09):** `mcp_run_outcome`'s untyped fallback routes through `hkask_types::tool_response::error_kind_from_display`, which classifies rmcp's macro-layer rejections ("failed to deserialize parameters: …") as `invalid_argument` — the third model-caused class (schema-invalid args rejected below the kask typed-error surface; live-observed via the curator_memory_recall missing-`entity` probe), excluded from the reliability rate and visible in the per-kind breakdown. Pinned by `error_kind_from_display_classifies_rmcp_deserialization_rejection` + `test_mcp_run_outcome_untyped_deserialization_rejection_classifies_invalid_argument`. **Structural display hints:** `run_inner` pushes `display_hint`/`display_hints` fenced blocks as tool content so the D18 media renderer shows them deterministically (no model cooperation). Pinned by `agent::internal_tests::mcp_outcome_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable` + `context_server_registry::tests::{test_mcp_run_outcome_maps_success, test_mcp_run_outcome_maps_error_text, test_mcp_run_outcome_empty_error_text_falls_back, test_mcp_run_outcome_extracts_typed_kind, test_invalid_json_input_classifies_as_invalid_argument}`. The kask tool source (single spawn authority) is pinned by `kask_tool_source_hook_is_settable_replaceable_and_absent_when_unwired` + `kask_server_tool_dispatches_through_kask_tool_source`; the unwired-source degradation is surfaced (2026-08-30): `reload_kask_tools` warns once — a latch that re-arms when the source is wired, so the startup window produces at most one line — naming the startup-ordering caveat, pinned by `kask_tool_source_unwired_warn_latch_fires_once_and_rearms`; **startup-race poll (2026-08-30):** the registry re-reads the source on a 2s GPUI-native timer (`KASK_TOOL_SOURCE_POLL_INTERVAL`) — kask servers register with the governed runtime, not the ContextServerStore, so their late registration never fires a store event, and a registry created during window restore (before the deferred MCP launch registers tools) otherwise stays empty for the whole app session (observed live 2026-08-30: a fresh agent-panel session had zero kask tools until a store event was fired by hand). The merge re-inserts every entry so the wrapped descriptors stay fresh across kask server restarts, but emits `ToolsChanged` only when the visible tool-name surface changed. Pinned by `agent::tests::test_kask_tools_surface_when_source_populates_after_registry_creation` (tests/mod.rs — its mutable source is a D61 scoped test override, so the populated poll window cannot enter a parallel registry); the timeout-vs-transport-death retry classification by `timeout_and_transport_death_classify_into_distinct_retry_verdicts` + `test_mcp_tool_timeout_does_not_retry` (the timeout half of the self-heal path, full-path: exactly one CallTool, no restart-and-retry). The advice-apply bridge once wired into this wrapper is retired (2026-09-24; see D1). **Agent-turn native-first recording (2026-10-23, the native-first-rate sensor — spec D, `kask/docs/research/instrument-affordance-experiment.md` §4):** the drift feedback loop's unsensed leg. `record_mcp_tool_outcome` senses tool FAILURES; nothing sensed instrument ABSENCE — a session completing every substantive turn from native generation alone read as healthy because no tool ever failed. Three seams: `agent.rs` adds the process-global `NATIVE_FIRST_TURN_RECORDER` (`set_native_first_turn_recorder` / `record_agent_turn_native_first`, the same `RecorderHook` family as the other four recorders); `Thread::run_turn`'s Ok branch computes the turn's instrument summary at turn completion (pure `kask_thread_state::turn_instrument_summary` over the turn's message span from the pre-turn `message_ix` — a turn is native-first when it contains no context-server (MCP) tool call and none of `KASK_HOST_INSTRUMENT_TOOLS` (`lisp_eval`, `render_template`, `skill`); substantive = user prompt ≥ `SUBSTANTIVE_MIN_WORDS` (20) words, so acknowledgments are excluded; editor-native tools (terminal, file tools) are not kask instruments — a terminal-only turn is still native-first for this sensor); `main.rs` wires the recorder to `RegulationLedger::record_agent_turn` on the GPUI-global tokio runtime (a tokio-RwLock future — background_spawn would panic). The kask side (`hkask-regulation`, fork-owned): the ledger keeps a capped (200) rolling window of substantive turns; `NativeFirstRateSensor` is registered in `CyberneticsLoop::build` with `set_points.native_first_rate_threshold` (default 0.50, bounds [0.0, 1.0) — 1.0 rejected as silently-vacuous, the mirror of the tool_reliability 0.0-floor rule); the `native_first_rate` SignalMetric carries an Escalate→Curation policy rule (`native_first_rate_exceeded`), so deviations reach the reviewable board with rate/threshold evidence. Only completed turns count; failed turns are not drift evidence. Pinned by `native_first_turn_recorder_records_and_is_replaceable` (agent), the five `kask_thread_state` classification tests (`substantive_turn_without_instruments_is_native_first`, `short_prompt_is_not_substantive`, `context_server_tool_use_clears_native_first`, `host_instrument_tool_use_clears_native_first`, `editor_native_tool_use_is_still_native_first`), the `hkask-regulation` sensor/window/bounds tests (incl. the no-fabricated-zero `None` contract and the equality-is-not-a-deviation pin), and the exhaustive-metric policy test (the rule is mandatory).
- `crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs` + `crates/agent/src/tools/read_file_tool.rs` — `timeout_ms` and line-number fields deserialize through the shared maybe-string family (`hkask_types::maybe_string`, consolidated there 2026-09-27 from `crates/agent/src/tools.rs` — models sometimes send numbers as strings; the kata-kanban `prediction` field adopted the f64 variant after the live goal-create incident of three rmcp rejections of `"0.8"`). Pinned by the `maybe_string` tests in hkask-types and `test_terminal_tool_input_accepts_string_timeout_ms` in tools.rs.
- `crates/agent_ui/src/agent_panel.rs` — eager `SkillIndex` population + `create_thread_with_options` returns the agent used (Curator support, D2).
- `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs` — `render_agent_markdown` wires `.media_block_renderer(hkask_viz_core::block_renderer())` for D18 (`media and `graph fenced block rendering); the `hkask-viz-core` registry composes the media + graph widget renderers.
- `crates/language_model/src/language_model.rs` + `crates/language_model/src/registry.rs` — `api_url()` accessor.
- `crates/open_ai/src/list_models.rs` — generic `/v1/models` discovery for OpenAI-compatible providers (independent of the retired D12 credential spelling).
- `crates/zed/src/main.rs` (beyond the composition-root blocks) — kask MCP server auto-launch, `sync_kask_mcp_servers` re-sync when the inference socket becomes available. **Single spawn authority (2026-08-29):** kask servers are no longer registered with zed's per-project `ContextServerStore` — `KaskMcpDescriptor` and the per-project spawn path are deleted. The governed `McpRuntime` owns every kask server process; the agent's tool surface routes through `agent::set_kask_tool_source` (a process-global `KaskToolSource` wired in `main.rs`, cache refreshed event-driven from the runtime's tool-surface change signal, `notify_kask_tool_surface_changed` — the former 2s poll was deleted 2026-09-22). `sync_kask_mcp_servers` now only defensively unregisters any descriptor squatting a kask built-in ID — the extension proxy (`context_server_store/extension.rs`), the one non-test register site besides kask itself, registers descriptors under extension-supplied, unnamespaced IDs in the same process-global registry, so a kask ID can be occupied from outside (an "older build" cannot reach an in-process registry — the former stale-descriptor rationale was impossible and was corrected 2026-09-27) — and removes raw `context_servers` entries for kask IDs (the namespace guard — a raw entry would still spawn a keyless instance through the generic store path). The `.env` file loading has been fully removed — the keychain (zed's `CredentialsProvider`: data-service keys under `kask://credentials/<key>`, inference-provider keys at their provider `api_url` slots, see D5) is the single source of truth for API keys. `sync_kask_mcp_servers` also removes raw `context_servers` settings entries whose IDs match built-in kask servers: `ContextServerStore::maintain_servers` merges registry descriptors with `or_insert`, so a settings entry silently shadows the managed registration (the only path that composes env via `build_mcp_server_env`) — a stale `env: {}` entry shipped a keyless, socketless server to the agent's tools with no warning. Removals summarize in one warn per pass (count + IDs — a per-entry warn made every startup emit a wall of near-identical lines); the write is self-terminating (next observer pass is a no-op; D32's no-op-write skip covers the boundary). Removal logic pinned by `kask_bridge::mcp_servers::tests::{remove_shadowing_context_server_entries_removes_builtins_and_keeps_others, remove_shadowing_context_server_entries_is_noop_without_builtins}`. **Load/unload (D45, 2026-08-31):** `sync_kask_mcp_runtime_servers` now also handles `kask.mcp` load/unload toggles — it computes the load set (same expression as the launch loop) and stops/starts servers through the runtime's own primitives (`stop_server` / `start_server_with_env`), gated on a launch-pass-complete latch set at the end of the deferred launch loop; see D45 for the full mechanism. **Logging (2026-08-29):** upstream logs to stdout only when stdout is a PTY and to the log file otherwise; zed-kask always initializes the file sink and additionally mirrors to stdout on a PTY — post-hoc debugging of live defects (observer wiring, MCP restarts, credential resolution) must not depend on terminal scrollback. The settings-reactivity investigation was blocked because warns went to a scrolled-away PTY and no `Zed-Kask.log` existed. Composition-root code; pinned by review + the DIVERGENCE note (no harness reaches `main()`'s logging init).
- `crates/client/src/zed_urls.rs` — shared-session URL uses `ZED_URL_SCHEME` (D7).
- `crates/zed/src/reliability.rs` — 2026-09-10 merge retains upstream's weak `ProjectRegistry` for orphan-project diagnostics alongside Kask's existing fleet-health polling. `start_context_server_health_polling` + `classify_fleet_health` + `context_server_fleet_observable`: polls all open projects' `ContextServerStore`s every 10s (`read_context_server_statuses`), merges them with the governed `McpRuntime`'s live connections (`merge_fleet_health` — a server is healthy if `Running` in either path; the total is the union, no double-counting), and feeds a health snapshot to the cybernetics loop's `ContextServerHealthSensor` via `BridgeContextServerHealthSource`. Only `Starting`, `Running`, and `Error` count toward the fleet total; `Stopped` and OAuth-waiting states (`AuthRequired`, `ClientSecretRequired`, `Authenticating`) are excluded — they are legitimate non-running states, not fleet degradation. Counting them produced constant false-positive `ContextServerFleetDegraded` alerts. The pre-login gate (`context_server_fleet_observable`, suppressing the snapshot while `Client::user_id()` is `None`) is **restored (2026-08-30)**: it was removed with the single-spawn-authority change on the theory that the kask servers it guarded against had left `ContextServerStore`, but code analysis showed `maintain_servers` has no login gating — user-configured servers keep the storm path open (a server legitimately sitting in `Starting` pre-login counts toward the fleet total and fires `ContextServerFleetDegraded` every tick). Pinned by `reliability::tests::{all_running_is_fully_healthy, starting_counts_toward_total_but_not_healthy, error_counts_toward_total_but_not_healthy, stopped_servers_are_excluded_from_total, oauth_waiting_states_are_excluded_from_total, all_stopped_produces_zero_total_no_alert, all_starting_signals_degradation, mixed_real_and_excluded_states, empty_slice_is_zero_zero, pre_login_fleet_is_unobservable, resolved_user_opens_the_gate}` (11 tests).
- `crates/agent/src/tool_retry_tracker.rs` + `crates/agent/src/thread.rs` — **tool retry death-spiral guard** (hard enforcement in the agent loop): two tracking dimensions — per-input `(tool_name, input_hash) → failure_count` (identical retries) and per-tool consecutive failures (trivially different inputs) — after 3 failed assistant messages the result carries a warning with the Bayesian success probability `1/(N+2)` and a switch-tools directive; after 5 the tool hard-refuses (per-tool override: `skill` at 12 — its multi-step PDCA cascade legitimately retries; `skill_tool_uses_override_hard_cap`). **Parallel-batch correction:** `Thread::run_tool` passes the owning assistant-message index to `record_failure_for_message`, so nine sibling failures count once per input and once per tool; a corrected call in the next message remains admissible, while five distinct failed messages still refuse. Raising `HARD_CAP` or skipping `invalid_argument` would weaken loop protection and is not the fix. `is_authorization_error` skips `permission_denied` (a denied call cannot succeed on identical retry — counting it produces bogus "switch tools" statistics). Dispatched from `handle_tool_use_event` → `run_tool` for every tool (built-in + MCP); lives on `Thread` (per-thread, never persisted). Pinned by `failed_parallel_batch_allows_corrected_request`, `repeated_failed_batches_still_reach_hard_cap`, the tracker's sequential tests, `test_parallel_tool_failures_preserve_next_message_retry` (Thread state + production callsite), and `test_tool_retry_tracker_integration_in_thread` (sequential thread behavior). **Success-repetition streak (2026-10-02, agent-loop guardrails plan Slice 1):** a third dimension — `(tool_name, input_hash) → consecutive_success_count`, deduped per assistant message — guards consecutive identical SUCCESSFUL executions, the class the failure side cannot see (a success resets it; measured on the source system, 71% of budget-exhausted sub-agents died inside runs of ≥10 consecutive byte-identical calls, median 87, worst 198 of 200). Same ladder and check-before-call point as the failure side (`handle_tool_use_event`, after the failure check): hint at `WARN_THRESHOLD` prior identical successes, refuse at `hard_cap_for`; a different dispatch in between breaks the chain (reset-on-distinct — `check_repetition` clears the key's streak when another key was dispatched since its last success; restored by the 2026-10-02 plan audit: the call-level revision had shipped a per-key map with no reset, so interleaved identical calls accumulated and the edit→verify loop would be refused at its 6th verification as a "zero-gain loop"; the Lean spec's `nextStreak_distinct_resets` now describes the shipped behavior); a failure of the same key resets the streak (each outcome resets the other, so a key never holds both). `record_success` is replaced by `record_success_for_message` (message identity enables the sibling dedup; all callers updated). Design revision recorded in git history (the agent-loop guardrails plan §7, removed from the active tree 2026-10-05): the original batch-signature design was revised to call-level granularity because zed-kask dispatches tool calls as they stream in — there is no pre-execution point where the whole batch is known. Pinned by `success_streak_hints_after_three_identical_successes`, `success_streak_refuses_at_hard_cap_and_changed_input_resets` (the no-starvation property, Lean-pinned in `kask/lean/repetition_guard_spec.lean`), `interleaved_identical_successes_do_not_accumulate` + `intervening_dispatch_resets_the_streak` (the reset-on-distinct contract), `sibling_identical_successes_in_one_message_count_once`, `failure_resets_the_success_streak`, `success_streak_uses_the_skill_hard_cap_override`, `success_streak_map_caps_at_max_entries`, and the updated `test_tool_retry_tracker_integration_in_thread`.
- `crates/language_model/src/api_key.rs` — **API-key env-var lock removed**: upstream locks `ApiKeyState::store` while the key's env var is set (the UI cannot update the key); zed-kask removes the lock — the keychain is the single source of truth (D5), and a UI-entered key must win over a stale env value (`load_status` becomes `SystemKeychain`, the next `load_if_needed` early-returns with the UI key). Pinned by `store_overrides_an_env_var_key`.
- `crates/collab/src/main.rs` + `crates/collab/tests/integration/db_tests.rs` — **local SQLite bootstrap schema**: for local dev the collab database starts empty; zed-kask applies a bootstrap schema guarded for idempotence (the bootstrap SQL uses `CREATE TABLE "users"` without `IF NOT EXISTS` — a second `collab serve` against the same file would crash; the guard skips re-application when the `users` table exists). Pinned by `setup_app_database_is_idempotent_for_sqlite`. The postgres test variants require a live Postgres (skipped without it).
- `crates/zed_credentials_provider/src/zed_credentials_provider.rs` — **credentials provider is always the OS-keychain one**: upstream uses a file-backed development provider in dev mode; zed-kask's release channel is always "dev", so an upstream revert would silently route every `kask://credentials/*` write to a JSON file while `build_mcp_server_env` keeps reading the OS keychain — every API key invisible to MCP servers while the UI reports healthy. Pinned by `new_always_constructs_the_keychain_provider`. **Test consequence (2026-09-24):** upstream tests that sign in through `Client` relied on the dev-channel JSON provider; with the keychain as the only default, their credential writes run outside the GPUI test scheduler and the scheduler panics "Parking forbidden". `crates/language_models` tests therefore install a `FakeCredentialsProvider` as the `ZedCredentialsProvider` global (`language_models.rs` `set_fake_credentials_provider`, used by `init_test` there and in `provider/cloud.rs`), which fixes `provider_authenticate_waits_for_current_user` and `sign_out_hides_cached_cloud_models`; the same `init_test` sets a `FakeFs` global, which OpenCode's dynamic model catalog now reads at registration (fixes `runpod_is_a_dedicated_provider_not_openai_compatible`). **D5/D9 async URL access (2026-09-23):** the provider and `crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page.rs` prefetch await `hkask-keystore` URL operations on async-std's executor instead of blocking GPUI background workers on a fresh OS thread per request. The keystore retains synchronous key operations for pre-app passphrase rotation and standalone processes, and shares the same oo7 URL operation logic with its sync path. This does not route oo7 directly through GPUI's background executor (which previously failed to drive its reactor) or change the keychain URL/schema. Pinned by `all_credential_operations_await_the_keystore_without_blocking_gpui_workers`, `async_url_round_trip_uses_disposable_keyring`, and `sync_and_async_url_operations_share_entries`; a live Secret Service/GUI latency comparison remains unverified. **KeychainError::Timeout arm (2026-10-04):** the keystore's synchronous read path (`retrieve_by_key`, used by every MCP child's startup credential resolve) is deadline-bounded at 5s after the wedged-portal incident — a session restart left the XDG secret portal accepting connections but never answering, and the unbounded read hung every child at startup; a read past the deadline now returns the typed `KeychainError::Timeout` so a required credential surfaces through the existing refuse-to-start path with a legible reason. This provider's async URL path gained the `Timeout` match arm and was then deadline-bounded at the same 5s (`retrieve_by_url_async`, `KEYCHAIN_READ_TIMEOUT_SECS` — the arm fired live on 2026-10-25/26 when the keyring daemon died mid-startup). The credentials-provider trait has no transient state, so a wedged portal surfaces as `None` — the same signal as "absent" — with a visible warn; the startup credential-recovery sweep (D5) re-reads after the portal recovers.
- `crates/remote_server/src/remote_editing_tests.rs` — test accommodations only: telemetry metrics disabled by default in the remote-editing test harness; skills execute via body injection (D1's mechanism) in the remote tests.
- `crates/marketplace_ui_common/src/marketplace_ui_common.rs` — the marketplace chrome crate survives the D30 marketplace retirement: its shared card/list UI is reused by kask catalog pages (the in-file marker documents why the crate is still compiled).
- `.github/workflows/kask-invariants.yml` — fork-added CI workflow ("Kask invariants"; added 2026-10-05, `c2d706ba14`): runs the `kask/scripts` invariant gates on every push/PR (isolation, build profile, formatting, dead-code allows, string errors, MCP tool tests/packaging/registration, regulation namespaces, skill crossrefs, retired-record, crate hygiene, docs count/frontmatter/citation drift/diagram-parity, duplication ratchet, gate selftests) plus tiered build/test jobs (`kask-tests`; main-only `zed-check`) and a non-blocking goal-traceability advisory. No upstream counterpart at this path; recorded here so the fork-added file is not read as drift at the next rebase.

**Supporting-surface citation closures (2026-10-27 audit):** the marker↔register reconciliation and full-tree diff-vs-upstream sweep found no unregistered behavioral divergences; the following files carry fork changes registered by description but not previously cited by path —
- `crates/context_server/src/client.rs` + `crates/context_server/src/transport/stdio_transport.rs` — D5/D3's child-process transport: `env_clear()` with a minimal passthrough set (PATH, HOME, D-Bus vars for keychain access) and the server identity threaded into the transport.
- `crates/agent/src/delegation_authority.rs` — D23/P1a's host-derived delegation identities (host-owned tool identities and persisted ceilings; never model-supplied aliases), consumed through thread persistence.
- `crates/language_models/src/provider/{llama_cpp,lmstudio}.rs` — the env-var key-lock UI arm (disabled input + reset tooltip) removed with the `api_key.rs` lock under the D5 single-source-of-truth ruling.
- `crates/language_model_core/src/request.rs` — D42's `reasoning_effort` request-field naming lives here since upstream's restructuring moved the field out of `language_model_core.rs`; upstream's 2026-10-27 `thinking_effort` rename sites are flipped back at each merge (the recurring-cost note lives in the D42 row).

**Marker taxonomy note (2026-10-27 audit):** 20 markers in `crates/agent/src/tools/context_server_registry.rs`, `crates/zed/src/main.rs`, and `crates/project/src/context_server_store.rs` read `zed-kask: D-seam — F<N>/P<N>`, citing the deleted MCP repair plan's functional-unit labels (the plan artifact is gone per the house docs lifecycle; git history holds it). The behaviors they annotate are registered under D3, D44, and the `context_server_registry.rs` entry above; those labels are historical taxonomy, not D-row citations — do not chase them as seam ids.

**Temporary foreground timing probes removed (2026-09-23):** the `[DIAG-thread-perf]` probes in `crates/gpui/src/{window.rs,elements/animation.rs}`, `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs`, `crates/sidebar/src/sidebar.rs`, and `crates/agent/src/{agent.rs,tools/edit_file_tool.rs}` were investigative instrumentation, not a lasting fork behavior. They logged ordinary frame, event and tool activity as warnings, flooding the operational log. The counters, hot-path clocks and call-site formatting are removed; animation scheduling and actual failure logging are unchanged. High frame counts observed in the captured log alone do not establish an animation defect or a measured improvement from this removal. A source-wide prefix sweep and scoped compilation check the cleanup; live performance still requires separate measurement. **Second probe set removed (2026-09-24):** the probes added in `1e0b56216d` (`crates/gpui/src/{app.rs,app/context.rs,window.rs,elements/animation.rs}`) and `70e856a197` (`crates/gpui_linux/src/linux/wayland/client.rs`, `crates/agent_ui/src/conversation_view.rs`) were reverse-applied after their before/after readings were taken (P7 in `kask/docs/plans/hkask-core-mcp-repair-improvement-plan.md`); `animation.rs` is byte-identical to upstream again, and the other five files match their pre-probe revisions.

## hKask workspace members (additive — upstream never touches)

The following are added to the root `Cargo.toml` `[workspace.members]` array.
Upstream merges never conflict with these paths.

- `kask/crates/` — hKask crates (`hkask-types`, `hkask-storage`, `hkask-memory`, `hkask-regulation`, `hkask-tool-port`, `hkask-keystore`, `hkask-event-store`, `hkask-mcp`, `hkask-mcp-server`, `hkask-inference`, `hkask-condenser`, `hkask-bridge-ontology`, `hkask-email`, `hkask-forecast`, `hkask-lisp`, `hkask-steer-core`) plus the `kask_bridge` bidirectional seam (D8, zed-kask-side). The `hkask-{media,graph}-widget` and `hkask-viz-core` viz crates live under `crates/` (zed-kask-side, see D18) because they render GPUI elements and must depend on `gpui`/`theme`, which §13.1 forbids for hKask crates.
- `kask/mcp-servers/` — 8 MCP server crates (`hkask-mcp-{corpus,curator,experimentation,kata-kanban,media,prediction-markets,research,scenarios}`). The condenser library crate (`kask/crates/hkask-condenser`) provides in-process thread condensation via `kask_bridge::BridgeThreadCondenser`.
- `crates/marketplace_ui_common` — shared catalog-page chrome (`MarketplaceCard`, search bar, empty state) used by `kanban_panel`

## Governing invariant (§13.1)

**hKask crates NEVER depend on zed-kask crates; zed-kask depends on hKask.**
The sole bidirectional seam is `kask_bridge` (D8), which lives under
`kask/crates/` and depends on both sides. Enforced by
`kask/scripts/check-hkask-no-zed-deps.sh` (wired into CI).

## Upstream-sync runbook

1. `git fetch upstream && git merge upstream/main`
2. Conflicts will only appear in:
   - The D-seam files listed above (see the numbered table; the full retired list is under Retired seams above — numbers are never reused)
   - `[workspace.members]` / `[workspace.dependencies]` in root `Cargo.toml`
     and `crates/zed/Cargo.toml` (kask deps + upstream's crate renames, e.g.
     `csv_preview` → `tabular_data_preview`).
   - `Cargo.lock` — regenerate from the merged manifests (do not hand-merge;
     `cargo check --workspace` rewrites it).
   - `kask/deny.toml` `allow-git` list — upstream may adopt new git forks
     (e.g. `zed-industries/async-tar`, `tree-sitter/tree-sitter`). Add the new
     source URLs; verify with `cargo deny --config kask/deny.toml check`.
   - `typos.toml` — deleted under D46 (2026-09-10, operator ruling); upstream
     merges show delete/modify conflicts — resolve by re-deleting.
   - Modify/delete conflicts — upstream may restore files zed-kask deliberately
     deleted under D7/D16 (icons, `.desktop` templates, bundle scripts, release
     workflows, community-bot workflows). Resolve by re-deleting; verify with
     `bash kask/scripts/build/check-zed-isolation.sh`.
3. Everything under `kask/` is additive → never conflicts. (Exception: kask
   tracing targets using the `reg.*` prefix must be registered in
   `CANONICAL_NAMESPACES` or retargeted to `hkask.*` — the reg-canonical gate
   scans `kask/crates/` and `kask/mcp-servers/`. Performative log targets
   belong on `hkask.*`, not `reg.*`.)
4. After resolving, run `bash kask/scripts/check-hkask-no-zed-deps.sh` to
   verify the §13.1 invariant still holds.
5. Run `cargo check -p kask_bridge -p hkask-types -p hkask-mcp-server` to
   verify the bridge + foundation still compile.
6. Run `bash kask/scripts/build/check-zed-isolation.sh` — verifies deleted
   upstream surfaces (icons, `.desktop` templates, bundle scripts, release
   workflows) were not silently restored by the merge.
7. Run `./script/clippy` (per `.rules` build guidelines — use this instead of
   `cargo clippy`; runs under `--deny warnings`). A `rust-toolchain.toml` bump
   may promote previously-allowed lints to denied — clippy catches these.
8. Run the full verification gate:
   - `cargo fmt --all -- --check`
   - `cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast -p 'hkask-*' -p kask_bridge`
   - `cargo deny --manifest-path Cargo.toml --config kask/deny.toml check`
   - `cargo machete`
   - `buf lint crates/proto/proto` && `buf format --exit-code crates/proto/proto`
   - `cd kask && bash scripts/check-unused-deps.sh` (nightly)
   - All `kask/scripts/check-*-selftest.sh` scripts

## Upstream-sync history

Dated records, newest first, kept verbatim. The current release facts live in Status at a glance above; these sections record how the fork reached them. Per-seam rulings inside them carry their own dates and pins.

### History collapse and layered reconstruction — 2026-10-27 (operator-approved)

The pre-collapse fork history was erased and `main` rebuilt as four layered
commits parented on upstream `main`. Measured diagnosis (pre-collapse): the
43,109 commits ahead of upstream were 39,857 upstream-authored pre-rebuild
lineage — inherited through years of normal merges after upstream
force-pushed rebuilt histories — plus 3,252 fork-authored commits; GitHub's
compare counted the inherited ancestry as fork work. The collapse
layers, gated as follows (layer 1 structurally — no upstream file or
root-manifest byte touched; layers 2 and 3 on full `cargo check --workspace`:
the first buildable state is layer 2, whose wiring carries the root-manifest
changes together with the kept-crate edits that detach them from the
surfaces layer 3 removes):

1. **Additive mass, unwired** — the `kask/` tree and the 14 new zed-side
   crates (panels, widgets, `hkask-steer`, `hkask-viz-core`,
   `hkask-tool-invoker`, `marketplace_ui_common`, `remote_server_download`,
   `hkask-media-benchmarks`) laid onto upstream without touching any
   upstream file or the root manifests.
2. **Divergence wiring** — the root `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` (workspace
   membership and the D7/D90 dependency removals travel with the crate
   edits they are incoherent without), the ~195 surgically modified
   upstream files, the fork-added modules inside existing upstream crates,
   `assets/` profile/keymap edits, build config. This is the reviewable
   divergence surface; audit it against the seam table above, row by row.
3. **Upstream-surface removals** — the 275 deleted upstream paths (the D90
   collab stack, upstream release workflows, bundlers, flatpak).
4. **Meta and records** — `.agents/`, `.rules`, `AGENTS.md`, `kask/docs`,
   this register. The final tree is byte-identical to the pre-collapse
   `main` tree plus this record.

The layer commits are reconstructed states, not development history: each
is buildable and structurally true (the current tree's decomposition), but
no intermediate existed chronologically.

**Erasure ledger (P5.5):** the fork's 3,252-commit pre-collapse line and
the 39,857 inherited dead commits; 2 stale sync branches
(`upstream-merge-2026-07-31`, `upstream-sync-2026-08-26` — superseded
parallel lineage); 574 local tags (upstream-inherited release markers and
moot fork markers); `refs/delta/*`; `refs/archived-worktrees/*`; origin's
orphaned version tags `v0.36.0`, `v0.40.1`, `v0.40.2`. The
`corpus-zk-ref-open` release and its origin tag are kept (the published
zk-ref-open corpus anchor). No archive branch exists — erased means
unreachable, by operator decision.

**Pre-collapse hash convention:** hashes cited in historical prose (sync
records, pass ledgers, skill case records, seam-table rulings) that predate
this collapse reference the erased pre-collapse history; they do not
resolve as git objects in this repository. The seam table is the operative
register for current lookups; where prose cites a pre-collapse commit, the
row or ledger line carries the durable record.

**Runbook consequence:** after any future upstream force-push, the re-link
procedure above restores the merge base, and re-running this collapse
(rebuilding the layer series from the then-current tree onto the
current upstream tip) is the only way to keep the compare display
truthful. The collapse is cheap enough to be the standing defense.

### Upstream merge of `37faef81f7` — 2026-10-27 (upstream history rewrite recovery)

Merge of upstream `main` (rewritten history) into fork `21f11b3855`, merge base
`93d7456688` (the 2026-10-10 content point). Before this sync, upstream Zed
force-pushed a rebuilt history: every commit after 2021-03-28 was re-created
under new hashes (content-faithful — verified byte-identical trees at the
fork's last merge point: `93d7456688` is content-identical to the previously
merged `d140451094`). The fork's recorded upstream ancestry (old-hash commits)
became unreachable from the new `upstream/main`, collapsing the merge base to
the 2021 root — a plain `git merge` would have computed against a 2021 tree.

**Recovery (two-step ancestry re-link):** first `git merge -s ours 93d7456688`
— a content-identical, tree-unchanged merge that re-adopts the new upstream
ancestry at the correct point; then a normal `git merge upstream/main`, whose
base became `93d7456688` and whose delta was the real ~17 days of upstream
development (528 changed files, 1896 fork-side files since 2026-10-12, 119
changed on both sides → 31 git conflicts).

**Seam decisions and re-homes in this merge** (upstream-touched rows only;
untouched rows unchanged):

- **D1** — upstream rearchitected command dispatch into
  `NativeRetryAction::for_prompt` + `execute_retry_action`; the fork's
  classification rulings moved with it: `/trace` classifies before the skill
  arms (D70), a missing scoped skill is a named error
  (`missing_scoped_skill_does_not_fall_through_to_other_prompts`), and the
  unqualified skill match uses the core-aware `select_unqualified_skill`.
  The v1 completion-based prompt path (`AgentSessionClientUserMessageIds`)
  was rebuilt over the receipt transport (`submit_native` + the completion
  channel) so tests and ACP v1 clients drive the same bookkeeping.
- **D27** — upstream adopted the non-interactive sandboxed shell
  (linux-gated; zed-kask is linux-only per D7); the fork's copy is retired
  onto theirs, pin retained.
- **D42** — upstream re-added a `supports_none_reasoning_effort` gate on
  `into_open_ai_response`; the fork's 8→7-arg form retained (explicit disable
  sends `effort: none` regardless of metadata). Upstream's generic
  mandatory-reasoning guard in `Thread::build_completion_request` was
  adopted under the fork's `reasoning_effort` naming (`thinking_allowed:
  thinking_enabled || !supports_disabling_thinking_at_effort(...)`), and the
  fork's `reasoning_effort` request-field rename survives (428 refs; upstream
  rename sites flipped back).
- **D73** — upstream adopted the fork's defer/materialize pattern as
  `ToolCallSync::{Live,Restore,Materialize}` + `restored_view_ids`; the
  fork's parallel `initialize_entry`/`sync_entry_impl`/`materialized`-flag
  machinery is deleted (retired onto upstream's). The hydrate pin was
  rewired onto `sync_entry_initial`.
- **D66** — RETIRED onto upstream: upstream resolved the equal-start
  contradiction with the OPPOSITE canonical order (longer end pops first,
  `check_invariants` flipped to match). Their design delivers the seam's
  purpose (deterministic equal-start ordering, no panic); the fork's
  shorter-first tie-break, its cmp line, and its pin test are replaced by
  upstream's. Number recorded in Retired seams.
- **D83** — upstream's new in-crate `opencode::completion::stream_completion`
  dispatcher restored a fabricated `unwrap_or(8192)` output cap; the fork's
  truthful `Option<u64>` passthrough re-applied at the call site.
- **D5/D9 supporting files** — `api_key.rs`: the upstream env-var store lock
  stays removed; upstream's new url-change reload block adopted.
  `zed_credentials_provider.rs`: the always-keychain provider (async-std
  keystore routing, typed `KeychainError` handling) was reconstructed after
  the auto-merge grafted its body onto upstream's deleted
  `DevelopmentCredentialsProvider`; the file-backed provider stays deleted.
  `zed` test init installs `FakeCredentialsProvider` first (the 2026-10-12
  upstream-helpers × fork-environment class, now in the shared `init_test`).
- **D80** — eval_cli `acp` (v1 schema) import dropped with upstream (v2-only).
- **D90** — re-deleted upstream's restored `Match::Channel` arms + channel
  imports in file_finder; the audio output-only `resolve_device` retained
  (upstream's `open_test_output` had zero callers — deleted).
- **Workspace `Cargo.toml`** — upstream's `wgpu` git pin (Vulkan surface-drop +
  WebGL resize fixes) and `webpki-roots` bump adopted; fork-added
  `circular-buffer`/`cocoa-foundation` workspace deps dropped (zero consumers);
  `webrtc-sys` stays removed (D90).
- **`kask/deny.toml`** — added `gfx-rs/wgpu`, `zed-industries/tree-sitter-go`,
  `zed-industries/oo7`, `rust-minidump/minidump-writer` to `allow-git`; removed
  the dead entries (`livekit-rust-sdks` — D90, `merman`, `yawc`, `proptest`);
  dropped the `RUSTSEC-2026-0204` ignore (upstream bumped past it) and added
  five documented ignores for new advisories against upstream-graph deps
  (tokio AsyncFd; four zstd decoder/encoder issues).

**Merge-introduced interaction found (verified by probe, 2026-10-27):**
`zed::tests::test_disable_ai_menu_update_with_malformed_keymap` fails NOT for
pre-existing reasons — an earlier draft of this record said so and was wrong;
`crates/fs/src/fs_watcher.rs` (+566 lines: folded watcher subscriptions,
preserve-child-watches rework) had been missed in the first diff pass. The
verified mechanism: with D41's parent-anchored keymap watch on `/` overlapping
the settings watcher on a not-yet-existing config dir, upstream's reworked
fan-out routes the file-creation event only to the `/` subscription; the child
subscription receives just the directory event, which D41's filter correctly
drops — so the settings write never reaches the store (a probe test reproduced
the split delivery exactly; it was deleted after the diagnosis). At
`21f11b3855` the old fs_watcher fanned the event to both subscriptions and the
test passed. This is an upstream-watcher × D41-pattern interaction, upstream-
reportable per the D41 precedent; flagged as the D41 follow-up probe — it can
affect live first-run settings reactivity when an ancestor subscription
overlaps a descendant-dir watch created later.

**Disposition:** an initial operator ruling removed the `disable_ai`
capability with the test, but it was REVERSED the same day on seeing the
surface — 39 files / 227 references against an upstream feature under active
development would buy permanent re-removal friction for zero user benefit
(nobody uses `disable_ai` in zed-kask; it defaults to off upstream). The
capability stays as upstream ships it; the failing test is `#[ignore]`d at
`zed.rs` with the verified reason quoted in the skip line, so the tripwire
stays compiled and visible until upstream fixes the watcher fan-out.
Also pre-existing (verified):
`kask/mcp-servers/hkask-mcp-research/src/ontology.rs` fmt drift (upstream never
touches `kask/`).

### Upstream merge of `93d745668805029476e6d7fbb19652116fed51de` — 2026-10-12

Merge of upstream `main` into fork `652715c15a`; merge base
`7fc2cf9b84005e3d46159b9b51a466cbc29ada42` (the 2026-09-24 sync). All 68
active seams were decided RETAIN in the Step 0 survival review; upstream
absorbed none of the fork's fixes. Re-homes, strips, and refinements carried
in this merge:

| D-seam | Change carried in this merge |
| --- | --- |
| D24 | `api_url()`/`api_key()` re-homed onto the `LanguageModelClient` trait (upstream moved credential resolution there); `crates/language_models/src/provider/open_ai_compatible.rs` joins the seam's file list; anthropic keeps `api_key` only, matching the fork. |
| D42 | Upstream dropped the `supports_none_reasoning_effort` gate: `into_open_ai_response` is 8→7 args at every call site; the fork's no-hidden-budget policy is unchanged. |
| D73 | Re-homed onto upstream's `synced_source_version` entry sync; `materialize_user_messages` deleted with upstream's new entry pipeline; the pin is renamed `test_historical_user_messages_hydrate_at_entry_creation`. |
| D78 | Rollback condition refined: the failure arm keys on the pipeline's own edits-applied record (`EditPipeline::edits_applied`, `WritePipeline::content_written`) instead of the buffer diff — an externally-changed buffer is never reverted and never claims a rollback. Pinned additionally by upstream's `test_streaming_edit_detects_new_ambiguous_match` (external mid-call change + ambiguous match → bare error, buffer preserved). |
| D83 | `BedrockAdaptiveReasoningEffort` rename carried (5 refs); `into_bedrock` takes `Option` args; bedrock `models.rs` tests migrated to truthful `Option<u64>` caps. |
| D89 | `telemetry.rs` re-homed to the new `crates/hang_telemetry` crate (`crates/hang_telemetry/src/hang_telemetry.rs`); `HangTelemetry::with_incident_sink` (typed batch + threshold/budget counts summed across triggers); `incident_log` gains `HangTrigger::LateFrame` + `during_active_use`. |
| D90 | Audio input strip completed against upstream's reworked audio crate: `ensure_devices_initialized`/`open_input_stream` deleted, `resolve_device` output-only; output/Sound API kept. |

Test fallout fixed in this merge (fork tests migrated to the merged
machinery; the only production-semantics change is the D78 refinement
above): the merged fake's snapshot semantics (`fake.model()` returns a
clone — tests capture `update_model`'s return value when mutating
capabilities), the mid-turn title generation racing tool-result follow-ups
on the one registered fake (tests select requests by `CompletionIntent`,
not list position), the `user_skills`/`non_core_skills` merge-dedup
collision (fork tests call `non_core_skills`), the subagent
delegation-authority snapshot (the parent must hold a tool for the child's
`delegation_for_child` authority to allow it), upstream's `sign_in`-based
test helpers writing real OS-keychain entries through the fork's
always-keychain credentials provider (edit_prediction tests install an
in-memory `ZedCredentialsProvider` global; the D24 provider override moved
to the default settings layer so upstream's `set_provider(None)` cases
resolve to `zed`), and one unregistered-fake binding in agent_ui
(`delegated_sibling_authority_precedes_first_completion`).

### Release identity — 0.40.0 (operator decision, 2026-09-10)

**D45 managed-server status — 2026-09-27:** operator changed the functional meaning of `Running` from "registered tools" to "callable now." The managed MCP row now combines discovered tools with `McpRuntime`'s non-blocking live-connection reading through the existing `KaskToolSource`; absent/contended/disconnected readings remain neutral `Unknown` for loaded servers, and a deliberate unload stays `Stopped`. The runtime's existing surface-change signal also announces unexpected connection loss so an open settings window redraws; discovered metadata is retained for recovery rather than deleted to force a cosmetic status. Pins: `kask_managed_server_status_requires_liveness_and_discovery` and the real-child `discovered_tools_outlive_a_killed_connection_without_claiming_liveness`. This changes the row's status only, not tool advertisement, dispatch, retries, or credentials.

**D45 discovery-only publication — 2026-10-20:** both deferred launch and settings-driven starts call `start_server_with_env` directly. It records the launch generation/specification before connecting and publishes metadata together with a live connection only after tool discovery. The empty preregistration API and its surface-preservation rules are removed. Configured rows remain visible from `BUILT_IN_MCP_SERVERS`; retained discovered tools still do not imply a live connection. `reconnect_integration.rs::second_start_with_same_env_is_a_noop` now holds discovery open to assert no premature metadata/event, then checks publication notification, unchanged metadata and PID, and no notification churn on a repeated start. The killed-connection integration case and UI liveness matrix retain the legitimate metadata-without-connectivity coverage; the weaker fabricated-metadata UI test and its now-unused runtime/Tokio dev dependencies are removed.

**D3/D8 foundation repair — 2026-09-18:** managed MCP dispatch wraps its
caller-owned deadline/request future in `tokio_util::context::TokioContext`.
Off-runtime polls use the configured host runtime without blocking the caller
or detaching request work; timeout remains an interrupted/unknown outcome,
never a replay authorization. Pins: `foreground_progresses_during_reconnect`,
`reconnect_from_a_non_tokio_executor_does_not_panic`, and
`off_runtime_deadline_and_drop_do_not_replay_effects` in
`/home/mdz-axolotl/Clones/zed-kask/kask/crates/hkask-mcp/tests/reconnect_integration.rs`.
The bridge now includes cache-read/cache-write token categories in prompt and
total usage; unrepresentable totals are unknown rather than truncated.
Pinned by `completion_usage_includes_cached_tokens` in
`/home/mdz-axolotl/Clones/zed-kask/kask/crates/kask_bridge/src/inference_chat.rs`.
These changes stay under `kask/`; no new upstream seam is introduced.

D7: the `zed-kask` application (`crates/zed/Cargo.toml`, package `zed`) now
inherits `[workspace.package].version` alongside the Kask crates. The release
is **0.40.0**, based on upstream Zed **1.21.0** at `595d62863e`; upstream
library package versions remain unchanged. `main.rs` already supplies
`CARGO_PKG_VERSION` to `AppVersion::load`, so default application version
reporting follows the workspace release (with existing build metadata).
The explicit `ZED_APP_VERSION` override remains available.

Pinned by `kask/scripts/check-version-sync.sh` and its self-test: the app
manifest must inherit the workspace version, rather than use an independent
literal. Observed RED on the upstream `1.21.0` declaration before the change.
The source installer's no-manifest fallback and current-version README
labels are updated to 0.40.0; historical document revisions are not releases
and are not rewritten. This change does not create a release tag or install
binaries.

Merge of upstream `main` (Zed 1.23.0) into fork `36252eab1c`; merge base

(the working tree of an aborted, uncommitted merge against `7d6a4c1d5a`),

both parents. It is not a restoration of the aborted index.

Upstream sends a limit only when a request sets `max_output_tokens`. | **Retired.** Requests omit an output limit by default, so OpenRouter may again reserve the model's default output size; limited keys can see 402 on small prompts. |

Upstream (#64667) now retains source text and reveals it by cursor, still at 16ms, and emits `EntryUpdated` per incoming chunk. | **Simplified:** keep only the 50ms interval. Upstream's per-chunk update is adopted because its reveal no longer mutates a pending string, and the fork's premature-update pin tested the replaced buffer API. |

| D42 | No hidden reasoning budget. Upstream cloud still sets `budget_tokens: Some(4_096)`; OpenRouter discovery is Adaptive. | **Retained.** |
| D67 | Double-lease panic names its call site. Upstream `double_lease_panic` omits the caller. | **Retained.** |

were surgical merges retaining both upstream changes and Kask wiring.

Recovered fork policy, not new product decisions: `5269cf00ca` keeps
`reasoning_effort` naming; `5f939570bb` and `a2134949e2` make explicit reasoning
disable unconditional at the provider boundary; `fe2bfc050c` keeps skill URL
import and marketplace code removed. The Responses API preserves its
seven-argument converter and explicit `effort: none`, pinned by
`into_open_ai_response_sends_none_when_thinking_is_disabled` and the existing
request-payload tests. RunPod (D29) now shares the core streaming mapper;
Kask tool schemas (D44/D47) use the new normalized-JSON interface.

The merge retains D7/D16 isolation, Kask workspace members, and the calloop
pin. `Cargo.lock` was regenerated against the combined workspace. Validation
ran on the uncommitted merge tree: full Zed and bridge/foundation checks,
scoped `script/clippy` with all targets/features, isolation and dependency
boundary checks, plus focused stream/schema, skill, persistence, provider,
and composition-root tests. This is build/test evidence, not an installed
application smoke test. The merge commit is the completion record.

**D8 algedonic board integration — 2026-09-26:** `crates/zed/src/main.rs` hands its `PanelToolInvoker` to `kask_bridge::BoardAlertEscalationSink` at the composition root. The bridge dispatches board/task operations through the governed MCP runtime, not through a second DB client or dashboard. First-use board creation carries a stable kanban idempotency key so concurrent status reads and alerts cannot create duplicate boards (`first_alert_creates_the_one_review_board_with_replay_protection`, red then green). A duplicate board name is surfaced as an error rather than silently splitting the worklist (`duplicate_review_board_names_fail_instead_of_splitting_the_worklist`, red then green). The kanban service owns standard columns and verification; the connector may advance a self-recovered card to Review but never Done. A fresh degradation before verification comments on that same card and moves Review back to In Progress, preventing a stale recovery from being marked Done (real-server integration test observed red before, green after). Regulation uses the authoritative `AlertDeliveryOutcome` from that one write to distinguish a new card from a repeat comment; the redundant `has_pending_alert` read was deleted, so a repeat no longer lists the board twice and does not replay the live alert or archive. `repeat_uses_the_delivery_result_without_a_second_board_read` was red before removal and green after. The previous queue-backed sink, queue storage, curator queue tools and advice-receipt channel were removed. `BridgeMetacognitionProvider` reads board cards not Done through the same governed invoker; failed reads remain unknown. Proposing skills file cards on the same board; `kanban_task_verify` is the sole Done entry for that board because `KanbanService::task_move` refuses its Review→Done bypass (`algedonic_review_done_requires_task_verify`, observed red then green on an in-memory driver). Other boards keep their existing transition policy. `kask_bridge/src/algedonic_board_real_tests.rs::alert_to_real_board_repeats_recovers_and_requires_verification` drives the connector through real kanban tool methods on `SqliteDriver::in_memory_driver()` (board creation, repeat comment, recovery evidence/transition, re-degradation back to In Progress, proposal-card inclusion in the non-Done count, direct-Done rejection and verification), replacing duplicate scripted connector tests; no fake kanban server or second review state. The test belongs in the zed-side bridge and dev-depends on the hKask kanban server, never the reverse (§13.1, `check-hkask-no-zed-deps.sh`); the server's three MCP request DTOs are public so this test can drive the exported tool methods directly. **Scope of dedup:** the production editor has one scheduled regulation tick loop per process; alert-card lookup and create are not an atomic cross-process operation, so simultaneous editors sharing a DB are not claimed to have exactly-once same-condition card creation. A live restarted editor produced the real `algedonic_log_approaching_cap` Backlog card `e52a23c6-7af4-4525-8c5a-0c45f8d85d4f`; its 385 identical ten-second repeat comments exposed a review-noise failure. The connector now uses `kanban_task_list`'s existing latest-comment activity to comment immediately on changed observations and once per hour on unchanged conditions (D63 heartbeat cadence), without extra tool calls or state; the real-service test failed with three duplicate comments before the fix and passed with two afterward, and a recorder test pins a stale/invalid activity fallback. The older comments are retained as actual history; this bounded-repeat change has not yet been observed in a rebuilt editor. A separate live board-service smoke card passed Backlog→Ready→In Progress→Review→verify→Done and was deleted afterward.


### Core-review continuation — 2026-09-04

**D8 embedding startup:** `crates/zed/src/main.rs` now passes
`ResolvedEmbeddingCredentials` directly to `LanguageModelEmbeddingPort::new`.
The bundle retains the provider descriptor with its key; bare endpoint/key
arguments and the bridge's permissive prefix-stripping helper are removed.
Provider-qualified mismatches fail before HTTP, including through IPC, per the
operator-approved D2 core-review decision. Pins:
`embedding_provider_mismatch_sends_no_http` and
`ipc_embedding_provider_mismatch_is_invalid_request`; the changed composition
root is also checked by `cargo check -p zed`.

**D8 embedding model binding (2026-09-28 migration):** commit `57c2bdea7a`
moves the embedding default to `OpenRouter/qwen/qwen3-embedding-8b` and binds
the zed-side embedding port through the same chain the MCP children receive:
`crates/zed/src/main.rs` calls `KaskSettings::effective_embedding_model()`
(`models.embedding_model` → `corpus.embedding_model` → code default) once at
startup, replacing the former second chain that read this process's own
settings/env and diverged from `HKASK_EMBEDDING_MODEL` after a model change.
The resolver lives in `kask_bridge/src/settings.rs` with the 8B default in
`KaskCorpusSettings::default()`; precedence and default are pinned by
`mcp_env_models_embedding_model_overrides_corpus` and
`effective_embedding_model_falls_back_to_corpus_when_models_empty`. A
mid-session model change still needs a Zed restart to re-bind the port
(kask-settings.md, "Embedding model lifecycle"). The 0.6B artifacts were
purged, not migrated — no backward compatibility; the changed composition
root is checked by `cargo check -p zed`.

**D3 runtime (Kask-owned implementation):** desired launch generations are
cancelled on stop/replacement. Startup publication shares a lifecycle lock with
stop; a drop guard retains failed-start cleanup ownership. Reconnect is polled
on a runtime worker rather than blocking its caller. Handshake/discovery each
have a configurable deadline (`HKASK_MCP_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_SECS`, default 60s).
Pins in `reconnect_integration.rs`: `unload_during_discovery_stays_unloaded`,
`discovery_failure_reaps_child`, `replacement_during_discovery_keeps_new_configuration`,
`foreground_progresses_during_reconnect`, and `stalled_discovery_has_a_phase_deadline`.
These supersede older runtime timing/ownership descriptions below, not the
outcome-unknown no-replay rule.

**D8/D9 authority and lifetime continuation — 2026-09-05:** the operator's
D1 ruling is implemented by `delegation_grants` and the `tool_invoke` gate.
`KaskMcpSettingsContent::delegated_tools` flows into `KaskMcpSettings`, then
`build_mcp_server_env` injects an opaque per-server `HKASK_TOOL_GRANT`. Empty
configuration denies tools; request allowlists can only narrow the parent grant.
Grant replacement/unload revokes old tokens. `main.rs` revokes before settings
unload; disabled-server env diffs cannot remint grants, and registry changes
precede credential awaits. Pins: `ipc_child_cannot_expand_parent_grant`,
`delegated_grants_follow_settings_and_launch_lifecycle`,
`grants_are_stable_until_changed_and_revoked`, `main_unload_revokes_before_stop`.
This is not PID binding or same-UID OS isolation. Provisioning is documented in
`kask/docs/reference/kask-settings.md`; existing card allowlists alone do not
provision parent authority.

The operator's D3 ruling bounds total admitted chat work at twice configured
concurrency, with `Overloaded` before dispatch and `Timeout` over queue/model
resolution/establishment/drain. A GPUI-clock expiry check also covers an unpolled
receiver; caller closure releases request-owned capacity. `handle_connection`
monitors actual Unix EOF and refuses pipelining; no unknown-effect replay is
introduced. Pins: `cancelled_queued_request_never_starts_model`,
`queued_and_established_requests_share_deadline`,
`streaming_deadline_covers_unpolled_queue_and_drain`,
`streaming_cancellation_releases_permits_with_disabled_deadline`,
`ipc_disconnect_drops_pending_dispatch`, and the inference client's
`ipc_client_receives_server_timeout_before_closing`. AIMD is unchanged.

**D8/D9 inference-resilience continuation — 2026-09-15:**
`LanguageModelInferencePort` now owns a configured closed/open/half-open circuit
beside its admission and concurrency gates. Consecutive transient failures open
the circuit; open requests return typed `CircuitOpen`; one probe is admitted
after `kask.general.inference_circuit_open_secs`, closing on success or reopening
on transient failure. `InferenceResilienceSource` exports atomic snapshots plus
cursor-addressed transition and permanent-failure receipts to
`CyberneticsLoop`; model rewiring replaces that source and resets its cursor.
Regulation records observed recovery without causal attribution; the initial
open remains local, while a failed half-open probe or permanent failure escalates.
The settings content and bridge settings
add `inference_circuit_failure_threshold` (default 3) and
`inference_circuit_open_secs` (default 30). Pins:
`transient_provider_storm_opens_live_inference_circuit`,
`half_open_admits_one_probe_and_success_closes_circuit`,
`permanent_provider_failure_is_observed_without_opening_circuit`,
`circuit_close_receipt_records_observed_recovery`, and
`initial_inference_circuit_open_does_not_escalate` and
`reopened_inference_circuit_routes_native_escalation`.

**D23/D8 native delegated authority — P1a, 2026-09-18:**
Delegated native work auto-runs within inherited restrictions; it does not receive
fresh authority merely by selecting a model/profile or opening a worktree.
The native seam additionally covers `crates/agent/src/{delegation_authority.rs,
agent.rs,thread.rs,db.rs,thread_store.rs,tools/create_thread_tool.rs,
tools/context_server_registry.rs}` and their tests. `SiblingThreadRequest` carries
an optional host-owned `DelegationAuthority`. `NativeThreadEnvironment` replaces
request-supplied authority with a snapshot of the actual parent's enabled tools
before invoking `SiblingThreadHost`; `Thread::new_subagent` uses the same source.
The D8 bridge-to-UI boundary supplies exact `server/tool` MCP grants through
`DelegationAuthority::from_mcp_tools`; a bare name, wildcard, provider alias, or
MCP tool named `terminal` cannot grant a native builtin terminal.

`AnyAgentTool::delegation_identity` supplies the trusted builtin identity or exact
MCP server/original name from both context-server implementations, independently
of model-facing sanitization/disambiguation. `Thread::restrict_delegation`
intersects (never replaces/widens) an existing ceiling and refreshes advertised
turn tools. `enabled_tools` filters both builtin and MCP advertisements, and
`run_tool` rechecks before invoking the implementation, including cached calls.
`DbThread.delegation_authority` persists the ceiling across reload and profile
changes; ordinary nondelegated/older roots retain `None`. Resume checks actual
parent identity (rejecting roots and another parent's child) and intersects the
child's ceiling with the parent's currently enabled tools. This is a tool-level
ceiling, not an OS sandbox or enforcement of free-text task instructions.

Native regression pins (`cargo test --offline --locked -p agent delegation -j2`):
`delegation_exact_mcp_identity`, `delegation_roundtrip_and_attenuation`,
`delegation_managed_tool_identity_is_not_an_alias`,
`delegation_child_profile_cannot_widen`, `delegation_persistence_and_profile_switch`,
`delegation_execution_gate`, `delegation_native_creation_and_resume`, and
`delegation_sibling_host_uses_actual_parent`. Bridge admission/cancellation and
UI application before first prompt are separate D8/D23 integration obligations;
these native tests do not claim to verify those paths.

Native verification on the P1a working tree: full offline/locked `agent` suite
with `-j2 -- --test-threads=2` passed **910 tests, 11 ignored** (including all
8 delegation pins); `cargo check --offline --locked -p agent --all-targets -j2`
and `HKASK_BUILD_JOBS=2 ./script/clippy --offline --locked -p agent` passed
(the latter checks all targets/features with warnings denied). Tests used a
checkout-local `TMPDIR` because the default `/tmp` quota was exhausted.

**P1a host/IPC integration (same uncommitted slice):** D23 additionally changes
`crates/agent_ui/src/{agent_panel.rs,agent_ui.rs,conversation_view.rs,
conversation_view/thread_view.rs,thread_metadata_store.rs}` and
`crates/zed/src/{main.rs,visual_test_runner.rs}` (the last two test-literal files
only initialize the persisted field). Sibling creation without host authority
fails before workspace effects. A native/Curator destination is required;
`AgentInitialContent::DelegatedSibling` installs the ceiling before first
auto-submit, retaining the envelope across connection retries. Shared-thread
export/import preserves the ceiling. `delegated_sibling_authority_precedes_first_completion`,
`delegated_sibling_rejects_non_native_startup`,
`restricted_sibling_destinations_are_native_only`, and
`sibling_missing_authority_is_denied` pin these paths.

The bridge passes `allowed_tools: Vec<String>` to `WorktreeSpawner::spawn`.
`host/create_worktree_thread` in the existing parent-held `delegated_tools`
registry explicitly authorizes creation; the agent card narrows the child's
MCP set. `WorktreeSpawnRequest::execute` rechecks revocation and closed replies
at dequeue; the queue holds at most 32 requests. Pins: `worktree_authority_denies_before_enqueue`,
`worktree_authority_is_intersected_and_revocable_at_dequeue`,
`worktree_disconnected_queued_request_never_starts`, and
`worktree_queue_rejects_over_capacity`. No MCP child processes are needed by
these tests (in-process fakes/socket pairs only). Kanban's fallback executor
and unused runtime constructor dependency are deleted; an uncertain reply
retains its idempotency claim, not a replay invitation. A queued UI thread is
reported pending, not running, and does not advance task status.

Native delegation retains the depth-one limit by excluding further
`spawn_agent`/`create_thread` from child ceilings. Native `kanban_task_spawn`
dispatch is denied pending P2: per-server IPC grants are not initiating-thread
authority, so native agents delegate via their bound environment instead.
This is a persisted tool snapshot, not continuous parent revocation, an OS
sandbox, or enforcement of free-text constraints. User-created root threads
and external-agent manual workflows remain distinct from delegated creation.
The historical D23 fallback description below is superseded by this section.

Final integration evidence (uncommitted): `cargo test --offline --locked` with
jobs 2 ran **911 agent tests (11 ignored), 223 bridge tests, 4 sibling/UI tests**,
and **83 kanban tests** after deleting the orphan fallback writer/two superseded
tests. The six focused worktree tests are included in the bridge total.
`cargo check --offline --locked -p zed --jobs 2` passed. Prescribed
`./script/clippy --offline --locked -p agent -p agent_ui -p kask_bridge
-p hkask-inference -p hkask-mcp-kata-kanban`, jobs 2, all targets/features and
warnings denied, passed after the orphan cleanup. This does not claim a live
worktree/provider/MCP-process smoke test or implementation of P2.

**D2 curator status visibility (2026-09-23):** `CuratorStatusTool` remains one read-only in-process implementation over `MetacognitionProvider`. `NativeAgent::new_session` now registers it for every native and Curator session, and the existing built-in registry, shipped `write`/`ask` profiles, and permission-UI exclusion expose that same tool to the model. `CuratorDirectiveTool` and `CuratorClearAlgedonicLogTool` remain Curator-overlay-only; visibility does not grant mutation authority. Pins: `test_native_session_reads_curator_status_without_curator_mutations`, `test_curator_session_registers_directive_tool`, `curator_status_is_enabled_in_both_shipped_agent_profiles`, and `test_all_tools_are_in_tool_info_or_excluded`. The `default_constructed_tools_and_macro_list_are_in_sync` gate treats `CuratorStatusTool` as session-registered (the `new_session` site above), alongside `SkillTool` and `RecordSkillFeedbackTool`; otherwise the test falsely demands default construction and would encourage duplicate registration to make it green. The native-session pins still require the status tool in both model-visible requests. No MCP duplicate or second health computation is added. Tool listing in an already-running session still requires the rebuilt host to publish the new registry; registration tests do not substitute for that live check.

**D2/D8 status fidelity correction (2026-09-23):** `crates/agent/src/curator_agent_server.rs` no longer describes the Curator overlay as registering a second status tool or instructs log clearing as a consequence of cap pressure. Its session-start block reads the bridge's `regulation_acceptance_rate` and displays unknown measurements as unavailable, never zero, false or nominal. `crates/agent/src/tools/curator_tools.rs` exposes that same measured rate under its actual name and reports incomplete monitoring explicitly; `kask_bridge/src/metacognition_bridge.rs` remains the source of the live snapshot. The curator MCP server's README/header distinguish its store-liveness `curator_ping` and persisted events from this in-process status tool. Pins: `session_state_does_not_turn_missing_measurements_into_health`, `session_state_displays_the_providers_actual_readings`, `status_reads_the_providers_acceptance_rate_without_inventing_effectiveness`, `status_serializes_only_the_measured_rate_name`, `status_line_never_calls_a_broken_or_unobserved_loop_healthy`. This correction adds no MCP status path and claims no proof of real-world effectiveness from acceptance data.

**D2/D9 curator interaction documentation cleanup (2026-09-27):** `kask/docs/architecture/functional-interaction-spec.md` §9 and `kask/docs/reference/kask-settings.md` now state what each selectable level asks the Curator to do, how per-new-thread Session Context is assembled, and why this prompt guidance is neither a tool grant nor proof of learning. The Curator Email UI comment (`crates/settings_ui/src/pages/kask_page/curator.rs`) and editor-process sink comment (`crates/zed/src/main.rs`) no longer claim the SMTP password is delivered to MCP children; the editor process owns that sink and the curator MCP credential allowlist excludes SMTP. No runtime behavior or permission boundary changed.

**D33 shared local swarm conversation extension (2026-09-23):** `crates/swarm_panel/src/member_turns.rs` and the selection/Steer wiring in `swarm_panel.rs`, `card.rs`, `compose.rs`, and `fetch.rs` show the server-owned encrypted member conversation beside—not inside—the Curator `ConversationView`. The panel reads `swarm_thread_local` for the selected local swarm; `swarm_list_local_threads` supplies an archived-history selector after deletion and restart without making a deleted swarm executable. The server enforces roster membership and commits turns through `SwarmThreadStore`; standalone local calls remain separate. Pins: `thread_tests::scoped_thread_is_structured_durable_isolated_and_archived`, `scoped_dispatch_tests::scoped_plan_broadcast_send_share_history_and_keep_verdict_and_board`, and `member_turns::tests` (seven state/shape tests). The 201 swarm tests, seven focused panel tests, scoped `./script/clippy`, and `cargo check -p zed` passed; no live visual or deployed MCP member-to-member test is claimed. The full panel suite's formerly-stale Kanban tool-count pin (expecting 26 against the server's 27) was fixed 2026-09-28 in the same pass as the D33 audit: the count pin now reads 27, the suite is 69 passed / 0 failed, and the server's own `tool_names_match_live_router` self-pin stays green.
